# The Dying Echo

## N11-1 Long-Awaited Melody

*How many faces does death wear?*

*There are the known ones: age, violence, war, plague, poverty, and the ever-rising tide of madness that fills the world... Omnipresent, unavoidable.*

*But it can also be light, so very light, like an escape, a promise, a dream ending...*

*...a song fading out.*

Anonymous User 1: Anybody know the name of that little idol who got erased?

Anonymous User 2: Who? Phanuel?

Anonymous User 1: Yeah, her. She pulled something major. Three days later, wiped from the net. Everything about her's gone. You know why?

Anonymous User 1: Those songs of hers had dirty stuff baked into them. All gone now too. Not a single copy left online...

Anonymous User 1: But I've got one.

Anonymous User 3: ????? You're kidding. You actually got your hands on it? Now I know who you are! The Belphegor replica master, back at it again!

Anonymous User 2: Send it over! You're my hero from here on out! Bless you always!

Anonymous User 1: Fine, fine. Since we've crossed paths, I'll send it. One minute only. Then the window closes.

Anonymous User 1: Oh, by the way. You can listen, but don't listen to the whole thing.

Anonymous User 1: The person who passed it to me was pretty explicit.

Anonymous User 1: Anyone who listened to the end is already six feet under. :)

News: In the early hours of July 14, a mysterious death was reported at a hotel in Eastside District 3. The victim was a forty-six-year-old male with no prior medical history, said to be in good health before his death. The cause has yet to be determined.

News: By the afternoon of the 26th, more unexplained deaths had surfaced across surrounding residential areas. The victims varied in age and occupation, but their deaths shared certain similarities, which have sparked widespread speculation online.

News: Speaker Max King is urging citizens not to fall for unverified rumors. The investigation into these cases is still ongoing. The public is advised not to share audio or video files from unidentified sources. The Public Security Bureau and relevant agencies are doing everything in their power to protect every citizen.

No one can say where a rumor begins. By the time it surfaces, it has already swelled into a quiet undercurrent that flows through the shadows.

More and more claim to have heard that melody. Some heard the voices of lost loved ones; others saw visions of what is yet to come. Most simply speak nothing at all, having taken the secrets somewhere beyond this world, perhaps.

Radio Host: Welcome back to DisCity Whispers! You're probably wondering why we're dropping an extra episode so soon. Well, get this: SEVEN homicides in a single night?! And they're clamping down on every journalist trying to ask questions?!

Radio Host: The dead range from students to factory workers to CEOs. So what's the link? Don't tell me... they all crossed paths with that so-called "Cursed Melody" making the rounds?

Radio Host: Hah! Even I can't say that with a straight face! People will spin anything for a headline. Coincidence? Probably. Rumor? Almost certainly. But the truth? Your host is already on the case. I'll have answers for you next time, so stick around!

The rumor feeds on its own speed. Bootleg records multiply. Audio snippets are spliced and reposted. Sheet music surfaces online only to be transcribed and twisted into everything from urban games to midnight movies.

DisCity, never short on bodies, makes the perfect stage; each new murder is conveniently pinned to the Cursed Melody, and each headline adds another layer to the myth. People swear they will only listen for a beat, a breath, a single bar... Fancy a gamble?

It almost seems as though living has worn out its welcome, and death has become the grand prize.

Street Passerby A: ♪ Hmm hmm, hm hm... Bang! The power of the Cursed Melody—death!

Street Passerby B: Yeah, yeah. Of course you'd be into this kinda stupid crap.

Street Passerby A: Hey, this is DisCity. We've got Mania, superpowers, you name it. A little "Melody of Death" is not exactly out of place.

Street Passerby A: Think about it. Sing a line, drop a body. You could be the city's top hitter, raking in cash. Sweet deal, no?

Street Passerby B: Sweet? Yeah, real sweet. We've been jobless for a year; can't make rent, can't clear our tabs. Next stop will be crossing the Rust to bust our backs in some Syndicate slum. Yeah, that's the dream, right?

He lets out a coarse laugh and elbows the slumped young man next to him.

Dejected Young Man: Haha... yeah... right...

09:07 PM, August 1st

Beyond Eastside District 20

Reclamation Housing Zone

He finally shakes off the laughter of outsiders, returns home, and throws every lock on the door.

He powers up his terminal. The cursor wanders across the screen like a ghost, weaving through a pile of pop-ups, until it comes to rest on an email that has waited for him far too long.

This melody brought me peace. May it find you as well.

Click to view attachment

The cursor pulses in his vacant stare. Slowly, it drifts to the file that was never touched and copies it to his phone.

Nightfall Suite, Op. 1

Here ends one who left at dawn,

seeking a dream till the last.

A melody of strings cuts through the static and electric hum, unfurling into the drab, suffocating room and enveloping its only listener.

Again, and again, and again. Time slips away. Then, barely audible, he begins to speak under his breath.

Dejected Young Man: The melody will sound...

He stands up. He works through each lock in turn, pushes the door open, and walks away from it all. Nothing comes with him but the melody still threading through his ears, trailing him as he moves into the empty, waiting streets.

03:07 AM

2 km East of the Rust, Eastside

Dawn has not yet arrived. The chill still clings to the air, and the melody moves forward, accompanied by a faint, murmuring whisper.

Dejected Young Man: The melody will sound... just as...

Scavenger: ...!

A scavenger sleeping by the roadside feels a foot come down on her and startles awake.

Scavenger: Sorry, I didn't mean to... sorry...

The sky is still dark. She does not dare pick a fight. She clutches her tattered coat and tries to slip away. But as she rises, she bumps straight into another person. She looks up and finds three, maybe five figures, swaying along the street like bullies on their rounds. The woman does not have time to be afraid.

She hears the melody.

As it winds around her, her gaze goes as blank and faraway as theirs. For the first time, she looks just like everyone else in the city. She loosens up, and asks with a smile:

Scavenger: ...Is it almost time... for the performance?

03:55 AM

The Rust Contamination Isolation Belt

FAC Sentry: ...What's that?

Miasma spreads through the Rust, filling the riverbed where water used to run. The old factories and mounds of waste keep their usual mute watch. But something is different...

The sentry squints. There, inside the sealed contamination zone, a few figures drift with unsteady, weightless steps. Corruptors? No! He springs up, snatches an Anti-Mania device, and bolts out the door.

FAC Sentry: Who are you? What are you doing here? Wait—stop!

FAC Sentry: It's dangerous ahead! Where are you going? Stop—

He never reaches the "citizens" he meant to protect. A drifting string melody reaches him before he can, and his footsteps slow down.

FAC Sentry: ...Stop...

The miasma rolls and heaves. Ghostly silhouettes awaken and creep closer. But all of that has lost its meaning.

The melody drifts through the darkness. Before such exquisite temptation, even death holds no fear. The audience has arrived and takes their seats, one after another.

Entranced Crowd: The melody will sound... just as we...

Entranced Sentry: ...will find each other again.

Corruptors from the Rust: *ROAAAAR...!!*

07:24 AM, August 2, N.F. 116

MBCC Bureau

You blink awake, temples throbbing. You reach for the clock, then hesitate. The pale light seeping through the blinds gives it away: morning is already here. You slept in.

You swear under your breath, snatch the wet towel off the table, and cue up the terminal news as you move. With the broadcast droning on, you rub the cool cloth across your face.

News: At 4:00 AM, a mass death event took place at the Rust. Four citizens from Eastside illegally entered the restricted isolation belt and were later discovered dead in the contaminated area. The FAC sentry on duty abandoned his post and was also caught up in the incident.

News: The case has prompted widespread concern. All relevant departments have launched emergency investigations. Following an exhaustive search of the site, the victims' terminals were recovered. After data restoration, investigators uncovered a suspicious audio file.

News: Coupled with prior rumors of the Cursed Melody, the incident has sparked a public frenzy of speculation, with growing calls for the investigation team to clarify...

You have been receiving case files on similar incidents for more than a week now. At those words, your hand moves instinctively to reach for them, only for an irritating little thing to drift up and block your line of sight.

Flawed Schorl: Good day, Chief.

Flawed Schorl: You look exhausted. According to your biometrics, out of the past 5 hours you spent sleeping, 3 hours and 28 minutes were in abnormal REM states. It's a classic indicator of heightened anxiety. Your deep sleep cycle is critically insufficient.

Flawed Schorl: A nightmare again? Is it the same one as before?

You brush past the faint probing in its words and strike back.

**【Advance】**

- I asked you to wake me in 15 minutes.

**end**

Flawed Schorl: But you've been running on empty for far too long. If you keep pushing through the strain, the wear will accumulate until your body can no longer sustain it.

Flawed Schorl: One full night's rest won't derail your schedule. EDGE has already recalibrated task priorities and elevated "maintain your physical health" to match the importance of "ensure your personal safety."

You shoot it a glance, and the black triangle immediately ducks lower.

Flawed Schorl: Naturally, I'm also prepared to assist with workflow optimization. Possible approaches include...

Chief: Save it, you little traitor. Not buying it.

You bat it away and walk toward the shower. Somehow, a handful of words with that thing, and your brain goes soft. You need to keep your wits about you.

The water comes down cold. It hits your face, seeps into your hair, and trails from your ears across your chest and down your spine, slipping over your skin to your heels. Every muscle braces. The chill sparks your nerves alive and drives itself straight into your skull.

You stay rooted until you grow used to it, until the tension leaves your limbs and the world pulls back into focus. It works every time. The thoughts you left waiting the night before come creeping back.

*The spread of the Cursed Melody remains under close surveillance, yet without exception, every original digital file has been corrupted or destroyed shortly after each incident. How does it remain so elusive? What mechanism allows it to infect and influence its listeners? And once it has claimed its victims, how much farther can its reach still extend?*

Lemma: We've combed the entire hub and cleaned up every trace of contamination. The three of them destroyed everything linked to Phanuel... and still, the interference won't stop.

Lemma: BR-003's internal anomaly is faint, but it's there. And it's slowly getting worse. The signature matches the breach from the leak incident almost perfectly. And it's practically identical to the so-called "Cursed Melody" that was just flagged.

Lemma: I'd love to say we just messed up the sweep. But if that's not it... then the enemy's reach this time might be wider and deeper than anything we've dealt with before. And we don't even have a clue where they came in.

*The idol incident, still fresh in everyone's memory, continues to cast a long shadow. Aside from the key Sinner Phanuel and her power to induce Hollow Syndrome, a suspect violin tone has been detected in the fan anthem that propelled her influence.*

*Is this just another Underground's typical move, spreading Mania to fuel aimless disorder?*

*Or is there a deeper design at play?*

EDGE-02: Great music has a way of reaching people on an emotional level, and ordinary humans are particularly susceptible to its pull. On top of that, the Mania spectrum hidden within is remarkably difficult to detect.

EDGE-02: Above us, the Observation Hub is shrouded by BR-003's Mania field, which lets it linger undetected and siphon intelligence for a long time. And if that worked up there, isn't it plausible that it's already been spreading through the city just as stealthily, just as extensively?

EDGE-02: Especially since BR-000 is right here on the ground. It's never left.

EDGE-02: A fitting enemy for humanity, I must say. The method is remarkably refined.

*—Enemy.*

*The Sillage Society. The Underground. The impossible fanatics, the foul creatures of shadow that you have fought at every turn. They whisper in the dark, manipulate desire, unleash Mania, carve Black Rings into the world. All of it, every last act...*

*For what?*

Adelaide: You still haven't figured out who your real enemy is...

Chief: Phew...

After letting out a long breath, you twist the tap shut, change into clean clothes, and walk out of the bathroom.

The black triangle waits where you left it, drifting above your desk, rotating in an unhurried spin.

Chief: That detention warrant I submitted last time. Any word?

Flawed Schorl: This application is particularly delicate. The subject occupies a sensitive position. She's registered as a Sinner, has consistently complied with inspections, and has never left any tangible evidence of wrongdoing across multiple incidents. To move against her, we still lack the procedural footing.

Flawed Schorl: And her legal team, formidable as always, has been actively maneuvering behind the scenes. A hasty move would bring public backlash and potentially diplomatic complications. According to our projections—

All that talk, and nothing to the point.

**【Advance】**

- Where's the person I asked for?

**end**

Flawed Schorl: ...

Flawed Schorl: She's already here. In the interrogation room.

Chief: Now that's what I wanted to hear. Well done.

You give the tip of its triangle a flick, your usual wordless nod of approval. Schorl takes it, then slips back to its accustomed spot, sixty centimeters behind you, and picks up where it left off.

Flawed Schorl: As I was saying, procedure does not allow indefinite detention. You only have 24 hours, Chief.

Chief: 24 hours is all I need.

A quick sweep through the desk files. You grab the essentials, bag them, throw on your coat, and head out.

Down the corridor, into the elevator, through the checkpoint. Schorl unlocks the interrogation room door and moves aside, leaving the path open. It stays where it is told. You step inside.

There, in the chair, sits a familiar figure, and from the look on her face, she has been here for some time.

Eirene: Hello, Chief.

Eirene: A security check... in a place like this?

Chief: Let's talk about that Nightfall Suite that's been making the rounds first. Or... should I call it the Cursed Melody of Mania instead?

Chief: I'd love to know your perspective, President.

## N11-2 Eirene Campbell

Eirene Campbell, President of Quinn. She built her empire on WhiteSands shipping and is the de facto controller of Phantom Entertainment, the agency behind Phanuel.

She was notably active during the Ash Campaign, making large donations to the front and actually increasing her standing among rival city-states. The DisCity Council later held her up as a prime example of strategic commercial partnership. In the following months, Quinn acted as a bilateral bridge in Aurum Secretum tech exchanges, and many anticipated her next move would be into the West District mine project.

But unexpectedly, Quinn soon pivoted its investment focus toward finance and entertainment. Analysts speculated that Nirvana had effectively sealed Quinn out of the core Hypercube industry, and that this capitalist from Fraser's Campbell bloodline would never be fully embraced by DisCity.

When the idol incident broke, Phantom Entertainment voluntarily stepped forward and cooperated fully, offering the assistance necessary to resolve the crisis. The media followed with extensive coverage of their financial losses, their renewed commitment to public welfare, and their declared mission to address the spiritual crisis of modern life. The company emerged with its victimhood firmly established and escaped the storm unscathed.

She is probably not eager to be called in again.

Eirene: I've lost count of how many times I've sat through this kind of questioning. "I'm from Fraser, formerly a Campbell, blood kin to the war criminal Adelaide. And now the artist I just signed is tangled up in the Sillage Society Mania case..."

Eirene: It seems my instincts and my fortunes have both abandoned me. The only comfort I have left is a pleasant conversation with a dear friend.

**【Choose One】**

- The honor is mine.

- I'm relieved to hear that.

**end**

Chief: Do pass that sentiment on to Quinn's legal team, President. A little goodwill toward the MBCC would go a long way.

Chief: Mr. Fox and his army of litigators have already flooded the city council with countless warning letters. My bureau is drowning in calls from upstairs.

Eirene: Quite so. My counsel informs me that this detention has no valid legal footing. I could leave at any moment if I chose.

Eirene: And of course, there's my little sister Hypatia Campbell, a brilliant Aurum Secretum researcher DisCity is eager to court...

Eirene: I believe the Farland Alliance embassy has already reached out.

Chief: My concern is DisCity's safety and the welfare of its people. With Mania and the Underground in play, I'm confident they'll see reason.

You set the heavy bundle of files down on the table.

Chief: The Cursed Melody cases are multiplying. We've tightened the lockdown, but there are still leaks we can't plug. I need Phantom Entertainment to open up and give us what we need to dig deeper.

She smiles, completely unruffled.

Eirene: Suicides are a daily occurrence in this city, too many to even track. Yet a person of your standing chooses to lose sleep over rumors and folklore...

Eirene: Is the Underground really that elusive? That much of a headache for you?

**【Advance】**

- Then let me put it differently.

**end**

Chief: Bringing you in wasn't just about questions; it was about safety.

Chief: The Phanuel incident exposed just how dangerous music can be as a vehicle for Mania. The Underground won't ignore that. They'll likely double their efforts, and Phantom Entertainment is the obvious instrument for their next move. As the one pulling the strings, you're a target. You need protection.

Eirene: Heh. Protection? For me?

**【Advance】**

- Correct.

**end**

Chief: You didn't make it here unscathed, did you? Those Undergrounders didn't just happen to be passing by.

You take out your terminal, find the recording, and press play. Eugene's voice breaks through, buried under gunfire, crashing impacts, and the piercing cries of Corruptors.

Eugene: Bureau, do you copy? Get word to Chief! The convoy's been hit. We're surrounded! Corruptors closing in, and... Underground sorcerers calling the shots!

You fix your gaze on Eirene.

Chief: Your escort convoy was ambushed on the way here. Corruptors, led by the Underground sorcerers we've been hunting. I'd appreciate your account of what happened.

She brushes it away with a careless flick of her wrist.

Eirene: I'm no soldier. Wouldn't your own people give you a better report?

Chief: Funny you should say that. My subordinates all sang your praises. They insisted I thank you for your composure, your command, and your exceptional use of your power. They said you read the enemy's every move before they made it.

**【Advance】**

- "Almost like you knew what was coming."

**end**

Something changes in the air. Her eyes find you at last, and a smile creeps across her face, slow and deliberate.

Eirene: ...Heh. I wouldn't say I *knew*.

Eirene: But I'm well aware that every inch of that convoy was wired with cameras, feeding every gesture I made back to your desk... I must put on a proper show, wouldn't you agree?

As she speaks, she reaches over and takes the terminal from your hand. She taps the projection key, and the surveillance footage tied to that audio unfolds between the two of you.

The clip is short. When it ends, you have seen a stunning display of combat led by her. Eirene meets your gaze with a smile.

Eirene: So, Chief, was it to your liking?

Chief: Hard to say.

Her entry into the battle did throw the Underground into genuine disarray, clearly taking them by surprise. But before that, those sorcerers had only ever targeted MBCC personnel, leaving this particular Sinner remarkably unharmed.

Naturally, you have your doubts about what their attack was really meant to accomplish.

Chief: MBCC's been squeezing the Underground hard lately. Most sorcerers are lying low. So imagine my surprise when someone decided to attack an MBCC convoy in broad daylight.

Chief: Could it be your brother, the Underground Sage Adelaide Campbell? He's known for his devotion to family. Watching this footage, I almost thought...

**【Advance】**

- Maybe he sent them to spring you out.

**end**

She chuckles, and something softer creeps into her voice.

Eirene: And yet, here I am, back to your side.

It only underscores her utter lack of care. You lock eyes with those strange, mismatched irises. A beat passes, then two. When you finally speak, you enunciate every syllable.

**【Advance】**

- Let's cut to the chase, President.

**end**

Chief: We analyzed the special powers used on-site. Your Mania signature has changed, subtly, but noticeably. It's closer now to that of the Apostles of Ashes tied to the Famine Sage. I see two possibilities—

Chief: One: They acted against you and did something to you. That gives MBCC a reason to protect you. Two: You reached out to them and made a deal. That gives MBCC grounds for a real arrest.

Chief: You walked in here willingly and show no intention of running. I'd like to take that as a good sign. So tell me plainly what happened. Keep dancing around it, and I can't promise I'll stay so charitable.

She hears you out, then releases a quiet sigh and finally speaks in earnest.

Eirene: They came to me with offers. Asked me to take the captured Sage's place, or should I say, to avenge my "brother."

Eirene: But I have little feeling for a brother in name only. And the Underground's offerings are hardly enticing.

She settles back into her chair. It's been four years since your first meeting, when ambition flared in her eyes with knife-edge clarity. Today, that same gaze looks muted, in a way that unsettles you, as though time has carved grooves that refuse to heal.

Eirene breathes out another sigh, and her voice, when it comes, is tinged with an exhaustion she no longer bothers to hide.

Eirene: Wealth, power... I've had them for years. And politics? The more I see, the more tedious it becomes. DisCity or Fraser, it's all the same—short-sighted mediocrities at the top. What satisfaction is there in besting them? None.

Eirene: This world keeps crumbling, Chief. There's nothing left worth pursuing with any real fire.

Eirene: …There is one thing, however, that's been troubling me lately.

She lowers her gaze. It is a rare sight, the President of Quinn showing anything close to sorrow.

Eirene: Two months ago, a friend of mine passed away. His death weighed on me more than I expected.

Eirene: The investigation ruled it self-inflicted. The scene was clean, with no discrepancies.

Eirene: ...Even in his final moments, "he had the presence of mind to play some music for himself."

Chief: ...Music?

You zero in on the key point at once.

**【Choose One】**

- Two months ago?

  Eirene: That's right. It happened on June 6. Earlier than every Cursed Melody case on record.

- What kind of music?

  Eirene: A solo—a beautiful violin solo. The same timbre hides in Phanuel's song you're investigating. I suspect every version of the Cursed Melody shares that signature.

**end**

Eirene: If the Cursed Melody is truly behind this, then my friend was its first victim. I expect you to uncover the truth.

The weight of the lead is unmistakable. You relay the detail to Schorl immediately, and the verification returns before long. As for the woman who gave it to you, the more you learn, the less you understand.

Chief: ...Maybe you do have someone worth going to war for. With your reach, you'd have no shortage of ways to investigate the truth or seek revenge on his behalf.

Chief: Why wait? Why this roundabout approach? And one more thing...

**【Advance】**

- Why me?

**end**

Eirene: Because you will never side with them. You'll get me an answer; I'm certain of it.

Eirene smiles. She sits up straighter, and the haughty poise you noticed at the door slides back into place, as if it had never left.

<ins>Eirene: Move quickly, sir. I'm a busy woman, and I won't give you much time. But for the 24 hours you negotiated, I'll be a good guest and wait at your Bureau.</ins><br><ins>Eirene: Move quickly, miss. I'm a busy woman, and I won't give you much time. But for the 24 hours you negotiated, I'll be a good guest and wait at your Bureau.</ins>

Eirene: Deliver the results, and I'll compensate you handsomely. I trust your judgment won't disappoint.

You look her in the eye.

**【Advance】**

- Is that encouragement, or are you just fanning the flames?

**end**

Eirene: Call it a firm push. Don't lose to those Undergrounders, Chief ${PlayerName}.

She offers you a parting smile, one that says more than words ever could.

## N11-3 The Earliest Case

Wells Ashford, Eirene's butler, passed at 78 years old. He had served her since before Quinn was even founded. You met him once, briefly, at the Midsummer Night's Club during the Ambrosia incident in Eastside, in the late summer of N.F. 112.

In the photograph, he looks exactly as you remember him: graceful and soft-spoken, standing straight with a full head of white hair, his eyes carrying that quiet gleam of wisdom. Eirene told you he turned down the mansion she had prepared for him, choosing instead a modest old neighborhood in Eastside for his retirement.

You step into the room. A leather sofa and matching armchairs face off across a low table, where a modest plant and a stack of books sit in quiet companionship. The employer had pressed for finer touches, crystal decanters and rich furnishings, perhaps for evenings when the two of them would share a glass after a chess game.

You move toward the shelves. Among the gifted objects, you spot photographs and letters, pieces of a long life, all arranged with care and precision. This is a home well looked after, just like the man who kept it.

Eirene: Wells was an old friend of mine. From the Campbell estate to the WhiteSands and DisCity, he was always by my side. Loyal. Dependable. There are few I trust with my back. He was one of them.

Eirene: What's rarer still, people came and went over the years, but he alone stayed. He promised he always would.

Eirene: For that promise, I spared no expense on the finest healthcare plan DisCity had to offer. I wanted him to last.

You gently open the cabinet and draw out the photographs and correspondence. Folded within the letters are newspaper clippings, each one about Quinn, filed in perfect chronological order, with the most recent coverage of the farlands reconciliation taking pride of place.

The photographs tell their own story: not a single Campbell appears in them, not Bryce, Eirene's father, nor Adelaide, whom you have watched with particular care. The only Campbell name that turns up in the clippings is Hypatia's.

A nearby photo album catches your eye, its pages filled with images of Eirene at every stage. You turn, and the photographs mellow into older, amber tones. There she is at fifteen, a quiet girl in a library, absorbed in a book, untouched by the sharp ambition that would come later.

You flip again. A woman with a gentle face holds an infant Eirene, smiling into the lens. Probably her mother. Throughout the album, a single loyal man moves like a thread, never intruding, just watching over her across the decades, from his own youth to his very last years.

Flawed Schorl: They're here, Chief.

You move deeper into the study. Schorl hovers over the files, its scanning beam trailing across the endless sheets of paper, searching the labyrinth of documents for even anything tied to Campbell.

Flawed Schorl: These cover the Campbell family and their various business dealings in Fraser.

Flawed Schorl: All externally sourced. He wasn't directly involved, but he clearly kept a close eye on the family legacy.

Chief: Anything on the Underground?

Schorl emits two short beeps, signaling that the paper files have turned up nothing. It is a disappointing answer, but you catch sight of an old terminal resting on the desk.

Chief: What about that terminal?

Flawed Schorl: Still analyzing. That said, his lifestyle appears rather traditional. Paper records seem to be his preference.

Flawed Schorl: Terminal activity is sparse. Last powered on June 6... Almost no residual data. It was likely wiped.

**【Advance】**

- Can you recover it?

**end**

Flawed Schorl: Initiating decryption. It will take some time.

Chief: Then get more processing power. Sync with EDGE. I want it in 10 minutes.

You return to the living room and run your eyes over the shelves again. A few faceless statues catch your attention; the carved markings on their chests stir something in your memory.

At first glance, they resemble Underground runes, but they do not match any known variants. The initial investigation has marked them as items of interest, awaiting more thorough on-site examination.

**【Advance】**

- Where did it happen?

**end**

Eugene: The bedroom. Mania Inspection's already on it.

Eugene's report guides your attention to the far wall of the bedroom, where a vintage fireplace stands, its hearth filled with cold ash and charcoal. Two months have bled away since the flame died.

In June, a fire in DisCity makes no sense, even by Fraser standards.

He lit it deliberately, the classic suicide method: charcoal burning in a closed room, the air turning to poison. The forensic report lines up with that reading.

Eirene: I visited him on the evening of June 6. He kept a tidy home, but that day it was pristine. The bedroom door was closed, unusual for him. When he didn't answer, I tried to push the door.

Eirene: The door opened. The room reeked of charcoal, with fire still burning and windows all sealed. It was warm, stifling. Wells just sat there, immaculately dressed, a chessboard before him, music playing behind.

Eirene: The game was unfinished. He'd taken his usual afternoon nap, drifting through some fond memory. Then he woke, brewed a pot of tea, put on his favorite music, and spent the rest of the afternoon with an old photo album, lost in the past.

Eirene: He was wearing a smile. When I walked in, the melody was still playing on repeat.

**【Advance】**

- Go check it out

**end**

You continue searching and find the record she mentioned, sitting quietly on the turntable.

Eirene: The violin's phrasing in that piece... It's remarkably similar to Phanuel's master recording.

Inspection Member: Chief, we've screened all suspicious evidence. No active Mania signatures found.

The problem most likely lies with the turntable. You nod once, signaling the others to step out, then move forward alone.

You need to hear this dangerous "Cursed Melody" with your own ears.

Chief: Monitor my vitals. If anything goes wrong, disable the turntable remotely first.

Inspection Member: We could stay with you, Chief.

Chief: We'll do controlled testing in the lab later. Conditions here are limited, and I have a resistance to Mania. You don't.

Chief: Besides, I've got a cutting-edge killing machine right beside me.

Three full seconds have passed, and the black triangle remains still. Only when you fix it with a look does it finally stir, rising and spinning in a circle.

**【Advance】**

- Play the music

## N11-4 Ritual

The moment you enter the manor, darkness closes in. Heavy velvet drapes hang from above like moving walls, carving the space into narrow corridors, just wide enough for two to walk side by side. Flickering candles offer the only light, and farther in, even that is swallowed.

A thin lace blindfold is placed over your eyes, sealing away the last trace of that faint glow. As you move forward, the drapes shift, and through the velvet, you catch the faint murmur drifting from another passage just beside yours.

Voices Nearby: ...situation... hard to predict. We need guidance...

Voices Nearby: ...Vision, huh... I'd welcome one...

In the darkness, the soft rustle of fabric approaches. Someone is coming. Eirene takes a small step forward and murmurs a few words to the unseen figure.

Their voices are low, words fragmenting into pieces you half-catch and half-lose, punctuated by low, unsettling chuckles.

Does the tension in your shoulders give you away? The murmuring pauses, and then Eirene's hand comes to rest lightly over yours.

Eirene: "Such devotion, my dear. Ease yourself; everyone here is a friend. Walk forward. This is the divine guidance. Forward."

Those words are meant for many ears, but you take away only one thing: she is about to leave you, and you must go forward alone.

**end**

Through the veil of lace, you see only fragments: silhouettes slipping past one another, candle flames trembling, velvet drapery falling like liquid shadow. The scene feels almost hallucinatory, like a painting in motion.

And with sight stolen, sound comes alive. Music pulses beneath the surface, voices rise and ebb, laughter trickles through; the hush of fine gowns, the chime of glass on glass. Some notes are startlingly clear, others float just out of reach. You listen, straining to make them out.

A dozen more figures file into the hall and ascend the steps. They are not like the jeweled guests scattered across the floor; they are robed, hooded, their faces tucked deep into shadow. Tall, but each one stoops, their movements slow and stiff, hovering somewhere between reverence and rigidity.

The hall suddenly plunges into darkness. Only a few candles remain, flickering at the base of the stone statue, their glow carving a strange altar out of the dark. A robed figure moves forward and guides the girl to step up and kneel. She does so without hesitation, her face blank. Then the chants begin: the opening of the rite, the offering of prayers, the formal words of the ceremony.

Amid the droning chorus, the presiding figure bends to the girl's ear, his posture grave, and asks in a soft voice...

Ritual Officiant: Helix, can you see it? Do you know how many thorns the Divine has bestowed upon you?

Helix looks lost. She shakes her head, and the officiant's shoulders sag at once. He lets out a long, disappointed breath.

Ritual Officiant: Very well. If the answer comes to you, speak up, and speak truthfully. The Divine is watching.

Ritual Officiant: Then we begin. The Divine grants you Its blessing... and Its thorns upon your flesh...

The girl watches blankly as he straightens to his full height and raises both hands. A pale violet smoke stirs in the wake of his robes, curling upward and gathering slowly until, before the stone idol, it coalesces into a spike nearly a meter long. Then, without warning—

The spike plunges down, piercing through her right shoulder.

Chief: !!

Your voice is swallowed at once by a swell of gasps, then applause and laughter. The onlookers shift and murmur, caught up in the charge of the moment.

At the center of it all, the girl stays kneeling, her gaze fixed on some distant point above, her body trembling ever so faintly.

Ritual Officiant: Does it hurt?

"Helix": It... doesn't hurt...

Ritual Officiant: Good. Very good... Let the ritual continue. Let the thorns cleanse you of sin and suffering...

A second spike coalesces and falls. Your body answers before your mind does, muscles tightening, and Eirene's hand is already there, settling over yours.

Eirene: It's a necessary "show." They need the spectacle of pain to make it sacred. Steady yourself, my dear. Don't break character.

Eirene: In this game, you think before making every move.

The spikes keep coming... third, fourth, a slow and relentless rain. Yet the girl does not flinch, does not try to dodge or flee, not even a whisper of resistance. Even her trembling has dulled, almost imperceptible, as if something has drawn all sensation from her.

Is it the incense? The music? Or the low, unbroken chant of the officiant, that voice so flat and empty at first, yet now blooming with a strange, creeping fervor?

Ritual Officiant: Sillage shall guide you through the narrow gate. Sillage shall guide you through the narrow gate. Sillage shall guide you through the narrow gate...

"Helix": ...Ngh...

...Tenth. Eleventh. Twelfth. The candlelight pulls her shadow long and thin, like a clump of soil planted with flowers, lifted onto the altar and offered to the nameless statue. Her blood flows in thin streams, filling grooves cut long before tonight, and the full pattern seals itself, an eerie circular sigil.

Helix's lips part, but no sound comes out. The guiding officiant beside her wears a mask of grief, but his eyes betray him, bright and hungry. He whispers in her ear, soothing, encouraging, feeding the flames from the inside.

Ritual Officiant: You're doing beautifully, Helix... Just one more. Remember this pain. This is the weight of devotion. Endure, and the Divine will grant you your reward.

The violet smoke gathers in the air once more, slower this time, sharp and solemn, poised above the scapegoat's heart. The spectators look on, necks craning, utterly entranced by the sight. One by one, their voices join the chant, swelling in a single, eager breath...

Chanting Crowd: Sillage shall guide you through the narrow gate; death shall ferry you into eternal life...

The world narrows to a dull hum in your skull; the edges of your vision blur. The spikes, slick with her blood, appear to shiver. Her hollow gaze breaks from the cruel statue at last as her body tips backward, her eyes finding nothing to hold on to.

"Helix": ...Please... reward me... let me... offer myself... to the Divine... It does not hurt...

At last, that hollow gaze drifts in your direction.

"Helix": ...It does not hurt... at all...

The moment the spike pierces her heart, her body seizes with a sharp, involuntary shudder, and yours answers in kind.

Chief: Those things... Ngh!

Within minutes, a dozen cuts have been carved across your body, and you are being forced step by step into the corner. Close combat like this is unforgiving for anyone not trained to the bone.

You drive the shackles forward, lashing out in a blaze of red, but it is barely enough to keep them at bay. The Puppets keep coming, and some are reckless enough to push straight into your reach. This is, after all, a gathering of the privileged, and the Mania saturation in the air is too thin to fuel the power you need. And besides...

"Helix": ...

The girl you took from their grip is still pressed against your side. Her torn body clings to life only through your shackles, and that same connection feeds her pain directly into you, piling weight upon weight.

Do you regret the recklessness? Should you cut the dead weight loose and salvage what you can? Hah... If you could do that, you wouldn't be in this mess. You spit blood and slash a charging Puppet open with the shackles.

At least the terrain isn't a total loss. You have two walls at your back, enough to hold a line. You dig in, reset your stance, and scan the crowd. All you need is time, space, and your backup must already be on its way—

Voice from Behind: Found you.

The strike comes from nowhere, a hard, cold intrusion that punches through your back and out through your right ribs. It's a blade of black crystal. The wall you had leaned against has come apart, opening into a crevice that was never there, and from the dark behind it, the officiant's masked head surfaces, his voice curling against your ear.

Flawed Schorl: Data analysis incoming from EDGE-07. A faint spatial disturbance has been detected in this area, consistent with the Underground's typical patterns for concealing dimensional fissures. Operation target confirmed.

Chief: ...Cough... a... fiss—

A Mania dimensional fissure. But the words stick in your throat. Your blood and the corruption on the blade have fused, setting your lungs ablaze; each breath scrapes through you like fire, and the taste of iron floods your mouth.

Helix's body slackens, beginning to slide from your grasp. You want to hold her, but you also need to free a hand to strike. The shackles answer neither. The spikes flicker and fade, and from the shadow comes your enemy's low, mocking laugh.

Ritual Officiant: Did you really believe it would obey you? Did you think I wouldn't end you? No... only the merciful Sage is too soft-hearted for that. Every last one of us wants you dead.

Ritual Officiant: You stole the power of the great one. Even death is too good for you!

He rips the blade free from your body and raises it again. Then a thin, precise beam of blue light slices through the chaos, forcing him back into the fissure. You fall to the ground, Helix still cradled against you, and a familiar voice presses into your mind.

Flawed Schorl: Operation terminated. Forced purge initiated. Chief!

The east wall caves in, fire and dust billowing through the gap. A palm-sized black triangle shoots through the breach, weaving through the screaming crowd and frenzied devotees, heading straight for you.

Fresh fractures run across its shell, arcs of electricity spitting from each scar. The struggle to reach you must have been brutal; the noise of battle still bleeds through the opening, your backup locked in their own fight. This was a strike thrown together in haste.

Or worse: the enemy had already laid their trap.

Chief: ...Too late... pull them back... fissures everywhere... it's all... traps...

Flawed Schorl: Your condition is critical. Stop thinking and focus on using the shackles to purge the contamination from your body. Leave this place to m—

Chief: Schorl!

A dark maw, three meters wide, tears open beside the black triangle and consumes it without pause. The connection between you is gone in an instant, swallowed by a flood of Mania too dense to push through. And an attack like this could only have been delivered by—

Eirene. The space beside her, once kept open for you, is now occupied by hooded zealots who regard her with something close to worship. In that moment, every piece falls into place.

- ...It's you... you... don't—

**end**

You open your mouth to invoke the prohibition, but only a wet, broken rasp comes out. Your right lung is done. You crumple, air dragging in shredded gasps, and above you, Eirene stands still, watching it all unfold.

Eirene: Charity is a fine virtue, Chief, but not on this board... The game is lost. End it now, and you'll at least have some peace.

Eirene: I genuinely wanted to witness your victory. A shame.

...Genuinely? A trap from the start, or a game played on both sides? The question is meaningless now; you have nothing left to press it with.

The incense folds over you, your senses smearing at the edges, leaving only the sharp, ringing ache in your ears. From all sides, sound floods in: footsteps, curses, laughter, the rising tide of a room that has already surrendered to frenzy.

The ritual was close to its end, but you have given them something better. A new source of amusement, unexpected and therefore exquisite. Voices multiply, twisting into the chant. The golden eye contorts and warps within the rifts, the sigil growing more grotesque with each flicker of the firelight.

<ins>Incense-Shrouded Crowd: Kill him! Kill him! Sacrifice the beast!</ins><br><ins>Incense-Shrouded Crowd: Kill her! Kill her! Sacrifice the beast!</ins>

Incense-Shrouded Crowd: The Great One is about to descend!

The chanting swells in waves, rolling over you like a flood. The incense smoke thickens, condensing into a spike in the air, and this time, it's aimed at you.

Ritual Officiant: No one left to fool this time. We will clear the path for the great ones...

Pain recedes into the distance. The edges of your vision draw inward, narrowing like a closing door. One by one, your cells seem to let go, your body sinking deeper into a cold, growing darkness. Only sound remains. Only sound...

Then, abruptly, the world falls silent.

You are not dead yet.

A sliver of clarity cuts through the fog. The incense recedes, the air cools, and sound pulls away entirely. Your shackles register a faint tremor in the Mania field. A dimensional fissure? Where have you been taken?

Ritual Officiant: ...No, you shouldn't be out here... An intruder slipped in. It's not safe... Please, Virtuoso, go back. We have it handled.

Thin Female Voice: ...But I heard a different melody. As if someone had come... someone I have waited a very long time to meet...

Thin Female Voice: Is that you?

Someone draws close, crouching beside you. A hand lifts to your face, fingers brushing the hair from your forehead with a gentleness that feels out of place. You cannot see who it is.

All you register is the cold of her touch, a chill that matches the one spreading through your own body.

Thin Female Voice: ...No... I was mistaken. Forgive me...

Thin Female Voice: You two really are so alike...

*Like whom?*

You part your lips to speak, but nothing leaves your throat.

Ritual Officiant: No... No! This one is an enemy! Those shackles were stolen! Don't be fooled!

Thin Female Voice: ...Yes, I understand. I know who stands with me...

The voice shifts away. At your side, "Helix" lets out a small, broken sound as blood begins to well from her wounds again. The shackles are losing their hold, and she no longer has the strength to say it doesn't hurt. Her body is no longer hers to command.

"Helix": Sob... sob...

Thin Female Voice: There is still time. If anyone can, it would be you...

You cannot tell if her words were for you. The figure beside you withdraws, and what takes her place is music, a single violin playing the same melody that had poured through the ritual hall... The Cursed Melody.

Thin and lingering, yet profound. It flows slowly, brushing against you, wrapping around you, then gently lifting the last thread of your consciousness upward.

Thin Female Voice: ...Next time... please take better care of yourself...

Thin Female Voice: I still want to talk with you...

Your consciousness rises, soft and unburdened, as your body releases its grip without resistance. The violin's melody carries you, weightless, away from the ground and into the distance.

Below, the last thing you see is "yourself," motionless on the ground, and beside that still shape, a slender, indistinct figure whose face you cannot make out.

## N11-5-B "Vision"

The drapes are thrown open. So noisy, and it stinks! You rip off whatever is covering your eyes.

...It's the black lace blindfold, the one Eirene tied over your eyes before you entered. As your thoughts begin to clear, you take in your surroundings—

Drapes, candlelight, a stone statue, incense saturating the air. A violin's melody weaves through the space, and faces, strangely familiar, pass in and out of view.

Ritual Participant: The "Vision"... I know about that. Word is that's exactly how Stewart pulled off that comeback a few years back. Everyone in the Society wanted it back then, but those so-called saints or sages just wouldn't budge. Heh... now that they're backed into a corner, they finally decide to show their hand and beg for help.

Ritual Participant: You mean Sillage's Sage of Death herself is actually going to show up tonight? Really? There are quite a few matters I'd like to have her "divine" for me.

...What's going on? Where's Eirene?

The shackles orient you, and you turn. Eirene is a dozen meters away, laughing and trading words with someone in fine clothes. They wear that particular mix of deference and arrogance, the kind that usually belongs to Sillage Society types. She is working them easily, gathering information—just as you expected.

...Just as you expected? Strange. Why are you so certain of her move? Since when is she someone you trust?

Eirene: I went sniffing around. First time here for me too. It's easier to move alone.

Her eyes find yours, and she gives the barest tilt of her glass, her smile smooth as silk. Then, right before your mind's eye—

Eirene: The game is lost. End it now, and you'll at least have some peace.

Eirene: I genuinely wanted to witness your victory. A shame.

${PlayerName}: !!

*What was that?*

*Your mind playing tricks on you? A hallucination? You can't go to her before you find out the truth. You have to—*

A body pitches toward you without warning. You move on reflex this time, stepping aside, and a face you recognize swings past before the body hits the ground hard.

Glamorous Young Lady: Ah! Ow...

Glamorous Young Lady: You... you dodged me?! Do you have any idea who I—

Glamorous Young Lady: Come on now... There's so much more to the ritual. Let me teach you.

You recognize the face. You also know exactly what she came here to do.

**end**

You free yourself from her reach and press through the crowd, scanning behind the wall of curtains for a way out, but the room resists. The incense clings, the violin winds, and your chest tightens in uneven pulses. Your heart pounds, cold sweat tracing down your spine.

In the end, you find a shadowed corner, press your back against it, and breathe deep until the trembling in your body begins to subside.

Then, more memories surface like fragments of a shattered mirror. You reach for each, turn them over, and try to read what they hold.

Eirene: Then we'll have to watch closely, my dear. This time, they might actually send someone worth our time.

Ritual Officiant: Found you.

Flawed Schorl: Operation terminated. Forced purge initiated. Chief!

"Helix": ...It does not hurt... at all...

Is that a hallucination, or something lived? And is it only you? The sensation clings like a second skin: the weight of dying, the piercing pain in your right lung, the corrosive heat of that blade still threaded through your nerves. But when you press your hand to your chest, there is nothing but whole flesh beneath your palm.

You press harder, using the pressure to hold yourself in place.

*Breathe.* The disorientation started after you entered. What could be the cause? The cloying incense? Some hidden sorcery? Or maybe—

Thin Female Voice: ...Next time... please take better care of yourself...

The final image of that memory is still stamped behind your eyes, and with it, the same violin melody still wrapping through the air. Is this the power of the Cursed Melody?

Whatever the answer, one thing is already certain: you are under the influence of an enemy's power. Something is wrong. And this operation has just become a different kind of gamble.

9th: If anything goes wrong, Chief, create a disturbance on your end. We'll breach at the signal and link up with you as quickly as possible.

It cleared inspection as a simple brooch, but hidden beneath the ornament is a fine needle, coated in neurotoxin, enough to serve as a weapon if needed. And for backup, you know the terminals are already in place, hidden along the outer edges of the manor, the courtyard, the sills of windows, waiting to be used.</ins><br><ins>Your hand moves to the silver piece at your waist. But you cannot overlook the enemies woven into the crowd. In the "Vision" you were shown, this space is laced with dimensional fissures, and Eirene...you can't let yourself trust her too easily. Be careful. You must be very careful. That "ritual" is almost here, and you—

Chief: ...?! This feeling...

A faint, unanticipated presence stirs at the other end of the shackles.

The bell tolls, punctual and heavy. A dozen robed figures enter the hall and ascend the steps, their frames bent, their movements stiff, almost inhuman. The spectators turn, just as you recall, fixing their attention on the coming spectacle.

In the low drone of chanting, the lead figure lifts both hands high.

Ritual Officiant: Bow before the abyss, chosen ones. Offer your prayer to the chaos.

Then, the petite girl is brought forward again, barefoot, hood drawn back to expose her pale cyan hair. This time, her complexion is ashen, and she has forgotten even the words of the chant.

The hall plunges into darkness. A robed figure moves forward and guides the girl to step up and kneel. Amid the chants of ceremony and prayers, the officiant leans in and asks the girl in a soft voice.

Ritual Officiant: Helix, can you see it? Do you know how many thorns the Divine has bestowed upon you?

Helix does not answer. Her gaze remains fixed somewhere above, her lips trembling in silence as the pause stretches. The officiant's shoulders drop; he exhales, rises, and begins to summon the violet smoke—

When the cyan-haired girl at the altar snaps her eyes open and shouts—

"Helix": I see it!

Ritual Officiant: Stop!

Helix shivers, her eyes fixed on the stone statue, on something far beyond. The hall holds its breath, every voice stilled in a single, waiting hush.

"Helix": ...I see sacred thorns piercing through my body, cleansing my sins.

Ritual Officiant: Do you see the thorns?

Her body stiffens. She gives a trembling nod.

Ritual Officiant: Who... showed it to you...

"Helix": ...It's the Vision... I... I saw the Vision...

The mention of "Vision" ignites the room. A restless stir passes through the gathered breath, and the officiant, trembling all over, bows low over her, his voice taut with reverence and barely contained fervor.

Ritual Officiant: Then... in the Vision, how many blessings do you see the Divine send down?

"Helix": ...Thirteen. There are thirteen...

The officiant lets out a shriek and bursts into a wild, senseless tirade.

Ritual Officiant: All... correct! Without knowing the ritual details, our lamb has foretold the blessings! She has received the divine guidance! The ritual is a success! The Vision is upon us!!

Ritual Officiant: By the sacred melody, your soul is redeemed, your sins washed away. Behold the Vision for us and show us the future!

The hall surges into an uproar. The crowd roars with approval; they understand none of the officiant's ravings, yet they sing praise to the Divine and applaud the promise of fortune. Even you manage to release the breath you were holding.

"Helix" is helped down from the altar, still trembling, but she no longer needs to endure the trials again. Her face is pale, yet something radiant lingers in it; her eyes, no longer hollow, are bright with a strange, unmistakable joy.

She saw it too. She knew exactly what death was waiting for her, and she spoke the number thirteen with unsettling certainty. She witnessed the full ritual, just as you did. And more importantly...

The shackles. The connection is faint, almost lost, but unmistakable. There is a thread of your power still lingering on her, the force you released in the "vision" to save her. It has somehow followed her here.

Now, pulled by that same thread, your eyes meet across the space between you.

"Helix": ...You're...

- Shhh—

**end**

You press a finger to your lips, a silent reminder to be cautious.

"Helix": ...

Your heart hammers against your ribs. It feels as though you have just survived death beside her, again. But this time, she is still here, and so are you. Linked through the shackles, you hold each other's presence through the surrounding madness.

What was only your private doubt now has a second witness, and the shackles to confirm it might all be true.

Did you both live through a rewind of time? Or share the same illusion? The shackles on her are faint, maybe because of what happened. Is this the glimpse of the future they spoke of?

Whatever the answer, you are not alone anymore. And fate is not sealed. The future can still be rewritten. This time, she will live. You will both live. You will see to that.

You have to think it through. Weigh your side against theirs. The Undergrounders have not sensed a thing; they are still parading "Helix" in front of their patrons, hawking the promise of the "future." Even Eirene hasn't so much as glanced your way. That is a significant advantage. Before you draw a line, there are many things you can still set in motion...

You are still weighing options when your gaze catches Helix on the stage, watching you for a long moment. Then, her hand rises. The motion is slow, almost dreamlike. And suddenly—

Fire catches in her eyes. She points directly at you and lets out a shriek that cuts through the room.

"Helix": The faithless! The infidel! The Vision has revealed the devil to me! The serpent is here! Right before us!

"Helix": A devil who desecrates the Divine! An enemy among us!!

Chief: ????!

## N11-6-B Through Fate

"Helix's" cry cuts through the air so abruptly that it shocks you and everyone else in the hall. Even the robed figures are caught off guard. You seize the last opening, yanking at the drapes as you run, throwing the space into disarray, and race away from their entry point.

You clear the main hall and find yourself in a long corridor. A handful of people loiter there, still oblivious. You hurry past them and press yourself into a corner, out of sight.

"Helix": The faithless! The infidel! The Vision has revealed the devil to me! The serpent is here! Right before us!

Chief: ...Damn it.

You were too quick to assume. You saw her as a victim and believed she would stand with you. But her eyes told a different truth. She is one of Mania's most fervent followers. She shares your experience, which means she is either your companion or your most lethal foe.

Intelligence is thin on the ground. You strain to think clearly while keeping your senses trained on the world beyond. Outside, the footsteps draw nearer.

Nearby Socialite A: What is this? I was promised absolute security tonight. How did a stranger slip in?

Nearby Socialite B: Tch. So much for your precious "Vision." We're exposed. Where's the escape route? Get us out, now. We can't afford to be implicated.

Ritual Officiant: Keep your composure. It's only one person.

<ins>Ritual Officiant: The Vision is upon us. Fate is with us. Seal every exit and find him.</ins><br><ins>Ritual Officiant: The Vision is upon us. Fate is with us. Seal every exit and find her.</ins>

You cannot stay here much longer. Your eyes sweep the corridor's far end. To one side, a towering stained-glass window looks out onto the courtyard, a drop of roughly seven or eight meters. To the other, a narrow passage meant only for servants. Your choices are few.

The footsteps grow louder by the second. There is no time left to hesitate.

  ...Is this the end?

  That melody again. You heard it the last time too, in those final moments before you "woke."

  this time, you would find a better way.

  The shackles release in a violent surge, painting the corridor blood-red. The towering window shatters and blasts outward. You do not hesitate. You sprint forward, launch yourself through the gap, and let gravity take you. You hit the courtyard shrubs with a bone-jarring crash.

  <ins>Socialite A: The window?! He jumped out the window!</ins><br><ins>Socialite A: The window?! She jumped out the window!</ins>

  <ins>Socialite B: Quick! To the courtyard! Cut him off!</ins><br><ins>Socialite B: Quick! To the courtyard! Cut her off!</ins>

  You drag yourself deeper into the thick shrubs, the fall stealing the air from your lungs. Glass shards are buried deep in your shoulders and arms. Once you're sure no one is nearby, you push your shackles to work, mending the wounds.

  Hand-to-hand combat is not your forte, but a communicator is close. If you can hold on long enough to heal, you might still make it out.

  But the moment your shackles glow red, Helix's face flickers across your mind. It is already too late.

  Ritual Officiant: Found you.

  A familiar dimensional fissure splits the air once more. From its depths steps the faithful of the Underground's golden eye, unhurried and deliberate. He stands over you, looking down at your broken form with quiet judgment.

  Ritual Officiant: Did you truly believe the shackles would protect you? No... we have Helix now. The Divine chose her, blessed her. She sees the Vision, the future, and the Divine's power itself...

  Ritual Officiant: And by reaching for what you stole, you've given yourself away...

  He lowers himself into a crouch. Then he presses the corrupted blade back into your wound, pushing it deeper, inch by agonizing inch.

  Ritual Officiant: Now, die.

  Your right lung again... First comes the searing burn, the desperate choke for air. Then the cold creeps in, dull and heavy. As the world fades, you piece it together. It's Helix.

  The very shackles you used to save her life are now her weapon against you. The connection is faint, but the instant you call upon your Potestas, she will know exactly where you are. Then the killers will pour through the fissures, and your legs will not carry you far enough.

  You cannot touch that power. Not once. Not ever.

  Ritual Officiant: Virtuoso? Why...

  Ritual Officiant: Don't tell me—

  That melody again. You heard it the last time too, in those final moments before you "woke."

  Your consciousness rises, soft and unburdened. The violin's melody carries you, weightless, away from the ground and into the distance.

  If you could start over, you would remember this one truth: the shackles must never be used lightly again.

- Both options are risky, think again

  The manor's second floor is a good seven or eight meters above the ground. You are no Eugene. A leap from that window would either kill you outright or leave you broken beyond use. The service passage is too exposed; slipping in there is practically an invitation to be cornered, and another chase solves nothing. You need time. Real time.

  Perhaps there is still one move left to make—

  **【Advance】**

  - Create a diversion!

  **end**

  You rip the curtain down with one hand and snatch a service cart with the other. You bundle the fabric over the cart, lash it tight, and drive the whole thing straight into the stained-glass window.

  Wine bottles, silver trays, and candlesticks plummet into the courtyard along with the overturned cart. The heavy curtain unfurls in a glittering cascade of shattered glass, twisting and catching the light as it falls, a perfect mimicry of a body crashing through the window.

  <ins>Another Socialite: The window?! He jumped out the window!</ins><br><ins>Another Socialite: The window?! She jumped out the window!</ins>

  <ins>Nearby Socialite: Quick! To the courtyard! Cut him off!</ins><br><ins>Nearby Socialite: Quick! To the courtyard! Cut her off!</ins>

  The crash pulls every eye and every footstep in that direction. They will see through the trick soon, but soon is not now. And now is all you need.

  You have already slipped soundlessly into the service passage. You erase your tracks with care, reset the entrance as if untouched, and melt into the waiting dark.

  But you cannot linger here. The service passage offers cover for now, but it will be the first place they search once the ruse wears thin.

  Yet the corridor weaves through the manor like a hidden vein, with several exits within reach. The nearest lead to the back kitchen, the VIP lounge, and an odd room labeled the effigy chamber.

  In your hands are a shard of broken glass and a bottle of strong liquor taken from the cart. Now, you must pick a direction and commit.

  **【Choose One】【Key】**

  - Kitchen

    The kitchen at least offers weapons, fire, and plenty of fuel for chaos. With that thought, you push the hidden door open.

    The stoves roar with heat. Knives and long forks gleam on the counters. The problem is that the room is not empty. You step straight into someone the instant you cross the threshold.

    Maid: Ahhh!!!

    The maid shrieks and stumbles backward, and every pair of eyes in the room locks onto you immediately. Screams erupt. Someone runs to raise the alarm. Others grab whatever is near and brace to strike. You smash the liquor bottle into the roaring stove and snatch a cleaver from the counter, brandishing it like a warning.

    But this is not a simple kitchen brawl. And you know it.

    Ritual Officiant: Found you.

    Something sharp sinks into your back. That same black crystalline blade, unmistakable. Behind you, beside the roaring stove, the air splits open. A Mania fissure gapes wide, and the fanatic pushes halfway through.

    Ritual Officiant: Quite the gamble. Or have you panicked to the point of believing this could be your way out? Heheh...

    The right lung again... Is this the end? Or perhaps...

    Ritual Officiant: Virtuoso? Why...

    Ritual Officiant: Don't tell me—

    Your consciousness rises, soft and unburdened. The violin's melody carries you, weightless, away from the ground and into the distance.

    The VIP lounge should hold key figures from the city's upper circles. You recall that this entire gathering was orchestrated by the Underground as a barter of interests. If you could take one of them hostage, maybe—

    You press your ear to the panel, gauge the movement inside, and shove the hidden door open. The VIP guests barely turn before you have the nearest woman in your grip, the glass shard pressed against her throat.

    Chief: Get me a terminal. Then I'll go. Now!

    <ins>Female Socialite: Sob... What are you standing there for?! Do what he says!</ins><br><ins>Female Socialite: Sob... What are you standing there for?! Do what she says!</ins>

    The room stays frozen. You keep the woman close, your back edging toward the door, eyes scanning every corner of the room. But the dread in your chest does not fade. And then—

    Ritual Officiant: Found you.

    The air splits open. A Mania fissure gapes behind you, and the fanatic surges through. The black crystalline blade sinks into your back once more, driven forward with savage force. It pierces through you and keeps going, punching straight into the hostage in your arms. They do not care. They will kill anyone to reach you.

    Ritual Officiant: Quite the gamble. Or have you panicked to the point of believing this could be your way out? Heheh...

    The right lung again... Is this the end? Or perhaps...

  - Effigy chamber

    You push the hidden door open and step into a dimly lit chamber. Tall, unfinished stone statues loom in the shadows, some adorned with tendril-like wings, others scarred by three deep slashes across their faces. Flickering candle stubs cast unsteady light, and a faint incense hangs in the air. The vast room holds only one occupant.

    He kneels before the grotesque assembly, murmuring prayers to no one. Then he dips his hand in water to trace a third line on his brow, swaying as though the ritual has claimed him entirely.

    Robed Sorcerer A: Sillage shall guide you through the narrow gate... death shall ferry you into eternal life...

    **【Advance】**

    - Take him down quietly

    **end**

    You slip through the shadows cast by the towering statues, closing in without a sound. One clean motion, and the silver needle punctures the side of his neck. The nerve agent floods his system in an instant. His body locks up, frozen mid-prayer, and no sound leaves his throat.

    Robed Sorcerer A: Mmm... mmph...

    He slips into unconsciousness. You lower him gently, then drag his body behind the statues. There is no time to breathe. Footsteps are racing down the hall, growing louder by the second. You quickly pull the fallen man's cloak over your shoulders, return to his kneeling spot, and bow your head, matching the posture he held just moments ago.

    Across the room, a wider door bursts open. The chaos spills in, shouts and commands flooding the chamber. The entire manor is being turned upside down in search of you.

    And the closest threat is the robed figure now filling the doorway of the effigy chamber.

    Robed Sorcerer B: Did you see the intruder?

    You glance back over your shoulder, staying tucked in the shadows where light does not reach. Your voice carries with just the right amount of bewilderment.

    **【Advance】**

    - Intruder?

    **end**

    Robed Sorcerer B: ...You don't know anything? You've been here the whole time?

    He poses the question, yet makes no move to approach. He remains rooted in the doorway, framed by the light, his gaze fixed on you with quiet scrutiny.

    **【Advance】**

    - I've been at prayer. What happened?

    **end**

    He gives no reply. Instead, he lifts a hand and gestures for you to come closer.

    Robed Sorcerer B: It's not safe here anymore. Come with me.

    He is not calling you over out of care. The gesture is a command, sharp with suspicion. He wants you to step into the light, to bare your face. The incense curls upward in lazy wisps. The statues cast long, jagged shadows. In the distance, the hunt continues, shouts echoing through the building. You feel a gathering force of Mania coalescing in his palm.

    Do you close the distance, or do you bolt? Use the shackles, or the silver needle? How long until his people flood this room? Can you survive a fight with them?

    Your hand finds the silver needle in your sleeve; the other brushes the liquor bottle at your waist. Just then—

    BONG—

    BONG——

    BONG——————

    Chief: ?!

    Three bell tolls roll across the night, and the building goes still. The fanatics who had been hunting you through the corridors freeze mid-step. It lasts only a heartbeat before—

    A chain of blasts rocks the ground. Windows blow inward. Dust showers from the ceiling. It is a declaration of war, loud and unmistakable. At the doorway, the robed sorcerer also spins toward the noise.

    Robed Sorcerer B: ...The east wing?

    It hits you too. It's your allies. They've made their move!

    <ins>Eugene: Jeez, I almost had a heart attack there! I thought Chief just fell off the building! I mean, with those glass bones of his, two meters and he'd break something; three and he'd be done for. Thank goodness...</ins><br><ins>Eugene: Jeez, I almost had a heart attack there! I thought Chief just fell off the building! I mean, with those glass bones of hers, two meters and she'd break something; three and she'd be done for. Thank goodness...</ins>

    Wynn: Thank goodness for what?! All that ruckus means it's bad inside. Go in there and get Chief away from those freaks!

    9th: Any word from EDGE's high-tech machine? Still no trace of Chief?

    <ins>Flawed Schorl: ${PlayerName} exited through a window on the second floor, south corridor. Confirmed as a diversionary tactic, but it is the last verified location of his activity.</ins><br><ins>Flawed Schorl: ${PlayerName} exited through a window on the second floor, south corridor.     <ins>Flawed Schorl: 9th and I search outward from that point. His pace is fast; pinpointing his position will require time. Eugene, lead your team into the east wing.
