    Flawed Schorl: Remember: avoid engagement and do not compromise your cover. The priority is reaching Chief. Everything else is secondary.

    A laugh almost escapes you. That staged fall from the window, a last-ditch distraction, somehow became your allies' homing beacon. Before you could reach a communicator, they had already moved!

    Now, in front of you, the robed devotee lifts his hand. A faint light of sorcery glows at his fingertips. He is about to open a fissure, to escape, and maybe to summon help against your people. But this time, the fear is gone. You step forward, fast and sure.

    Chief: I'll go with you.

    He no longer sees you as a threat. A low grunt of acknowledgment, and he turns his back without a second thought. You strike. The silver needle sinks into his neck with surgical precision, and the body of your second prey goes slack. You drag him into the shadow of the statues and search him quickly.

    A sorcerer, just as you suspected. In his robe, you find a black talisman carved with cryptic symbols. The Mania still clinging to it is cold and alien. This may be the trigger for their dimensional fissures.

    You have no grasp of the Underground sorcery, so you leave the talisman behind. You memorize the engravings instead, then wedge the thing beneath a statue's base. Staying is not an option. You tug your hood low and slip out the door.

    Outside, the corridor is thick with robed figures, most surging toward the east wing. You fall in with the flow, letting your cloak obscure you, weaving through the chaos. All around you, muffled curses hiss through clenched teeth.

    Chaotic Crowd: Filthy, stupid, wretched swine... You bring your dirty explosives into this holy place? You dare challenge us—

    Chaotic Crowd: We'll slaughter you all... That little rat who crawled in with you too... We'll pick your bones clean, every last one of you...

    You cannot afford to relax. Your allies' attack has punched a hole in the enemy's defenses, but dimensional fissures are still scattered throughout the building. You have seen how they strike, emerging from nowhere without warning. You must reach your allies before the enemy uses the fissures against them.

    Chaotic Crowd: ...Who's there?

    Tsk, are you caught again? You are about to change direction when a hand darts out and seizes your wrist. Before you can react, you are hauled behind a heavy curtain. Footsteps pound closer. The drape is ripped aside, and the pursuer freezes in place.

    What he finds is a pair of lovers wrapped in a close, breathless embrace. No one likes an intruder in these moments. And when the pursuer recognizes the woman, his face goes pale. He backs away, stammering apologies as he retreats.

    Nearby Socialite: ...Sorry, I didn't mean to interrupt.

    The footsteps recede into the distance. Light seeps through the gaps in the fabric, catching the edges of your faces. Half in shadow, half in glow, Eirene Campbell peels the disguise cloak from your shoulders. Her smile widens, slow and deliberate, as she meets your eyes.

    Eirene: Look at you struggling. Should've come to me first...

    Eirene: ...My dear.

  **end**

**end**

## N11-7-B Pawn

The thick velvet drapes drown out the distant noise of the hunt, reducing it to a muffled hum. Candlelight bathes the small space in warm crimson tones. Eirene's hair grazes your hand as she leans in, her whisper soft and close against your ear.

Eirene: One person, and you've turned the whole place upside down. Impressive as always. Now tell me, what happened? You were restless before the ritual even began.

Eirene: Why the sudden panic? That little lamb who "exposed" you, the Sinner called Helix. When did you meet her? Before she pointed her finger, what were you trying to say to her?

Eirene: They're guarding her like a treasure now. "Chosen by the Divine, able to see the future"... What about you, Chief?

Eirene: Have you seen the future too?

She is too close. Her perfume drifts into your space, layering over the dust, the sweat, and the blood you have carried through the night. It blurs a line you are not sure you want crossed.

Eirene: You should have come to me sooner. You know you are fighting a losing battle, so why face it alone? You can sense my position through the shackles.

**【Advance】**

- Move aside. I have nothing to say to you.

**end**

Eirene: Hm? Oh... I see now...

She takes another step, and there is no room left between you. Her fingers find your chin and push it gently, drawing your gaze down to meet hers. Those different-colored eyes hold yours without flinching.

Eirene: You're wary of me. In that "future" you saw, did I turn on you?

Your entire body goes rigid. You force the words out between clenched teeth.

**【Advance】**

- You know which side you're on.

**end**

Eirene: I'd rather be on yours, of course. Four years is a long game. I don't want to lose that investment. A businesswoman has her limits, even on losses.

Eirene: If I'm disappointed enough, I'll cut my losses and walk away, painful as it may be.

Her stern tone fades just as quickly as it came, replaced by that familiar, easy smile.

Eirene: But of course, things are different now. You've come far, farther than I expected, and you've gained something extraordinary. I won't let you go now.

Eirene: This time, I'm with you. Let's win this game together.

She motions for you to look past the curtains, toward the turmoil beyond. Her gaze sweeps across the scene like a chess master studying the board, every piece accounted for.

Eirene: Look at the board now. Two sides, two "aces"—you and that Sinner named Helix. Foresight? Time reversal? Whichever it is, it's a game-changer.

Eirene: If I'm right, that "Mania dimension concealment technology" was just a smokescreen. This power we're talking about is what has kept the Underground hidden from EDGE all along.

Eirene: Heh... if I were them, and I knew you'd seen through my secret and copied my trump card, I'd have you killed on the spot. It's the perfect kill-or-be-killed board... Dangerous, certainly. But if we take them all, the spoils will be unprecedented.

Her eyes gleam with something unguarded. She is unreadable, untrustworthy, a woman of shifting motives. But the hunger in her gaze, at least, is genuine. Her greed has always been her most honest quality.

**【Advance】**

- ...Keep listening

**end**

Eirene: They have two advantages over us now. First: they understand the power better than you do. Second: from their deployment, the whole venue is likely laced with dimensional fissures. The terrain is theirs.

Eirene: Luckily, little Helix isn't the sharpest tool. She hasn't truly realized what she can do with that power yet... So before she does, we strike fast and play smart with everything at our disposal.

**【Advance】**

- ..."Everything at our disposal." Like what, exactly?

**end**

Eirene: Intelligence we've already gathered, chaos we can weaponize, their mistakes we can exploit, and above all... bait.

Eirene: Chief, your backup teams are already in motion, aren't they? Splendid...

Eirene: Right now, they are the perfect expendable pieces on the board.

Her words hit like a shockwave. Your head rings, and your companions' faces appear instantly, vivid and unrelenting.

Chief: What are you saying?

She feels the tension in your shoulder before you pull back. With a quiet smile, she lets her fingers slide down and settle over yours.

Eirene: It's a fair trade. If you can't stand the cost, once the path is open, you can always turn back time, can't you?

**【Advance】**

- And if that fails?!

**end**

Chief: This so-called Vision is the enemy's power. We don't know how it works. If anything happens to Eugene and the others—

She keeps her pose, her smile, her gaze fixed on you. But something behind her eyes darkens. Her grip on your hand tightens just a fraction.

Eirene: ...You're not really fooling yourself into thinking this power gives you unlimited attempts, are you? I told you, we must strike fast. The window is tight.

You freeze.

At some point, you had grown comfortable with the idea that "death" was not the end. That no matter how badly you failed, the melody would arrive in time to pull you back from death's door, grant you another chance, and set you back on course again.

But that melody is the Underground's Cursed Melody, their power. What makes you believe it turns back time for your sake? Is your death really the trigger? Are your chances truly infinite?

And what if one day you act on that faith, you die, and then... there is nothing after?

Eirene reads the recognition in your eyes, the weight finally settling in. She eases her hold on your hand and carries on.

Eirene: Those people came to back you up. Their best use is to carve a path for you. They should be ready to pay that price, and so should you. This is only the first round in the game of life and death. Don't disappoint me too soon.

Eirene: Now, while we still have time, tell me everything. I'll see to the strategy.

**【Choose One】【Key】**

- Accept her proposal

  ...Deep down, you know the truth of her words. The enemy outmatches everything you have faced. There is no path to victory that does not demand sacrifice.

  And if that price is the blood of your own comrades, you will still have to pay it.

  You accept Eirene's proposal. You rendezvous with Schorl immediately, while giving Eugene and 9th orders to advance along the designated route, drawing attention and misleading the enemy. They do not know they are merely pieces on the board, placed to buy time.

  Three Schorl units arrive in silence, carrying enough force to level this stronghold. Eirene moves on her end as well. Operating under the guise of the Underground's ally, she feeds false intelligence, guiding the enemy's main force step by step into your trap. Her betrayal leaves no trace. The Undergrounders only realize their mistake when the trap snaps shut.

  Now, the enemy and the expendable pieces are all in place, gathered within your reach.

  You press confirm. The attack order goes through.

  Wynn: No—!!

  BOOM!

  The explosion rips the corridor apart. Fire engulfs everything in its path: the Puppets, the sorcerers, and the silhouettes still charging forward under your command.

  Eugene does not even have time to look back.

  9th: ...Victory is all that matters. The fissure is open... Seize the chance, Chief... I have faith... in your resolve...

  9th: Since you've gambled us all on this... from here on... you must not fail...

  The explosion's aftershock severs the comms. You offer no reply. You force your eyes on the result, on the enemy you have sworn to hunt down, no matter the cost.

  In her elaborate design, Eirene leaves one route deliberately open. The Underground sorcerers throw themselves into a desperate rearguard, and she reads it as confirmation: the prize, the one who matters most, will be escorted through that very route. You set your ambush at the end, ready to close the trap.

  But the prey never comes.

  The escape path stays empty, through every last second.

  Ritual Officiant: You came back?! Go! We'll hold them off! Leave with the offering, Virtuoso!

  Afterward, you finally push through the fissure. High-energy strikes breach the dimensional barrier, dissolving the space within. Every Undergrounder perishes in the bombardment. The fallen rest beside broken Puppets, faces gone, their bodies merging into one indistinguishable ruin.

  Eirene: Still here, I see... Hah. I thought as much. Still letting personal feelings get in the way, even in a moment like this. That's precisely why you people never amount to anything.

  Eirene: Now, time to celebrate, Chief. Today was a strong start. With the two of us in this game, we'll take it all the way.

  The victory is absolute.

  In the time that follows, the Underground turns brittle. Their once-impeccable stealth crumbles, and the Sillage Society's ranks thin rapidly under arrest. Perhaps it's because they've lost their "foresight." They seem to have lost the one thing that kept them safe.

  You never grasp the true nature of the "time rewind." But in the end, it does not matter. You paid the price, and you won.

  Flawed Schorl: EDGE has dispatched scientists to retrieve the... remains. There may be actionable data from the analysis. Reports concerning those who fell during the operation have also been properly handled. No questions will be raised.

  Flawed Schorl: ...This is a meaningful victory. You chose the right partner and made the most rational decision.

  It lingers beside you for a heartbeat after speaking, then winds down to silence. The diagnostics confirm accumulated damage beyond recovery. It can no longer hold itself together.

  Not long after, a new Schorl arrives at your side. It is flawless, pristine, exactly like every other unit in its line, with no trace of the one that came before.

  The Underground falls silent for a long, long time. So long that you start to believe it is over, that peace has finally settled.

  Until that day—

  The Cursed Melody plays again.

  No longer scattered fragments on the dark web, but a Mania resonance that blankets everything. New murders, active Puppets, the Black Rings trembling, contamination readings spiking completely out of control. This time, the enemy is no longer the Sillage Society you once hunted. They have become something truly sinister, crawling back from hell itself, and you have already lost the only lead you had.

  And at last, in that grand and unstoppable performance...

  Chief: Cough!!

  A spike pierces your chest, driving straight through the heart, then fractures into a swarm of living, biting things that invade and devour you from within.

  It's the shackles. Someone else's, and much stronger than yours.

  "Master of the Shackles": Heheh... The so-called blood ties. One clean cut, and the satisfaction is immediate.

  The voice reaches you through the weapon itself, resonating with your very soul. You recognize it, from—

  Chief: Why... I thought the virtuoso was already...

  Where did you go wrong? What clue slipped past you? What chance did you fail to take? After everything you gave, is there still no path to a decent end?

  No one answers. Through the haze of your fading sight, two figures step into view. They look down at you. This time, you are the "spoil."

  "Master of the Shackles": Don't blame me for playing rough. This one's all about being rational and decisive. Couldn't care less about your sympathy.

  <ins>Sage of Death?: Alright, my mistake. When you were not around, I saw a bit of you in him, so I went easy. But I came around eventually, didn't I?</ins><br><ins>Sage of Death?: Alright, my mistake. When you were not around, I saw a bit of you in her, so I went easy. But I came around eventually, didn't I?</ins>

  Sage of Death?: ...You two are nothing alike. This is good. This is the best possible ending.

  You reach the end again. The familiar violin sound still lingers in the air, but it is no longer played for you.

  The melody that once pulled you back, again and again, is gone. Your awareness sinks, fraying at the edges, with nothing but silence left to fill the void. And then, together with the world around you...

  You arrive at the absolute, true death.

  DEAD END

- ...Absolutely no.

  You seize her wandering wrist and shove it back toward her, forcing space between you.

  Chief: ...I know what this is. You're pushing me because you've already hedged your bets. If I win, I'm a good piece on your board. If I lose, I'm your ticket into the Underground.

  Chief: I don't mind being used. But President, when you talk about the price to be paid—

  **end**

  Chief: You knew how much he cared for you. And you used his devotion, even his death, to gain my trust and drag me into your game. Was he just another pawn you were willing to sacrifice?

  Eirene: ...

  Her face goes cold. The warmth between you vanishes, replaced by something brittle and frozen.

  Eirene: Don't be foolish. Did you think victory was free? You want to win, you pay the price. And emotion is the cheapest thing you can spend.

  Eirene: Don't lose sight of what matters like them, Chief... Or you'll fall hard.

  Chief: But this isn't your chessboard. My comrades aren't your pawns. I'll win on my own terms.

  Chief: Stay out of my way and watch.

  You finish your words, snatch the cloak from her reach, and tear through the drapes without a second thought. You throw yourself into the battle beyond.

  Boom—

  Another blast rocks the manor. The building shudders. The hall dissolves into pandemonium, sorcery detonating in wild, scattered bursts. This is not the cleanest strategy. Too blind, too costly. Eirene would never approve a move like this.

  But this is not her move. This is the charge of your comrades, of those who are willing to burn everything for you. The least you can do is stand beside them through the fire.

  Robed Sorcerer B: ...The east wing?

  You still remember his words, and you immediately push east. It's not long before you spot the first familiar face among the chaos.

  He is sprawled in a widening pool of blood. Above him, a robed sorcerer, eyes wild with madness, plunges a dark dagger into his body over and over, laughing with every strike.

  **【Advance】**

  - Back off!!

  **end**

  You snatch a weapon from a dead hand, firing as you advance, and bring the stock down hard onto the sorcerer's skull. You then give him a furious kick, and he crumples. That takes everything you have left.

  When you finally gather Eugene's broken body into your arms and take his hand, the words will not come.

  Eugene: ...Chief? You're all right... Good...

  Eugene: Don't stay here. I'm just a decoy to draw their eyes. You go that way... find Schorl... get out...

  Eugene: I'll buy you time. Go... Chief...

  He stretches out his hand, mustering the last shred of strength left in him, and sends the candle tumbling against the wall. Fire catches the oil trail, races up the curtains, and spreads in every direction, sealing off the path of the hunters.

  The glow flickers across his pale face. He gives you one last smile.

  Eugene: ...Don't tell Wynn...

  You hear his final words, and you remain at Eugene's side for a long, still time. The fire climbs, the light wavers, and the strange cycle of everything that led here unfurls behind your eyes like a turning reel. Scenes spin past: shock, then panic, then this—

  Slowly, you steady yourself. In the shifting glow, every face, every choice, every second scattered across the chaos draws together and weaves into a single clear line.

  Only when the flames almost consume the corridor whole do you rise. You close Eugene's eyes.

  Chief: She won't know. I promise...

  You slip Eugene's weapon from his grip and stand. You run, not toward where he pointed, but away from it. As you move, you flip open his bloodied communicator.

  Chief: MBCC Chief speaking. How many of you are still up? How many can fight?

  Flawed Schorl: This is Eugene's frequency, Chief. Have you linked up? Send your coordinates. I'll pull you out now.

  **【Advance】**

  - No retreat

  **end**

  Chief: All personnel in the area, whatever happens, even if I'm wounded, don't let it distract you. Remember, you have only one objective—

  *You will not accept a tragedy like this.*

  Chief: All units, focus fire on every dimensional fissure you see. Do not engage the main hall. I repeat, do not engage the main hall. No one goes in there until I arrive. I want them all trapped there.

  Wynn: Roger! Support is moving in. All of you, stay safe!

  *No one deserves to bear that pain.*

  Chief: Schorl, you too. You only have one objective. Even if I fall, see it through. That's a priority directive!

  You know the way forward.

  Now you fight until there is nothing left to give.

**end**

Boom—

You are the first one through the door, charging back into the ritual hall and sending the "distinguished guests" into a panic. The ritual officiant at the far end locks eyes with you at once, and his expression curdles into pure disgust.

Ritual Officiant: You dare... you dare show your face here again?! You've defiled our sacred ritual with your filthy violence! You should have fled while you still could!

He is too far for you to make out his words, and you do not care to try. You raise your weapon toward the stone altar. The crowd throws itself around Helix, shielding the Vision seer, but you do not hesitate. You fire at the towering statue above them.

Shot after shot finds the statue's hollow eyes, and the rain of stone fragments draws a sharp, piercing scream.

Helix: NO—!! You can't—

<ins>Helix: A blasphemer, just as I saw! He's the Divine's greatest enemy! Kill him now! DO IT NOW!!</ins><br><ins>Helix: A blasphemer, just as I saw! She's the Divine's greatest enemy! Kill her now! DO IT NOW!!</ins>

She wails in grief, but you do not stop. You keep firing, shredding the statue's proud stone face until it is nothing but rubble. That breaks the hall wide open. The robed ones, sorcerers and Puppets alike, surge forward with a unified roar, and even the officiant abandons Helix to charge at you from the altar.

Spikes of violet smoke spear toward you from all sides. Chaos swallows the hall. Screams and furious roars blend into one deafening wall. Your strength is bleeding away, the music is silent still, and the rewind has not yet come.

You feel Eirene's presence in the hall through the shackles. Is she watching? Is she mocking you for charging in blind, for believing the rewind would always catch you?

Eirene: ...You're not really fooling yourself into thinking this power gives you unlimited attempts, are you? I told you, we must strike fast. The window is tight.

But none of that changes anything. Your comrades are not expendable pieces, and you will not leave them behind. Even if the rewind is the enemy's power, you will turn it against them. Even if fate is their weapon, you will wrest it from their hands. You have already decided. Now only one answer remains to be found—

**【Advance】**

- Helix!

**end**

Helix: !

Helix stands alone on the altar now. She flinches at the sight of you, soaked in blood. Her body shifts, instinct driving her to place herself between you and the statue, eyes fixed and wary.

Chief: Look at these spikes. They were meant for you. They were the weapons they used to kill you. I know you remember it all. You felt the pain. You were scared. You didn't want to die. And even after all that, you're still willing to lay down your life for them?

Her lips part, trembling, but no sound comes. The sight of you, pierced and bleeding, seems to wake something inside her. Her eyes waver, and she retreats a small step.

Helix: ...Pain... is sacred... I feel no pain... I am not worthy...

Helix: If a sacrifice so small could bring forth the Great Divine... then... I would ask for no greater honor!

Her moment of hesitation vanishes, swallowed by the familiar blaze of devotion. You recognize it now. She is too far gone. At least for now, there is no pulling her back.

Chief: ...Then there's nothing left to say. Remember the pain. Wake up! And then, clear the way for me!

**【Advance】**

- *Stay there. Don't move!*

**end**

Helix: !!!

Your voice cuts through the chaos and finds her. The prohibition locks in place immediately. Crimson shackles erupt from within Helix, causing no injury but freezing her completely in place.

Helix: No... why? I belong to the Great Divine... why can this thing...

Helix: Let go of me! I won't serve a devil! Let go...! The Great Divine... help me!

Helix: Nngh!

A blue laser burns clean through Helix's chest. Schorl appears at the opposite end of the hall, its shot precise and final. While you pulled every Underground sorcerer away from Helix's side, it delivered the killing strike.

Flawed Schorl: Target hit. Vital signs fading. Death in approximately two minutes.

Ritual Officiant: HELIX!! YOU FILTH... YOU DARE!!!

The fanatics encircling you finally grasp the deception. Schorl is buried under a barrage of sorcerous attacks, its motions grinding to a crawl. The unfolding scene seems to overload its systems, feeding it contradictions its logic cannot process.

Flawed Schorl: Forced directive... executed... but this is not... rational... calculations... difficult to override... Chief... zzzt... your safety... should be...

Schorl's signal flickers in and out. You know your other companions are still fighting somewhere beyond the chaos. Pain rakes through every wound, each breath a burning razor, and your limbs sink into coldness. Still, you force your eyes to stay open—

You need to see the rewind happen with your own eyes. All of it, every loss, every sacrifice, must be undone. There is no room for luck. If the "virtuoso" of the Cursed Melody will not help you, let her see what becomes of her own kin!

Ritual Officiant: No... she's our precious vessel... we can't let her...

Ritual Officiant: Virtuoso! Save her, our precious offering... save her!

Again and again, they trace the sacred symbol of the golden eye across their brows, chanting before the stone effigy with its wild tendrils, begging for the arrival of a Mania miracle. And then...

A soft, lilting melody breaks through the chaos and shrieks, finally arriving on the battlefield. It comes to guide a dying companion, to grant Helix one more chance.

But you can take that chance too.

Under the Cursed Melody, the two of you, both pierced and bleeding, meet each other's gaze across the hall. The world blurs through blood. Your senses begin to fade away. And together, your consciousnesses are drawn toward the same past.

Helix: It does not hurt... not at all...

Helix: The virtuoso will send me back... by then, the Vision will... kill you... next time... I will kill you...

Chief: Of course you'll be sent back.

**【Advance】**

- And I will be there with you.

**end**

When the shackles register the disappearance of Helix's consciousness, you finally allow your eyes to close.

Get ready, lunatics. This is only the beginning.

The path is laid out. You just have to walk it.

Next time, victory will belong to

the "blasphemous devil."

## N11-5-C Change

The drapes part without warning. The familiar scent of incense and the hush of music press in from all sides. Figures flicker and blur in the haze, voices overlapping in a low murmur that fills your ears. You are back at the start of the loop.

Ritual Participant: ...You mean Sillage's Sage of Death herself is actually going to show up tonight? Really? There are quite a few matters I'd like to have her "divine" for me.

You tear off the lace blindfold and shake off the lingering ache from the last loop, snapping into full control of your body. Your thoughts are clearer than ever. You remember exactly what must be done. There is no uncertainty left.

Helix, the fanatic who rode the rewind beside you, will identify you and try to kill you immediately. You have to move now! You turn and grab the familiar figure just beside you.

Glamorous Young Lady: Ahh! You're...

**【Choose One】**

- "I finally found you."

- "Your kind of prey."

**end**

You pull her with you and move fast through the maze of curtains. You know the layout now. You slip through narrow gaps, dodge behind drapes, lean in close to her ear like the other guests do, and murmur back the words she once spoke to you in a different loop.

Chief: Life is too short. Free yourself. I'll take you to the Divine.

Glamorous Young Lady: ...! Is this really your first time here? Daring and charming, just the way I love it... Ah!

The silver needle finds her skin. The neurotoxin spreads at once, and her body slackens in your arms. You ease her to the floor, hidden from view, and wrap her shawl around your shoulders before slipping away.

Only then does the noise from outside begin to swell.

<ins>Helix: There's a devil among us! With gray hair... he slipped in alone to scout, and there's a whole group coming! They're here to ruin the ritual and desecrate the divine effigy! And they have guns!!</ins><br><ins>Helix: There's a devil among us! With gray hair... she slipped in alone to scout, and there's a whole group coming! They're here to ruin the ritual and desecrate the divine effigy! And they have guns!!</ins>

<ins>Helix: I'm telling the truth! I saw it! They're almost here. We have to find him. We have to stop him, or—AAH!</ins><br><ins>Helix: I'm telling the truth! I saw it! They're almost here. We have to find her. We have to stop her, or—AAH!</ins>

The officiant's patience snaps. He shoves Helix forward with brutal force. She stumbles and slams against the base of the stone statue, her head ringing as quiet laughter ripples through the crowd around her.

Ritual Participant: The ritual hasn't even started. What kind of Vision could she have seen? She's just making excuses to run because she doesn't want to die.

Helix: No... I'm not lying... I swear, I swear to the Divine... wait!

She clutches at the officiant's robes.

Helix: Thirteen thorns! In the ritual, I'll be pierced by thirteen light thorns! I'll be purified... and then... and then there will be sacred music! The virtuoso will save me!

Helix: Yes! I lived through it! I saw it all! The enemy wields a power of red spikes, stolen from the Great Divine! A dangerous enemy is among us!

Ritual Officiant: ...Shackles...? You... saw the Vision?

Helix finally catches a sliver of hope. She rushes to explain, words spilling out before she can even catch her breath.

Helix: Yes. And I saw the danger and the enemies in the future. Please trust me! I'm not afraid to die! Everything I do is for the Divine...

Ritual Officiant: ...I believe in your courage and devotion. But, Helix, you must first tell me... what does this person look like?

Helix: What... what does that person look like?

She freezes.

Ritual Officiant: You are the only one among us who can see the Vision. Tell us what this devil looks like. Otherwise, where do we even begin to search?

<ins>Helix: He... I...</ins><br><ins>Helix: She... I...</ins>

Helix falters, her mouth opening and closing without a sound. She has always dismissed "other people" as beneath her notice, reserving every ounce of devotion for the great divine. But now, pressed for answers before a watching crowd, she finds herself utterly without words.

You move out of Helix's sight before she can call you out. She cannot find you, and her fellow devotees do not know your face. You hold the advantage now. Time is short, and much is to be done. You wait for the first person on your list to show up—

Eirene Campbell steps out of the hall and turns into the curtained corridor. You fall in behind her, silent, and press the silver needle against her lower back in a quiet corner. She stops cold.

Eirene: ...I'm glad you came to find me. Though I didn't imagine it would be like this.

Chief: Oh, you imagined plenty. Playing both sides, reaping the rewards from both ends. You came out here to find me through the shackles, didn't you?

Chief: How's the fence-sitting treating you, President?

Eirene: Just a reasonable hedge. A businesswoman's instinct—you'll have to forgive me. Besides, you know my heart leans toward your victory.

Chief: I will win. If you really mean that, then help. I know how much they trust you. How much they value you.

Eirene: Are you planning to use me as leverage?

You offer her a smile.

**【Advance】**

- That would be boring. You'd be disappointed.

**end**

She is not someone you can trust, and she walks a road that does not align with yours. But for now, at least, she does not bother hiding her desires, and that is something you can use.

Chief: I'm about to move. All you need to do is stay here and watch them. Be careful not to expose yourself. Unless—

You lean in and speak just above a breath. As the words sink in, a faint, amused smile takes shape on Eirene's lips.

Eirene: …Now that sounds like something worth looking forward to.

You step away from Eirene and navigate around the edgy guards, slipping into the service passage that is already etched in your mind. Guided by the blurry memory from the Vision, you slip into the kitchen for a bottle of liquor, then return to the main corridor and find the spot where the drapes hang thickest.

Eugene: I'll buy you time. Go... Chief...

Chief: ...You won't have to do that again. This time, I'll handle it.

**【Advance】**

- Use the liquor to set a fire

**end**

You crack the bottle open, splash the liquor across the curtains, and toss a lighted candle into the soaked fabric. Fire erupts on contact, surging upward in a rush of red, devouring the entire corridor in a blaze.

Chief: "Create a disturbance on the spot. Breach at the signal and link up as quickly as possible."

Chief: Come now. Let's do this together.

Flawed Schorl: Abnormal activity detected. A fire has broken out in the southeast corner of the venue. Smoke has already triggered the emergency alarms across multiple corridors. Based on internal personnel reactions, the probability of arson is high.

Eugene: A fire, right now... Could it be...

Robed Sorcerer B: Fire! Someone started a fire!!

Helix: It's the devil!!

## N11-6-C New Path

<ins>Flawed Schorl: ${PlayerName} has set a fire in the south corridor on the second floor. This is our signal to breach. 9th and I will search outward from that point. This is our signal to breach. 9th and I will search outward from that point. Her pace is fast; pinpointing her position will require time.</ins>

Flawed Schorl: Remember: avoid engagement and do not compromise your cover. The priority is reaching Chief. Everything else is secondary.

Footsteps approach from behind. Eugene wheels around, weapon raised, and stops short.

Eugene: Huh... Chief?!

Eugene stows his weapon at once. He takes in your soot-streaked face, finds you intact, and nearly pulls you into an embrace before stopping himself. He straightens and offers a crisp salute to his superior officer instead.

Eugene: Thank god you're okay! You have no idea how worried we were!! ...Wait. My team's supposed to be the decoy to draw their fire!

**end**

Chief: Is Wynn online? I need an urgent upload. Have the Ring Research Institute run an analysis on this immediately.

Your hand moves fast, sketching the symbol you committed to memory in the effigy chamber. You press it into Eugene's palm before the image begins to slip.

Chief: Now, this is our plan of attack—

Eugene takes it in, breath catching. His hand moves to his chest of its own accord.

Eugene: Chief... this is insane. The risk... if anything goes wrong...

**【Advance】**

- It'll be fine.

**end**

You know the possible failures better than he ever could, because you have lived through each one already.

A missed ambush. A plan exposed too early. Comrades falling at your feet, again and again...

You have lived through them all. And every time it reaches this point, your hand finds the silver needle and drives it home without a second thought.

Robed Sorcerer B: ...Hmph. Finally accepted your fate and decided to end it yourself?

Of course not. It's just a signal to a "partner in crime." You are here to win.

Helix: Nngh!

Eirene strikes without warning. Her power takes hold of Helix, creeping through her limbs and core. Helix trembles violently beneath her robes, her life fading with every second. The room is caught entirely off guard.

Ritual Officiant: HELIX!! YOU FILTH... YOU DARE!!!

Ritual Officiant: Virtuoso! Save her, our precious offering... save her!

Eirene's eyes stay fixed and icy. She lets the final exchange with ${PlayerName} replay in her thoughts.

Chief: You and I are bound by the shackles. Only death can break the tie. So if my consciousness slips from the brink, you'll know. And when it does, I need you to take care of one person. Keep her alive, just on the edge, until that music plays.

Chief: I'll show you what a true "Vision" looks like.

Eirene: As you said, when the shackles break, I'll trigger the fuse... But you can't expect me to invest in just a "story," especially one as vague as a "future" I can't even see...

Eirene: Next time, I want to see a real victory. Show me you can win.

No point sharing any of that with Eugene. You pat him on the shoulder and move on.

**【Advance】**

- Let's go. We will win this time.

**end**

The chaos spreads, and the Undergrounders begin to feel the weight of something closing in. They know they are being played, but cannot see by whom. The officiant's hand tightens around Helix's arm.

Ritual Officiant: Helix, come. We're leaving. You are the Vision seer, the offering chosen by the Divine. We cannot let them take you away or break you. Go to the virtuoso. We will keep you safe.

Helix retreats half a step, pressing her back against the tall stone statue with its tentacle-like wings. She stands as if guarding the sacred three rifts in her heart, shielding the majesty of the golden-eyed god.

<ins>Helix: No... I'm not afraid. Please let me stay. That devil is using those corrupted shackles to wreak havoc. He thinks he can just take the Divine's power and do as he pleases... But I see.</ins><br><ins>Helix: No... I'm not afraid. Please let me stay. That devil is using those corrupted shackles to wreak havoc. She thinks she can just take the Divine's power and do as she pleases... But I see.</ins>

<ins>Helix: Every time he blasphemes, I feel it. Every time he uses the power, I see where he is. This is the Vision, the Divine guiding me. If I stay, I can help you hunt the devil. I can fight.</ins><br><ins>Helix: Every time she blasphemes, I feel it. Every time she uses the power, I see where she is. This is the Vision, the Divine guiding me. If I stay, I can help you hunt the devil. I can fight.</ins>

Helix: Give me a chance to protect you all... I have something to give to the Divine, too. Please, let me stay!

Ritual Officiant: ...Very well. We are counting on you.

She takes no comfort in being shielded. Her pride lies in the knowledge that she was chosen, that she has a role to play. That pride only sharpens her hunger to give more.

<ins>Helix: I'll prove myself... Now! The shackles are active! He's in... the effigy chamber!</ins><br><ins>Helix: I'll prove myself... Now! The shackles are active! She's in... the effigy chamber!</ins>

Ritual Officiant: Go through the fissure, quickly!

A sorcerer follows the command and tears open a fissure, intending to cut through to the effigy chamber and strike down the target. Then, in that instant, everything veers off course—

Helix: Sir!!

A blue beam lances from the fissure and strikes the officiant's mask dead center. The mask dissolves under the heat, and the robed figure beneath erupts into black smoke, vanishing with a dull pop.

Then a small, sleek Schorl drifts out from the very fissure that was meant to serve the Underground.

Ritual Officiant: ...Impossible... how did you...

Flawed Schorl: Your overly frequent use of dimensional fissures has provided us with an abundance of data. The eigenvalues of the phase space have been fully analyzed and verified. This space can no longer offer you any cover.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, your plan is a success. Shall we regroup at the enemy's position?

You walk out of the effigy chamber at a measured pace. On the other end of the communicator, despite the speaker being off, a girl's shrill voice cuts through loud and clear.

EDGE-07: Are you out of your mind?! A handful of incomplete samples, a sorcery sigil that looks like chicken scratch, and you wanted it deciphered in half an hour?! Who exactly did you think can crack a Mania dimensional fissure with that?!

EDGE-07: An outrageous request like that! Who else could possibly pull it off besides yours truly, the Observation Hub's leading academic genius, the one and only Lemma Herzfeld?! I already cracked it! Now where's my thank-you?!

**【Advance】**

- Okay, okay, you're the best, little genius.

**end**

EDGE-07: I don't need your empty flattery!! Just win, you hear me?! I'm dead tired over here! EDGE-07 orders you: destroy those Sillage Society bastards and bring back all the raw data to me!!

You respond with a smile, then stride into the main hall for the final confrontation. This time, you hold every edge: the first strike, the layout in your mind, your allies all in position, and their fissures rendered useless.

After being hunted through loop after loop, the roles are finally reversed. The Underground is the one cornered now.

Chief: You're not the only one who's seen the Vision. So, what now? Ready for what's coming?

Chief: I've waited a long time for this.

You open your eyes. The oppressive strangeness that had filled the hall is gone. Whatever anger or bitterness you carried has been forged into a fire of pure defiance. You will not be swallowed by this place's hollow delirium anymore. You are the one who sets the flame, and it will spread far beyond these walls.

Thin Female Voice: ...This melody is...

The virtuoso. If you do not force her out, if you do not cut her off, every move you make will be read and used against you before long.

Chief: I know she's in the fissure. This absurd farce ends now.

Chief: ...Tell her to put down the bow and show herself!

The fleeing sorcerers are pushed deep into the fissure, breaking into scattered remnants to evade capture. Yet every route they take, every turn they try, the hunters remain locked on their trail.

Whispering Puppet: *Did they steal the Vision? They foresee our moves? Otherwise, this doesn't make sense... The Mania residue we left behind is too thick. They'll figure out the fissure sorcery soon... We can't win... We must retreat...*

Helix: *...Retreat? Are you telling me to flee? ...To shame the Divine before our enemies?*

Whispering Puppet: *The virtuoso's safety is above all... The Sage entrusted her to us... Said it again and again... She cannot be taken. Someone must stay and hold them back... Ah, that one will do...*

Helix: *Then... I'll protect her! I'll go with you! I'll be the one to hold them off!*

Whispering Puppet: *...She's already been corrupted by the shackles. If she stays with us, we'll never escape their sight...*

Whispering Puppet: *A shame to lose the offering... But as long as the virtuoso lives... the Cursed Melody... will find another vessel...*

The escorts stop mid-stride. They turn as one toward Helix. The atmosphere curdles. She tries to pull back, but a hand locks around her arm.

The officiant lowers himself slowly to her level, his eyes fixed on hers with earnest intensity.

Ritual Officiant: I'm sorry, Helix. This is as far as you go.

Helix: What?

Ritual Officiant: You are a Sinner... The Divine has chosen you to see the Vision. Only you can stop the devil.

A shiver passes through her. These are the words she has waited her whole life to hear. So why...

Helix: But I... I want to be with all of you... I want to protect you...

Ritual Officiant: The shackles connect both ways. You can sense the devil, and the devil can sense you. If you stay with us, you'll only lead the enemy to our virtuoso.

Ritual Officiant: You are not alone. The Divine will never leave you. And through the Divine, neither will we.

The officiant lifts his golden-eye talisman from his own neck and places it over hers. His finger draws the three sacred rifts on her forehead. Then he gathers her into an embrace, holding her firmly as she trembles, as if he has no doubt the fear will pass.

He knows Helix will convince herself soon enough.

Helix: Ha... haha... You're right... I understand.

Helix: For the Divine... for the Divine...

For the Divine, she is left behind. The sacrifice she always craved is finally hers.

The abhorrent, blasphemous devil approaches her, and she is on her own now. For the first time, Helix feels real fear. Her body starts shaking. Her world fractures, and the figure before her dissolves into a smear of sound and light.

"Devil": ...Why her? ...Why is she the only one left?

Trembling, she lifts the pitiful weapon in her grip and calls out, knowing it will not save her.

Helix: ...Stay back! Don't come any closer! I won't let you pass. I won't let you hurt them... Kill me if you want! I'm not scared!

Evil Black Triangle: She's buying them time, Chief. The virtuoso is retreating. We need to cut them off before they vanish. That's our real target. Deal with the obstacle quickly, or I'll handle it.

"Devil": ...Get out of my way, Helix. I'm not here for you, and I don't want to kill you. If you die now, it's all wasted.

Helix: No! This is my mission! The Divine gave me this mission!

Helix: I live for this very moment! For this one great act of sacrifice!

**【Advance】**

- They DUMPED you here!! You fool!

**end**

You seize her and clamp your hands firmly over her shoulders.

Chief: They left you here to die. They used you as a scapegoat. And you're fine with that?!

Chief: When you were offering yourself up, did they ever treat you as one of them? Would your so-called god really smile on this?!

Helix shakes violently, her voice barely holding. She has never been afraid of death, never flinched at pain. But now her eyes burn, and she cannot hold the tears back.

Of all people, she is about to break down in front of a devil!!

Helix: ... Shut up... I don't feel the pain... I'm not scared...

Helix: The Divine is watching. The Divine sees everything. It will welcome me after I die...

Chief: Your god doesn't even—

Chief: !!

A sharp bow stroke slices through the exchange. A bolt of flame follows, splitting the space between you and driving you apart.

Helix: This is...

Flawed Schorl: It's the Mania spectrum of the Cursed Melody!

Helix: Virtuoso...?

For a fleeting moment, light returns to Helix's eyes. Her face twists from a burst of pure joy into an unnamed fear.

As if catching a voice from nowhere, she lifts her head and speaks into the emptiness.

Helix: ...You're hurt...? No, you shouldn't have come... It's not safe here. Go back!

Helix: This is my mission. I'm supposed to protect you—

Whispers in the Strings: ■ ■ ■ ■ —

Helix: But...!

She hesitates for a long, fraught moment, but eventually, she yields.

It does not take you long to know whom she has been speaking to.

A melodic strain rises from the strings, sharper and closer than ever before, as though the musician is just out of sight. You freeze for a moment.

In the music... something else is there.

Flawed Schorl: ...Chief?

You listen for a long, quiet moment. Then you speak, your voice low.

Chief: ...This Sinner named Helix won't get in our way anymore. We don't need to hurt her. Let her go. Someone traded herself for Helix's freedom.

Chief: The virtuoso will stop running. She's just ahead.

Inside the fissure, another layer of space peels open, deeper than the last. The music rises from within, so clear it seems close enough to touch.

You know this presence... You have been to that place before, in a different turn of time.

Chief: ...I'll go find her.

Eirene: ...Well played, ${PlayerName}. A fine match. Very satisfying.

Eirene: Now then, what should my next move be...?

Voice from Behind: To come back and toast the victory with us, of course.

A gun barrel presses cold against the back of Eirene's head.

9th: I hear you played a significant role in this operation—a masterstroke of planning. Chief ${PlayerName} insisted that we not let you walk away just yet. Chief would like to invite you back to the Bureau for a proper chat.

9th: Shall we, President? This way.

Behind him, Nightingale and Wynn arrive with another squad of Sinners in tow. Hecate's Nightmare coils in its dragon form, looming behind Eirene. Every figure is poised for battle.

So she is not just a "partner," Eirene realizes. The Chief must have glimpsed more of the board than she expected. A moment of reflection, then a quiet smile touches her lips.

Eirene: ...This path has its merits too.

05:23 AM

The joint raid against the Sillage Society, discreetly led by the MBCC Chief and executed with multi-department coordination, draws to a close by daybreak. The operation spans nearly three hours and is punctuated by numerous unforeseen developments.

News: Last night, a fire broke out at a private club in Eastside. The fire department responded promptly upon receiving the report, and the blaze was brought under control by approximately 4:00 AM. No serious casualties were reported.

News: According to sources, the club had already ceased operations pending internal restructuring. The fire is believed to have been caused by inadequate facility maintenance. Management has since announced that during the suspension, they will carry out a full review of fire safety systems, enhance staff training, and take all necessary measures to prevent a recurrence.

The success, however, is unprecedented. All post-operation grievances and formal complaints are rejected in light of solid evidence. A number are even flagged as possible retaliatory fabrications by Sillage Society affiliates, and investigations into those claims are already underway.

Radio Host: But eyewitnesses say they saw monsters! And there was electronic jamming, special forces on the ground! Earlier, dozens of luxury cars were seen rolling in and out of the estate, and guess who was there? ****, ****, and ***! No official word? Not even a comment?!

Radio Host: Hah, then we'll dig it up ourselves! The truth can't be buried forever. To all our passionate, justice-loving listeners, make sure to subscribe and stay tuned! This host won't rest until we get to the bottom of it!

The truth beneath the organization's spectacle is laid bare. A world of self-indulgent elites on one hand, and plotting roaches in the shadows on the other. Allies only in convenience, never in conviction.

Once that truth takes hold, the enemy loses its fear. Cracks begin to show. And you know now how to widen them.

Glamorous Young Lady: I was invited by a friend. It's just a regular private party. I have no idea what's going on.

Glamorous Young Lady: P-put the camera down!

And more than anything—

You do not have to sacrifice anyone. No one is lost.

Wynn: Geez, you guys scared me to death! I warned you, the horoscope is looking really bad today! You had to be super, super careful! And now you're all banged up! I swear, over the comms I thought you'd been turned into a fireball, several times!

Eugene: Ow—ouch! Sorry, sorry! My bad! I'll be more careful next time, I promise! But... what was I supposed to do, just let Chief take the hit?

Wynn: You idiot! Big, big idiot!

...And you return with an unexpected "catch."

Helix: I'm not going anywhere! Where's the virtuoso? Let me see her!

Helix: ... What have you done to her?!

And in every sense that matters, you have struck a true and lasting wound against the enemy lurking in the dark.

The threat of the Mania dimensional fissures has been shattered for good. Under the cover of night, a handful of devotees creep back to the site, desperate for any clue about their captured companion.

They find nothing but a scorched bowl in the earth, left by their enemy's direct bombardment. Patches of blood. Fragments of a broken violin. Ruined remains. It is a scene of brutal, unambiguous murder.

Ritual Officiant: This blood... it's hers... They killed the virtuoso? Why... That girl's just an offering... she's nothing comparable! Why would you go back for her, Virtuoso...

Ritual Officiant: ...Is this my fault? How will the Sage judge me?

Voice from Behind: Indeed... You'd better think carefully about how to explain it to the Sage.

He whips around and catches sight of the figure that sends a chill through him.

Ritual Officiant: No... it's not what you think! I tried to stop her... we could have retreated! But the virtuoso... she stopped us with her power. You know how formidable she is! We couldn't hold her back—

Ritual Officiant: No... please, I beg you... AAAH—!

This marks the most significant breakthrough since the Cursed Melody first spread. Without their key "virtuoso," the Underground will find it difficult to mount any further threat. The danger of the Cursed Melody can finally be laid to rest. At least, that is what everyone believed on that day.

Hecate has been waiting at the side. Nightingale nudges her lightly, and she walks over to greet you.

Hecate: Welcome back, Chief.

**【Advance】**

- ...I'm back.

**end**

You offer her a smile, but she reads something hidden behind it.

Hecate: ...Did something happen?

EDGE-02: According to the action report, our dear little Chief suspects time travel is involved. Specifically, the so-called "Vision" is essentially a person living through events and then being sent back to a specific point in time, anchored by the Cursed Melody.

<ins>EDGE-02: He's quite shaken up, almost tying himself in knots. He's been grilling 07 on the grandfather paradox and parallel universes, and he's especially fixated on the certainty of his companions' deaths that he witnessed in the "Vision"... Heh, adorable, really.</ins><br><ins>EDGE-02: She's quite shaken up, almost tying herself in knots. She's been grilling 07 on the grandfather paradox and parallel universes, and she's especially fixated on the certainty of her companions' deaths that she witnessed in the "Vision"... Heh, adorable, really.</ins>

EDGE-02: What do you think? Mania's influence on the temporal dimension was your most dedicated field of research. Now it's finally moved beyond theory; there's a genuine "time ability" manifesting... Don't you want to see it for yourself?

EDGE-01: If I had the time, I would. Unfortunately, there are higher-priority tasks at hand. As for that old research, it was already settled long ago.

EDGE-01: Within the foreseeable level of civilizational development, time is not a concept within humanity's grasp. Minor disruptions—individual lives—will eventually return to equilibrium. And any oscillation exceeding the threshold will be met with punishment from true fate itself.

EDGE-01: I imagine our Underground friends understand this as well. Besides, "time travel" is merely ${PlayerName}'s personal perception. At present, we have no way to prove that the temporal dimension has actually been distorted.

EDGE-01: And any phenomenon rooted in personal perception can ultimately be explained on the level of consciousness. Especially when the Cursed Melody, Mania, and the Underground are inherently tied to it.

EDGE-01: That is your area of expertise, isn't it?

EDGE-02: ......

EDGE-02: ...Exactly right.

<ins>EDGE-02: We've captured a high-ranking Underground operative for the first time, and with an incredibly rare Mania power... Your little shepherd did exceptionally well this time. He's brought us an invaluable specimen.</ins><br><ins>EDGE-02: We've captured a high-ranking Underground operative for the first time, and with an incredibly rare Mania power... Your little shepherd did exceptionally well this time. She's brought us an invaluable specimen.</ins>

EDGE-02: ...I'll need to study her up close.

The victory is yours, immense and undeniable. No one has yet realized what it truly represents.

And fewer still understand how far its ripples will reach.

August 4, N.F. 116

03:23 AM

Thin Female Voice: You kept your word and let Helix go... Thank you, for hearing me out.

Thin Female Voice: We finally meet. Or perhaps...

Thin Female Voice: This isn't the first time?

Thin Female Voice: You really do resemble that person... the one I've been waiting for, for so long.

Thin Female Voice: It's a pleasure to meet you.

The "virtuoso" of the Underground, S-Class Sinner Requiem, has been secretly detained.

Eirene: You won. And you won beautifully. So, as a bonus, let me tell you something worthwhile.

Eirene: She's a rare prize. If you really want to take her with you... make sure no one finds out you left her alive.

## N11-8 "Virtuoso"

Schorl-II: Hello, Chief. It's not often we get an enemy of this level in our hands. You've handled this well. Push just a little further, and we'll hit a real breakthrough. EDGE has sent me in to help. My role is mostly on the research and analysis side.

Schorl-II: But the first and most critical step, the interrogation, still needs to be driven by you. Go ahead, expert. S-125's condition has stabilized and she's ready to talk. I have a feeling she'll be very eager to speak with you.

August 4, N.F. 116

Underground, Undisclosed Location, Outside MBCC

Special Mania Containment Facility

Inside the special containment cell, the virtuoso from the Underground, Sinner S-125, has been expecting your arrival for some time now.

Requiem: ...Hello. My name is Requiem.

Requiem: My age... I no longer remember. Where I come from... I couldn't say. I suppose I'm a violinist.

Requiem: You're the third person who's come to question me. What should I call you? "Chief"? Or... may I know your real name?

Requiem: Is that allowed to be asked?

She offers you a gentle smile. In response, your nerves tighten, and the vast mass of information you have just collected comes crashing through your mind all at once.

Nightingale: For security purposes, the three Underground-affiliated Sinners connected to this case have been moved to an off-site special containment facility. It's discreet and secure. Meanwhile, we've spread word of the virtuoso's death, and the Underground appears to have bought it.

Nightingale: As for S-125, she's been surprisingly cooperative. Hardly any pushback during the interrogation. I won't dismiss the possibility that she's feigning compliance, but I'm inclined to think she genuinely doesn't yet realize the full weight of her situation.

Requiem: ${PlayerName}... Not what I expected, perhaps. But it's a lovely name.

Requiem: Oh, the girl who was with me, Helix... She insisted on coming with you too, didn't she? Is she doing well?

Helix: ...I have nothing to say to you.

Helix: Imprisoning a divine envoy in this vile place, conducting your abhorrent experiments, binding her with those wretched shackles of yours... You and your people are absolutely monsters!

Helix: Where is the virtuoso? Where is she? She's different from me; she's precious. If you dare harm her, I will make you pay!

Requiem: That girl is fraught with tension and unease. Left as she is, she will only harm herself in time. If you would, please keep an eye on her.

Requiem: Just be as kind to her as you've been to me. Helix may seem strong-willed, but she needs that kindness more than I ever did.

9th: Given how desperately the Undergrounders tried to keep her out of our hands, this woman is the nearest we've ever gotten to the core of their organization. Her intel potential is immense.

9th: Chief, you're an expert in interrogating Sinners, the finest when it comes to discerning lies and uncovering the truth. I have every confidence that you'll draw out vital intelligence that we've never been able to obtain from her.

The Sinner sitting before you defies every expectation you have of an Undergrounder. She appears quiet, composed, with none of the volatility you have come to associate with her kind. Her complexion is disturbingly pale, as if her body has been emptied of blood and that very life force now thrums through the red strings she wears.

The containment cell, originally built to restrain Mania power wielders from the Underground, now feels more like an isolation ward keeping the world out, because even a breath of outside air might shatter her.

And yet this is the one behind the Cursed Melody. The irony does not escape you. You steady your nerves, fix your gaze on hers, and ask.

**【Advance】**

- There are some things I'd like to know.

**end**

Chief: If I ask, would you tell me the truth?

Chief: "Just some basic questions. I'd like to know you a little better."

"Requiem" meets your eyes. You have the distinct sensation of facing a sheet of glass, perfectly clear, yet utterly empty of anything beneath the surface.

Requiem: If I tell you the truth, will you return my violin to me?

Requiem: It matters to me more than you know.

A soft light pulses from the tablet in your hand. You keep the screen carefully turned away from her sight, and a single line of text materializes without a whisper.

[Terminal Notification]

*Agree to it.*

Your eyes drift upward to the ceiling's corners, where a network of micro-cameras captures every gesture from every angle. Requiem's hearing is far too acute for earpieces to be safe, so you and your colleagues decided to trade messages through the tablet's screen instead. Caution is not a choice; it is a necessity.

**【Advance】**

- "You can have your violin back."

**end**

Requiem: Thank you. Then please, ask away.

The interrogation presses on. Requiem remains composed as she explains why she took part in the Sillage Society's clandestine gathering, and provides a broad sketch of what the ritual entailed.

She claims she was asked by a friend and brought to a location to perform. And her friend, likely an Undergrounder, was also invited by people Requiem did not know to the ritual. Those were presumably the "distinguished guests" who sought a glimpse of the future.

More notably, Requiem points out that the Underground rarely grants ritual requests from outsiders. Recently, however, they appear to have encountered difficulties and have been compelled to offer the Vision in exchange for external support.

This suggests that your recent pursuit operations have placed considerable strain on the Underground, driving them to contact DisCity's upper echelons through the Sillage Society in hopes of securing greater protection.

She further notes that the Underground refuses to acknowledge any reliance on outsiders, even on the Sillage Society members. They have thus reframed the ritual as a search for those who can resonate with the Cursed Melody and perceive the Vision of the future. They label such people Listeners, and a case like Helix is exactly the rare find they are after.

But to you, these "Listeners" resemble tools more than talents. "Vessels" and "offerings," in the Underground's own terms.

As for Requiem's own standing within the Underground...

Requiem: I am a virtuoso. There are so many melodies drifting through my mind. I've been told they are the rhythm of the whole world, a great suite that has been lost to time.

Requiem: I want to capture them, recover the forgotten suite, and add a new movement of my own. That is what my friends need more than anything.

Requiem: But it's not so simple. I am limited. My mind wanders, and too often everything fades to black, and the inspiration eludes me. Most of the time, I can only stand by and let them protect me. They have been so patient... but still, after all this time, I have nothing to show for it.

Requiem: Perhaps staying in a place of safety for too long closes your eyes to the world... It's quite troubling.

She lets out a soft laugh. Light and candid, her words fall without hesitation, but they leave you frowning all the same.

Chief: So you agreed to come with me also because you were hoping to find inspiration?

**【Advance】**

- What kind of music are you looking for?

**end**

Requiem: The music I'm looking for...

She considers the question.

Requiem: The kind that can touch souls.

*...Touch souls? The new Schorl said something similar.*

Requiem: They say music touches the soul in ways words cannot. I want those who come to hear me play—even if they've never walked the same path—to still feel what the music carries. That even strangers, gathered in the same hall, can share in the same joy and sorrow when a melody is played.

Schorl-II: Music is an ideal medium for emotional manipulation. This has been observed at the ritual site and in the Rust mass-casualty event. A melody can synchronize the emotions of everyone within its range, with the feeling it carries seeping into its audience. It also serves as a powerful Mania catalyst.

Requiem: ...I also hope it can preserve memories. That the moments that matter can be written into the music, so that even if they gather dust over the years, they won't be worn away. No matter how far we stray, no matter how we change, the music will always carry us back to those moments.

Schorl-II: The experience of "time travel and rewind" is likely the same principle of resonance at work. For example, hearing an old song can bring back past emotions and memories, as though consciousness has crossed time and is reaching into the present from the past.

Schorl-II: The Vision granted by the Cursed Melody, however, works in the opposite direction. If in the "future," you hear this same song, then the you in the present, as a resonator, may catch glimpses of the memories and information carried in that future moment. It's as if your consciousness can cross time both ways: the present you can peer into the future, and the future you can reach back to the past.

Schorl-II: The exact mechanism, range, depth, and trigger conditions all remain to be confirmed. But there is no question that this power is extraordinarily useful for evading pursuit. It is precisely through their inventive use of the Cursed Melody that our old friends in the Underground have remained hidden for so long.

Requiem: ...It doesn't sound like anything special when you say it that way. It's simply what music can do.

Requiem: Still, if I want to touch souls with my music, I will have to see them first. Hiding behind others won't take me there, so...

She lifts her head and meets your eyes, though it feels as if she is looking straight through you, past you, into everything that lies beyond. She takes it all in, openly and without reserve. After a long moment, when she has seen enough, a soft, bright smile spreads across her face.

Requiem: Thank you for bringing me here. I feel like I've taken one step closer.

You pause for a moment, then ask.

Chief: ...So this music you're looking for... is it really what you want? Or is it what your "friends" want?

Chief: Can they really be called your "friends"?

Requiem: Or musical companions. That's what the virtuosos prefer. Musical companions are precious to us.

Chief: Then where are these precious musical companions of yours right now? And where are they planning to hold their next ritual, to stir up more chaos?

Chief: You've been helping them all along, haven't you? Using the "music" you cherish to help them slip away from pursuit and punishment. And spreading that music... it wasn't just for that, was it?

A pause settles between you before Requiem answers. When she finally speaks, her voice carries a subtle difference.

Requiem: These questions feel very different from the ones before...

Requiem: I can answer them, of course, and I keep my word. I won't lie. But since the stakes have risen, I'd like to make an extra request.

Her eyes curve a little more, and her smile grows deeper.

Requiem: Let's trade questions, Chief ${PlayerName}. You want to know who I am. I want to know who you are, too.

Chief: ......

**【Advance】**

- ...What do you want to know?

**end**

Requiem: First question... why are you asking me all of this?

Requiem: Why are you after my friends? If I give you the answers, are you going to find them? And when you do, what then?

Requiem: Chief, I've been honest with you. Now I'm asking you to be just as honest with me.

Chief: ......

The brief silence appears to serve as an answer in itself. She gives you a slight nod and withdraws.

Requiem: Forgive me, that was rather a lot. Answering so many questions in one go does wear on you. Shall we take a break?

Is she offering you an escape?

Chief: These questions are not really about getting to know me, are they? I think you asked because you want to find justifications for them, for those Undergrounders.

Chief: Why? Because you are on the same side? Because they've always been the ones protecting you?

Requiem: Because I am a virtuoso. And they are the gifts that music brought into my life.

Requiem: They gave me so much. All they ever asked of me was to play a single piece.

She lets out a breath so faint you almost miss it. She offers no further answers, nor does she turn the questions back on you, as she just suggested. The heavy silence that settles between you signals the conversation's quiet end.

You are about to press further, when—

[Terminal Notification]

*We're ready. Proceed.*

You glance at your terminal. The display reads 15:30. You draw a deep breath, tuck the device away, and turn your attention back to Requiem.

Chief: You're right. Let's take a break. May I ask something of you?

Requiem: Please do.

**【Advance】**

- "I'd like to hear you play."

**end**

Chief: You came here for inspiration too, didn't you? I see you've been observing. So, any thoughts so far? As a virtuoso from the Underground, now that you've seen this side of the world, what kind of melody would you write?

She pauses, as if caught off guard by your question.

Chief: If it's too soon for something new, you can always play the old piece. Either way, I wouldn't mind. It'd be nice to hear it again.

Requiem: ...You're willing to let me play?

Chief: Only here, and we've taken precautions. Besides...

**【Advance】**

- "That's something I already agreed to at the beginning."

**end**

Requiem: Thank you... I truly didn't expect...

Requiem: Your people have been so tense the whole time. I thought you all despised my music.

**【Advance】**

- "No. I like it."

**end**

Chief: "In return for your honesty with me, I want you to feel at ease here. Because..."

**【Advance】**

- "All Sinners are my friends."

**end**

Requiem: ...Is that so?

She tilts her head, studying you for a moment, and finally a smile surfaces.

Requiem: That is the loveliest thing anyone has said to me since I came here.

Requiem: Still, thank you. I've missed it so much.

The violin is brought into the room. Requiem's gaze finally drifts from you and lands on the instrument. She accepts it with care, fingers tracing its body gently. It appears unchanged since they were last together, and she is quietly thankful for that.

Then she lifts the bow and draws it across the strings—

So close to the performance, you miss nothing. Every motion of the virtuoso is visible: the calluses on her fingertips, the faint, rapid tremble of the strings under pressure. Sound spills from the instrument, swollen and sustained by the resonance of the violin's body, and echoes through the sealed room long after each note fades.

It is different from a recording. Here, you hear it raw and unfiltered. The energy is unmistakable, Mania curling through the melody, grazing your nerves with each phrase. And yet, equally unmistakable, is its beauty.

Requiem: I want those who come to hear me play—even if they've never walked the same path—to still feel what the music carries.

The violinist is utterly absorbed in the music, melting into the melody with an openness that leaves her completely unguarded before you. For a moment, it nearly makes you forget that you ever saw her as an enemy, as a threat.

Requiem: I also hope it can preserve memories. That the moments that matter can be written into the music, so that even if they gather dust over the years, they won't be worn away.

It is not only the frenzy of last night's fight that comes back to you. There is something else, older and more elusive, surfacing from the depths of your memory.

Requiem: ...It's simply what music can do.

...Listening to that melody, you almost let yourself believe.

The "rhythm of the world," is it? No one could resist such a performance, and even you find your emotions drifting, quietly tugged along by the current of the music.

The last note rings, holds, and falls away. The virtuoso lowers the bow.

Requiem: ...Thank you. I...

Requiem: ...!

Her words are cut short as the door flies open. HUSH elite units, untouched by the melody, rush past you. The violin is torn from her hands, and Requiem is forced back into her seat before she can offer the slightest resistance.

And then the pristine new Schorl glides into view.

Schorl-II: The experiment is a success. All parameters of the Cursed Melody have been recorded. Data is being retrieved. Ensure complete isolation during transfer; no unprotected exposure to these frequencies is permitted. S-125's ability is highly volatile. Heightened containment measures are necessary until the data is decoded.

Schorl-II: Excellent work, Chief. Time to go. The interrogation is over.

Before you can answer, a faint tug at your clothes stops you. It is Requiem. Stripped of her instrument, she appears to have lost her voice as well.

Requiem: Um... my violin...

**【Choose One】**

- Pacify

  Chief: I'll try to get it back for you.

  She holds your gaze, that same unwavering look she has given you during the interrogation. Her lips part, then close, the words never arriving.

  Requiem: ...I see.

  Requiem: You... don't have to go out of your way...
