  Chief: "The Cursed Melody takes too great a toll on you. We're researching ways to mitigate its side effects. Once we've made progress, I'll give your violin back."

  She holds your gaze, that same unwavering look she has given you during the interrogation. Her lips part, then close, the words never arriving.

  Requiem: ...I see. It's alright. I understand.

- Act tough

  She is a dangerous Undergrounder, the origin of the Cursed Melody of Death. That is the inescapable truth, and caution must take precedence over everything else.

  Chief: ...Answer my questions, and I'll consider returning it.

  She holds your gaze, that same unwavering look she has given you during the interrogation. At last, her fingers release your hem.

  Requiem: ...You won't. Even if I told you everything, you still wouldn't give it back. I know.

**end**

The virtuoso falls silent. The HUSH units lift her to her feet, and she follows them out without struggle. The long interrogation has finally reached its end.

Schorl-II: Requiem's psychological profile is being analyzed as we speak. Cross-referenced with the data just captured on-site, we should be able to decode her ability's mechanics far sooner. The Underground has lost one of its pillars. Our operations will see substantial gains.

Schorl-II: She also shared some rather intriguing things with you. They're well worth revisiting. As expected of the MBCC Chief. Your interrogation methods are truly sophisticated. You have a gift for earning Sinners' trust.

Schorl-II: Perhaps, in time, she might even turn her back on the Underground and join our side for you.

Chief: ...I don't think it was because of me...

Requiem: I was mistaken. Forgive me...

Requiem: You really do resemble that person... the one I've been waiting for, for so long.

Schorl-II: Oh? Then what was it?

**【Advance】**

- Forget it.

**end**

Requiem does not seem ill at ease here. She simply watches and listens, without a hint of malice, as if she only wishes to absorb everything down to the smallest detail. In any other Sinner, or any ordinary person, this would be a reassuring quality.

But she is, after all, the Underground's virtuoso.

When it comes to dealing with the Underground, you still prefer Helix's approach. Plain, unvarnished hatred is oddly easier to stomach.

Beep beep. A new transmission cuts in, and it sweeps the lingering shadows from your mind. That's from your dependable new ally.

Lemma: Your order's here. Pulled an all-nighter for this. It's valuable, so don't break it. You can't afford it.

Chief: Thanks a million. Seriously, you're a lifesaver.

Lemma: ...I can only do so much to preserve the spectrum's energy with what I've got... Consciousness stuff is out of my wheelhouse. For that, we'll have to wait on that one's report.

Lemma: But I can still back you up in the field like last time. Those Underground scumbags are still using the dimensional fissures, right?

Lemma: The leak's been patched up for good. Hand me enough samples, and I'll smoke every single one of them out for you.

There is a weariness in the genius scientist's voice that goes beyond the usual research fatigue. But you also know she would never accept words of solace, so you hold them back.

The new Schorl was right. You have to push one more step forward before hitting a decisive breakthrough.

Eirene: That virtuoso means far more to the Underground than I ever could. Hold your head high, Chief. Fate has smiled upon you and delivered a triumph to your doorstep.

Eirene: The question now is whether you can seize that advantage. I'll be keeping a close eye.

There are things that only you can do. And you will see them through.

06:24 PM, August 4

Office

Flawed Schorl: The case files of the Cursed Melody's new victims just came in from the Public Security Bureau. Three of them fit your screening criteria, Chief. If we move fast, we can still beat the clock.

## N11-9-A Special Power

Night falls over Eastside. Dim streetlight reaches only as far as the alley's mouth; deeper in, the glow is cut short by stacked shadows of surrounding buildings. In the darkness, only two things persist: the low hum of air-conditioning units and the frantic patter of running footsteps.

Two figures lurch into the narrow passage. One of them stumbles, nearly sending the laptop in their arms flying.

Cursed Melody Dealer A: How the hell did they find us... They're still chasing?! Ugh, this is the worst! Why the **** is this happening to me?!

Muttering curses, the dealer accelerates toward the agreed meeting place. It is the only route left in his panicked mind.

His shoes crash through puddles, sending water splashing. Each breath comes in harsh, scraping bursts, and his throat sears as if he has drawn in fire instead of air. Turn. Turn again. He's almost there!

The two dart out of the alley and into an empty clearing. The man spins on his heel. The footsteps behind him, the ones that had hounded every step, have gone silent. Have they lost them? Then...

Cursed Melody Dealer A: Ahhh!!

In the shadows just behind them, someone sits perched on a metal crate. Clearly, the person has been waiting there for a while.

**【Advance】**

- Relax. I've just got a few questions.

**end**

Chief: Play along and this'll go easier. The more honest you are, the lighter this gets.

Chief: Let's start with the Cursed Melody you've been sharing online. This is a new version, a first leak, and the Mania attached to it is even stronger.

Chief: Where did you get it? Who's above you? Did they give you any specific instructions when they handed it over? Just give me your supply chain, and we're done. At the end of the day, someone paid you to push their product. Nothing more.

Chief: Come clean now and I can mark it as unwitting involvement. Negligence, not conspiracy. Whatever other shady business you've been running, I'll let it slide.

Cursed Melody Dealer A: ...How could you possibly know? I haven't even—

Chief: I know you haven't. An hour ago, you contacted three buyers. Two private accounts looking to be the first to try it. You scheduled an automated transaction via custom software, set to go off in thirty minutes. Then there's a dark web admin who wants to buy your source file outright, but you're still hesitating on that one.

Chief: Oh, and you even drafted a sales pitch. "Elegant murder? Undetectable?"

With every word you speak, the dealer's face pales further. Impossible. How did you find out?! He had rotated through countless proxy accounts and masked his IP. He was certain he'd buried every trace.

Cursed Melody Dealer A: You... you've been watching me?! Th-this isn't on me! Nobody's dead yet, nothing's happened! I don't know anything... I don't know anything! I just take the cash and do the job!

The dealer pulls out a terminal and hurls it forward. He stumbles back, hands clamped over his ears. It hits you the moment you see it, and you brace yourself.

Cursed Melody Dealer A: I brought the one here! Your problem now!

Chief: You idiot! Don't—

A piercing, distorted wail of strings rips the night apart. The Cursed Melody!

Underground Puppet: *██████—!!*

Discarded by the rubbish, a handful of battered effigies tremble awake as the tune reaches them, unleashing a shrill, grating cry. Grime and fissures cover their forms, and from within, a dark, malignant matter bleeds out, taking shape as claws and blades.

Every pair of crimson eyes ignites in unison, locking onto the prey drawn here. A cheap, crude trap.

Whoosh! An Underground Puppet's claws rake toward your face. You snap back just in time, and in the same breath, a weight lifts from your pocket. A flash of cold metal arcs through the air, and then—

A searing laser blast ignites the entire clearing, reducing the lead Puppets to ash in an instant. Enraged, the remaining creatures hurl themselves forward with renewed savagery. You count the beats in your head, step back, and wait.

Boom! Another laser beam cuts through the air, striking the exact spot where you stood moments ago, and erasing another group of enemies. You glance up. Schorl hovers just ahead of you, angled and ready.

Flawed Schorl: Using yourself as bait is still far too reckless, Chief. I would advise deploying more HUSH units next time.

Chief: They're too loud. Would've scared our prey off. At best, they'd save me a bit of legwork, and that's about it. Besides, I only need one escort when that escort is you.

Schorl pauses, rotating a few degrees, and finally abandons its lecture. It drifts forward to hover directly before you, carrying itself with an unexpected gravity.

Flawed Schorl: Then, please give me your command.

Chief: Keep the dealer breathing. The rest...

**【Advance】**

- Take them all out.

**end**

Schorl holds the open ground, holding off wave after wave of Puppets with ruthless efficiency. As the fight winds down, you pivot toward the two poor dealers who were caught alongside you in this trap.

Infected Dealer: Uh... haha... knew it... knew you'd bite! Wanna chase them? Huh? Go on then! Go, GO!

Infected Dealer: You can't catch them!!Hahahaha, you morons! They're gone! All gone! Now it's just you!!

Chief: ......

The dealer is already lost to Mania, raving in delirium, a hollow tool discarded without a second thought. His every move was a misstep, a trail of breadcrumbs left deliberately by the Underground to pull you into their trap. They want payback for yesterday, and they are not subtle about it.

That suits you just fine.

Infected Dealer: It's a trap! A trap! Hahahaha! They're way smarter than you! Catch them? Dream on, dream on!!

Chief: Don't worry. They won't get away. I'm going after them now.

Infected Dealer: Going after? No... no way. You'll never catch them. They've already run, all of them!!

Chief: ...

You bend down and place your hand lightly on his forehead. The man, already teetering on the edge of frenzy, goes silent. His vacant eyes lock onto you, waiting for your words.

**【Advance】**

- But you're here to help me.

**end**

Chief: This trap was set by the Underground sorcerers. You did make contact with them, and that's all I need to know.

Chief: Next time, I will catch them. Before they even have a chance to run.

**【Advance】**

- Just like how I caught you.

**end**

He stares blankly, uncomprehending, his mind already consumed by the corruption spreading through him. The shackles emerge from your palm. At the very moment his corruption is complete, you cleanse everything.

Chief: Schorly. I'm heading back. Anything you want me to pass along to the lab to speed things up?

Flawed Schorl: Yes. Please memorize the following constants. New Mania spectrum range: 2634 to 2759 Hz. Critical distortion: 1.41q. Decay coefficient: 0.872. Also—

Chief: Okay, that's enough. My brain's full.

Your fingers find the hidden syringe in your sleeve, and in one swift motion, you bury it deep into your neck. The neurotoxin hits fast, and your sight is already dimming, yet the corner of your mouth curves upward.

Chief: I've got people to cut off.

Your eyes snap open. The music stops. Your terminal reads 15:33. The scene has shifted entirely. You are back, and the lights are harsh.

Lemma: Frequency just spiked! ${PlayerName}! Are you okay?

Hecate: Chief!

The playing comes to an abrupt halt. HUSH units rush in to restrain the virtuoso. Hecate supports you as you leave the interrogation room, guiding you to a quieter place. She takes your hand, helping to steady your mind with the shackles.

Nightingale: Chief, your Mania levels surged. Did you see the "Vision"?

Chief: ...Yes. While I still have it...

Chief: Mania spectrum 2634 to 2759 Hz, critical distortion 1.41q, decay coefficient 0.872. Get the numbers to EDGE. That's all I remember. Hope it helps. And tell them, don't hold it against me if I misremembered.

As you recite the words, your awareness returns piece by piece. You are still not entirely accustomed to the sensation of your consciousness being stirred, but the result is undeniable. The experiment worked. This power is now yours to exploit.

**【Advance】**

- Now we can catch those Undergrounders.

**end**

Following the detention of the Underground virtuoso Requiem, EDGE mobilized every available resource to analyze her Mania ability with maximum urgency. In enemy hands, this power would be a formidable obstacle. In yours, it became an unparalleled asset.

And here, inside this interrogation room, her music has indeed set a new anchor for time rewind. This marks your third return.

06:24 PM, August 4 Before the Rewind

Your Schorl relayed a message: the Public Security Bureau had logged multiple new deaths linked to the Cursed Melody. You scanned through the list, crossing out those that did not meet your criteria, and quickly zeroed in on usable targets.

The screening criteria were set before the rewind ever began:

1. The victim must have purchased the Cursed Melody through online platforms, allowing you to hijack their network identity via terminal intrusion.

2. The transactions must be timestamped after 03:50 PM, giving you a viable window to act upon returning from the rewind.

3. The purchase channels must be consistent across all victims, ensuring you can trace them back to a single distribution point.

With these conditions confirmed, you initiated the first rewind to test the mechanism. The experiment held. And then...

03:35 PM, August 4 The Second Rewind

The instant your awareness returned, you were in motion. You hacked terminals, intercepted the data, assumed the stolen identities, and inserted yourself into the transaction channel as a buyer.

Three accounts, three orders, one seller. You pinned down the Cursed Melody dealer, cracked his rudimentary security, and extracted his true location. From there, the rest was simple.

02:12 AM, August 5

After the Second Rewind

Cursed Melody Dealer A: I brought the one here! Your problem now!

You successfully took control of the two street thugs who had been reselling the Cursed Melody for profit, and confirmed the existence of the Underground sorcerers behind them. Now, with this new cover identity in hand, you followed the trail all the way down.

The basic mechanics were more or less clear. A so-called Listener could resonate with the Mania in the Cursed Melody, and this effect intensified at the brink of mental collapse, when the body's hold over consciousness grew fragile enough to allow a stark attunement. In other words, what you had experienced, hearing the Cursed Melody again at the point of dying, was precisely the two conditions that triggered a rewind.

As for the exact point in time you returned to, it seemed tied to the virtuoso's subconscious choice. At that moment, S-125 still seemed to be absorbed in the memory of her playing the violin in the interrogation room, so that was likely where you would land. This time, you were the only audience, which meant Helix would not be part of it.

According to that Schorl, your resonance with the melody ran far deeper than Helix's. More of your consciousness carried over, which was precisely why you could perceive a clearer vision of the "future."

Schorl-II: Whether we're looking at an actual consciousness leap across time, or simply a Mania-driven simulation of the future... the level of accuracy is astonishing either way.

Schorl-II: The ability to retry, to bypass all obstacles until the goal is achieved... it's an incredibly convenient tool. It's easy to see why the Underground has evaded our pursuit for so long.

Schorl-II: Now the tables have turned... You still have that gadget Lemma gave you, I assume?

The Cursed Melody inevitably degraded when transmitted through any intermediary medium, making it too unreliable for a rewind. To circumvent that, EDGE had leveraged the unique conditions of the BR-003 field to replicate S-125's performance, preserving both its acoustic signature and its Mania spectrum.

With the addition of her own blood, the reconstruction's fidelity improved significantly. For the attunement, you would play the recording and then induce a deep coma, loosening the mind from the body's constraints to actively trigger the rewind.

05:12 PM, August 4

The Third Rewind | Present

Only three rewind attempts, and you have already extracted key intel. You hijacked the dealer's network identity, reached the Underground sorcerers pulling the strings, and turned the entire operation around, all in a little over two hours—at least by the clock.

To everyone else, it looks like nothing less than magic: the Cursed Melody plays, your eyes open, and you return with victory in hand. How could that not send a surge of hope through every observer?

Nightingale: With the intel you brought back, we can plan ahead before the enemy even knows what hit them. They haven't detected our rewinds yet, and that gives us an unprecedented advantage.

Nightingale: Chief, should we deploy the Sinners alongside you? With their support, our odds will improve significantly!

Nightingale's enthusiasm is contagious. You feel it seeping into your own mood, and you glance over at Hecate standing beside you.

Chief: The Cursed Melody hits Sinners too hard. And watching all of you die, over and over... even with the rewind, I can't go through that even once. Trust me on this. Just wait for me to come back.

Chief: For now, keep the sorcerers in our sights and locate the four civilians caught in the Cursed Melody's web. Isolate them. Don't let any of them die. That includes the dealer.

Chief: This is only the beginning. And it's not just the sorcerers or the virtuoso...

Chief: We're taking down the Sillage Society behind it all. And their so-called Sage. Every last one of them, all in one sweep.

Hecate: Mm. I believe in you, Chief. You'll come back safely. I know you will.

Everything is in place, and you step out of the break room, intent on action. Your path takes you past the interrogation room just as Requiem emerges, flanked by HUSH units. They're heading toward the next phase of testing.

She is fully restrained now. Suppressors lock around her wrists, ankles, and her neck, the metal biting into her skin and leaving raw red rings wherever they press. Her steps are unsteady. Her face is even paler than before, yet her head stays lifted, her eyes fixed straight ahead.

She is looking at the violin. The HUSH units have taken it from her, and the humanoid machines have left fresh, careless scratches across its body. She watches, and that is all she can do. Until her gaze happens to meet yours, and you both go still.

A long beat passes. Then you hear her speak, her voice barely a murmur.

Requiem: ...I see... So that is why you asked me to play.

Requiem: Was my ability useful to you?

**【Choose One】**

- Answer

  Chief: ...Yes. For that, I'm grateful. It will save many lives.

  Does she understand? Would she even care? You cannot know what goes on inside an Undergrounder's mind. All that reaches you is a weak, hollow laugh.

  Requiem: ...Then, if I have been of some help, please show some mercy to my friends.

- Evade

  You stay silent and turn your gaze aside. Requiem reads the gesture clearly, a flicker of understanding passing through her face. She offers a small smile, bitter and edged with something like grief.

  Requiem: ...I see. This is a feeling I've never known before. Thank you. I have learned so much.

**end**

Requiem vanishes from your view. Schorl drifts silently at your side, and you can almost sense the eyes behind its lenses, fixed on you with a keen curiosity.

Schorl-II: Are you concerned about her feelings? Think you mishandled it and damaged her trust?

Schorl-II: There's no need. Her Mania levels stayed stable throughout the performance, unlike yours or the other listeners'. The virtuoso herself most likely does not participate in the consciousness sync. Next rewind, say something nicer. She won't remember that you tricked her.

Schorl-II: Then you can try earning her trust. We still have many questions for her.

You pause. A long silence passes before you answer.

Chief: ...Next time. There are more pressing matters right now.

## N11-10-A Return

Evening, August 6

07:44 PM

Abandoned Stadium, The Rust

Years of neglect have hollowed out the stadium. You and Schorl remain hidden in the shadows, watching the empty field below. But instead of a maniacal ritual gathering you had braced for, you find only a strange, suspended quiet.

A dozen figures encircle a slowly rotating vinyl record. Some stand with eyes closed, smiling as they trace the shape of a vertically slit third eye across the ground. Others sway gently, fingers plucking and bowing at instruments that exist only in their own minds.

An unnatural mist drifts through the air. The markings on the ground form an immense ritual circle, glowing faintly in the dim light. You clench your fist. You had thought that securing the virtuoso would be enough to strangle the Cursed Melody's spread at the source. Instead, the ones pulling the strings have only grown more brazen.

Is this the Underground's vengeance, or—

All those present are already lost to the Cursed Melody. They seem to see something beautiful in its current, a vision that has drawn them away from everything real.

Weary Youth: Hehe... yes... I remember...

Their faces are all hollow, their minds unmistakably warped by Mania. Then, the first victim appears.

Dejected Woman: ...Sob... Aaaahhh!!

Dejected Woman: ...Please, take me with you... I don't want to stay here anymore. Take me back!

She smiles through her tears, her arms opening wide as she drifts toward the center of the ritual circle in a trance. Whatever vision she sees, it has taken her completely. She sinks into it, her body moving in rhythm with the Cursed Melody.

As the music swells and falls, the woman picks up a jagged shard of broken vinyl like a puppet following unseen strings. She lifts it above her head, and drives it into her own throat without hesitation.

Chief: Is that... the "blessing"?!!

Blood pours from the self-inflicted wound, its flow synchronized with the melody's pulse. The woman sinks to the ground. The vinyl record in the center continues to spin. The others keep swaying, lost in their private illusions, blind to what just happened.

Your body tenses to act, to dash out, but you are stopped.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, patience. The one pulling the strings hasn't appeared yet. You must wait.

Flawed Schorl: Once you have the intel you need and understand what the enemy is after, you can rewind. Their deaths will never have happened.

**【Advance】**

- ...I know.

**end**

You clench your jaw. One by one, the figures in the ritual circle fall. Some quietly sink to the ground with faint smiles. Others whisper names through their last breaths. And many more meet fates as brutal and unsparing as the "blessing" you witnessed.

Logic tells you the black triangle is right. But bearing witness to this is a trial in itself. Slowly, even you find something seeping in from the music, something that feels... different from any version of the melody you have heard before.

It takes until the last body has stopped moving, until the blood pools and darkens and the runes gutter out, until the stench of it fills your lungs, for the moment you have been waiting for to arrive.

A woman with green hair, cloaked in black, walks slowly into the ritual circle as the melody still hums in the air. Beneath the folds of her robe, a crimson single-eye rune gleams.

Underground Sorcerer: ......

A quiet sigh escapes her. She reaches down, raises the needle, and the record winds to a halt.

The last trace of the melody slips away into nothing. The runes across the ground retract in unison, pouring back into the darkness at her feet like water into stone. The stadium holds its breath. Then, at last, silence.

Flawed Schorl: Target spotted. Underground sorcerer, at least mid-tier.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, no more waiting. You're good to go.

The HUSH units have been standing by, waiting. Now, all eyes are on you.

**【Advance】**

- Catch her!

**end**

At your signal, the HUSH units surge forward. But the sorceress is sharp, and she is ready. In the same breath, she raises a wave of Underground Puppets to block their advance.

The HUSH units pour out everything they have, locking down the Puppet swarm and buying you a window. You and Schorl exploit the opening, pushing past the line and closing in fast.

The ritual circle flares back to life, spawning another wave of Puppets that surge toward you. Schorl strikes, blue light carving through the air, cutting the key anchors of the ritual and crippling the summoning mid-rise.

The sorcerer tries to move again, but Schorl is already there. A precise beam of light strikes the seal forming between her fingers, shattering the sorcery before it can complete. In the next instant, a lattice of beams locks around her, forming a cage that both wounds and binds.

Underground Sorcerer: Cough...!

Some in the Underground are so consumed by their cause that they would rather die than reveal anything under capture. You and Schorl anticipated this and acted accordingly, immobilizing her before she could even think of resistance.

Chief: This isn't how you used the Cursed Melody before. What kind of ritual is this? Why were they acting like that? What's your goal in spreading it now?

Chief: Talk!

Underground Sorcerer: Heh... hahaha... Even the great virtuoso gave her life... and you think I... would bow to you? We've long been ready for this...

Her eyes burn with a hatred sharper and deeper than you have ever seen from other Undergrounders. Why?

Because she believes you killed the virtuoso, and despises you for taking one of their own?

What a farce! The Sillage Society, the Garden, the innocents lying dead in this very stadium... the Underground has used and discarded countless lives. That has always been their way. The only thing they truly hate you for is getting in their way, and that is a hatred you ought to wear with pride.

Chief: Your virtuoso is gone. No more Vision, no more rewinds. You have nowhere to run now.

Underground Sorcerer: No... She will return. The virtuoso, the Sage, the Shepherd... they will all return to us. The Vision has foretold it. This is what fate has already written. And you...

Chief: Where is the Sage? Where is the woman called Evelleon?

You cut her off and lean in closer, pushing to draw more out of her. She takes the bait. Ignoring her injuries, she lunges with a scream, and the spike she had been hiding drives clean through your chest.

Underground Sorcerer: You monster! You killed her! No one will protect you now... Everyone will hunt you down... The Sage will—

Her words die in her throat. A lance of blue light pierces her hand and heart in one stroke, and she slides to the ground, already gone. Schorl halts sharply in front of you, hovering with an edge of urgency. Worry, maybe?

Flawed Schorl: Provoking her at that range was reckless.

Chief: ...You should just leave me be. I'll go back soon. I'm not actually dead. I was trying to get more out of her.

Chief: She really had no idea I could rewind... Charging in like that, knowing she'd die. That's insane.

You turn to Schorl.

Chief: I've got time before the rewind. Check the victims. Whatever you find, tell me. I'll remember it on the way back.

The flawed Schorly hesitates for a moment, then moves to secure the scene and reach out to logistics. After a short while, it returns and begins its report.

Flawed Schorl: Several victims show Mania residue from the Cursed Melody. But unlike previous self-inflicted deaths, the disruption in them is much more aggressive. The signature is closer to—

Its words leave you stunned.

Chief: ...Say that again?!

You try to press for more, but your body gives out. The Cursed Melody crystal shatters on its own, and the melody rings out. Your consciousness fragments, caught in the current of strings, pulling you away fast. Just like always.

No. Not quite.

The melody pulls your consciousness in, weaving it into time itself. Yet this time, it is no longer only the current you. Layers of past rewinds surge together, colliding in a rush of memory that fills your skull and makes it impossible to tell where one life ends and the next begins.

Flawed Schorl: The disruption in them is much more aggressive. The signature is closer to a fusion of multiple distinct consciousnesses, spectra entangled. Fleeting and shallow, but the resemblance to S-125's state is unmistakable.

Pain, not your own, floods your body from all sides at once. You cannot breathe. Your skin feels scorched. Your bones splinter with muffled cracks. Through the haze, you see ghostly hands reaching for you, lifting you, setting you down on the stone altar in the center of a dim room.

Frenzied Cries: *Listen, listen! This is the sacred melody passed down through centuries... Surrender to it, inherit it, resonate with the Divine. Only you can understand it. Only you can play it—*

Schorl's voice cuts in and out. You fight to hold onto it. Every word matters, and you need to carry as much as possible into the next cycle.

Flawed Schorl: Also, despite their different origins and no prior connection to one another... they all share a trait absent in past victims. Each either studied an instrument or was a dedicated music enthusiast. Their sensitivity to music is far above normal.

Frenzied Cries: *Give your ears, give your hands, give everything that can perceive It! The unclean flesh will be cleansed. The vessel will be made new—*

Flawed Schorl: Combined with the accelerating spread of the Cursed Melody and the increased urgency in the Underground's actions. I believe it is tied to Requiem's disappearance.

Frenzied Cries: *It has slumbered for far too long... Everything is for Its awakening, Its return...*

Flawed Schorl: We cannot rule out the possibility that with Requiem now believed dead, the Underground may be attempting to create a new "virtuoso."

Schorl's voice fades, drowned out by the rising tide of frenzied shouts. The clamor layers over itself, a chaos of sound that slowly condenses into a single voice. All of them are chanting the same melody, crying the same name.

Frenzied Cries: *Requiem, the music for the repose of the soul, our virtuoso, will bring It back!*

Chief: Cough!! Cough, cough!!

You open your eyes. A searing headache steals your voice, and before you can steady yourself, a fit of coughing tears through you. Requiem's bow falters.

Lemma: Frequency just spiked! ${PlayerName}! Are you okay?

Hecate: Chief?!

## N11-9-B Gambit

Hecate: Chief!

Hecate shakes you gently, using the shackles to anchor your scattered thoughts. The tide of confusion begins to pull back, and sound seeps in again, piece by piece, sharpening into something you can finally recognize.

Nightingale: Mania flux is within the safe range, but this reaction... Chief, are you alright? Did something happen during the rewind?

Your mind is a haze; the sequence of events is still out of reach. What lingers is only pain, a relentless current of anguish that coils at the mere thought of the melody. The music is burned into you as deep as the memory itself.

But that pain was never physical. It was never even yours. Someone else's memories are crashing through you, battering your body and mind alike.

How many times have you rewound? Your memory is laced with some holes, blank stretches where something should be. Have you been corrupted?

Schorl-II: Consciousness is destabilized. High levels of interference detected. Consciousness corruption caused by too many rewind attempts? It's possible. Administer a sedative first.

Schorl-II: Chief ${PlayerName}, stay with us. Report the critical intel first. We need to cross-check the accuracy.

Chief: The eigenvalue starts with 6.28... The anonymous account ID is xnlfh97234, location is... the stadium ruins at the third fork on the old Rust road—

The fog in your mind gradually lifts, and the details lock into place. You pass along the rest of the key information, and then—

You turn to Requiem. Her violin has been taken by HUSH once more; her face is still pale and startled, but the look in her eyes has changed. It is no longer a plea.

Your reaction seems to have shaken her as much as it did everyone else.

Requiem: ...Are you all right?

Her voice and gaze carry a note of concern and... scrutiny.

Chief: The music you pursue isn't just about emotional resonance. The Cursed Melody the Underground is after doesn't just connect people. At its peak, it obliterates the boundaries of the self, forcing its way into other minds, whether they want it or not.

Requiem: ...It does have that power. Music can open the most guarded heart and show a world unlike any other.

Chief: You make it sound beautiful, but it's not. When the Cursed Melody's attunement goes too far, it doesn't just open you—it dissolves you. Once your boundaries are gone, other wills rush in and overwrite yours. Your consciousness is corrupted. Your self is erased.

Chief: Some people, desperate to follow the Cursed Melody to their "kindred spirits," kill their own bodies, thinking it will free their consciousness from its cage. That is why the Cursed Melody kills.

Chief: And those who try to hold on to themselves become vessels for the Cursed Melody's Mania. Their minds will be slowly eaten away, brainwashed, and used. That's what Helix is as a "vessel." That's what those unkillable Undergrounders become.

Chief: "The power of music"... Your people certainly took it to its extreme.

Requiem: For someone who has been trapped in silence for so long... a voice that answers is worth everything.

Requiem: To see the only light in the darkness and want to cast everything aside just to reach it, or to keep it close for even a moment longer... I think I can understand that.

Chief: But in the end, it's brainwashing and murder. You should understand that better than anyone, because that's how you were "made." Have you forgotten? I just lived through those memories.

Chief: I was invaded by memories that weren't mine. You were also made to listen, over and over, broken again and again, your body resisting, your self falling apart. That maniacal chaos... that's what made you their so-called virtuoso.

Chief: And you call them gifts that music brought into your life?

She takes in every word, letting your anger settle rather than pushing it aside. After a moment of thought, she responds in a soft voice.

Requiem: If you're asking how I feel... I don't find it painful.

Requiem: This body may once have had the "self" you speak of. Perhaps it was broken by them... But I don't mourn it now, and I don't resent them for it. I think... I can live just fine without that boundary.

Requiem: Without it, I can hear others more clearly. I can hold onto the threads that connect us.

She turns to look at you again. This time, something like a smile plays at the corner of her lips.

Requiem: Unlike me, your sense of "self" is remarkably strong. Is that unwavering feeling what you call being "right"? Then... how did you come by it?

Requiem: What shaped you? Was it someone who guided you? A friend? Did you find it out in a broader world, through all that you've seen and heard? Or does it come from your past, from your memories?

Requiem: Please, tell me. I really want to know.

After a moment of thought, she adds:

Requiem: Don't worry, no one is controlling me. My friends would be angry if they knew I came here. But I came anyway.

Requiem: Just as my heart chose to follow them before words could find me, now my heart is what keeps me here with you. Even if all my friends despise you, I still want to hear what you have to say... This is "my" will, isn't it?

You hold her gaze, and finally answer.

Chief: ...I thought you chose me because I remind you of someone.

Chief: The shackles I stole. The power, the Mania, the familiar feeling. All of it resembles the one you've been waiting for—the Shepherd of the Underground.

Chief: When you look at me, what you see is the one they shaped you to serve. Isn't that so?

Flawed Schorl: ...Chief.

Schorl-II: Hmm? Do go on. Why stop when things are getting so interesting?

One Schorl cuts you off; the other slides in to finish the thought. For the first time, the two hovering triangles seem to stand in quiet opposition.

You are not particularly inclined to answer them. But Requiem has been paying attention, and it appears she has reached her own understanding.

Requiem: This is simply your imagination. For someone of her power... you two are nothing alike.

You can tell she is covering for you.

And so the conversation draws to a close. HUSH units file in to collect the violin. You are guided toward the medical room for further examination, while Requiem is fitted with yet another set of instruments.

The intelligence you brought back this time has clearly piqued Schorl-II's curiosity. "Boundary breaking," "attuned resonance"... these terms seem to have lit a trail for it. It urges you, with some insistence, to postpone the next rewind until the latest round of experiments yields its findings.

Before you go, the virtuoso offers one last word.

Requiem: You have a firm will. Our music cannot sway you so easily. That strength will take you to many places, I think.

Requiem: But be careful not to rely on it too much. The hurt is real, and so is the sorrow. To die again and again, even if you return, that weighs on a person. Please, look after yourself.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, we've finished checking the list you provided. One of the online IDs led us to a suspect matching the profile of an Underground sorcerer, and her behavior lines up perfectly with what you described. She's now under close watch.

Flawed Schorl: As for the others... the buyers, the dealers, and those, uh... music enthusiasts, we've had them all in protective custody. They did show a tendency to seek out the Cursed Melody, but we got to them in time. They're no longer in danger.

Chief: ...The ones exposed to the Cursed Melody... do you have their profiles?

It sends the report to your terminal.

Cursed Melody Dealer A: Wh-what d'you mean "selling info"?! I didn't do anything that crazy! And nothing even went wrong anyway!

Cursed Melody Dealer A: We still won in the end, right? We've got big heroes doing the heavy lifting, so small-timers like me just scrape a little off the sides... Taking cash from the enemy is practically patriotic! Look, war, West District, Black Ring, Mania—whatever—it doesn't matter to us nobodies. Winning or losing, it doesn't change my wallet!

Cursed Melody Dealer A: Oh hey, big hero, I'm down five thousand from a bet the other day. Mind spotting me some DisCoins?

Weary Voice: Honestly, I'm fine with how things are. Just coasting along, not needing much... I used to save up for... well, for stanning Phanuel. I just wanted Phanuel to do well. Sigh...

Weary Voice: ...But she's gone, so what's the point?

Dejected Voice: The Black Ring isn't really that bad, if you think about it... It's kind of a miracle, don't you agree? A sign that we messed up, that we aren't good enough. Maybe it's God's last warning.

Dejected Voice: If God really exists... maybe living wouldn't feel so rough...

You scroll through the recordings, one after another. They were victims once, names on a case file. Now, for the first time, you are truly listening to their voices.

...These are not voices you are familiar with. The people around you—Eugene, Wynn, Nightingale, the Sinners—none of them sound like this. You cannot name the difference, but perhaps it is the thread they still carry inside them, something that still tugs them forward. Not everyone has that, it seems.

They seem unmoored from the city itself. What they are looking for does not exist anywhere. Is this who the Cursed Melody calls to? You cannot say.

Or maybe it is that too many rewinds have chipped away at something inside you. The melody has sunk in, and now you are more easily swayed by others than you used to be.

Eirene: She's the virtuoso of the Cursed Melody. The Underground shaped her to soak up emotion, to break every last boundary inside her, all in pursuit of the deepest resonance. To a living resonator like that, a self is nothing but surplus. So tell me, why are you pouring sincerity into an instrument?

Eirene: See her for what she is: a means, a weapon, the decisive piece on your board. Wield her wisely. Wield her ruthlessly...

Even those words put you on edge now. You cut the feed and return to your work.

Which is why you welcome the new message when it arrives.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, the sorcerer is making her move.

**【Advance】**

- Let's go.

**end**

## N11-10-B Rewind Again

Since then, you have kept the Underground sorcerer under close watch. Her movements have grown strange, straying far from the usual patterns of the Underground's routine Mania spread.

If those rituals are indeed meant to find or create the next "virtuoso," then an undertaking of this scale would certainly be reported to the Underground's chain of command. You cannot afford to lose the thread.

The enemy, however, proves more elusive and cunning than anticipated. Now that they have lost the ability to "rewind," you can see the Underground for what it truly is: furtive, skittish, all communications routed through disposable, untraceable networks. Like rats in the gutter, too cowardly to face the light.

And yet, when a conflict does break out and you close in for the capture, she throws herself at you with reckless abandon, willing to drag you both under rather than yield a single word. In those moments, that fanatical fearlessness becomes its own form of terror. Against such an opponent, tracing leads without exposing yourself is a steep challenge.

But fortunately, the power of rewind is yours. And that is all it takes.

More clashes, more rewinds, and finally, a thread that holds. This time, you have a real chance to pin down someone important from the Underground. Could it be that Evelleon?

In Eastside's reclamation housing zone, a sprawl of broken structures lies. Faded signage still carries the echo of grand plans, now buried beneath years of neglect and decay.

Flawed Schorl: Every leak from the 9th Agency has traced back to this same point. We've had our share of failures, but that's exactly how we finally pinpointed the core. The source is here. Based on your intel, it's highly likely a Sillage Society key operative.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, support units are in place. Do we proceed as planned?

Chief: Yes. No changes.

You wait, still and quiet. From the shadow of the ruins, a familiar figure emerges first: green hair, that crimson single-eye rune. But her bearing is much different this time.

Underground Sorcerer: You have come.

She bows, the gesture measured and respectful.

Underground Sorcerer: Forgive me. We have performed the ritual again and again, but still no...

From further back in the shadows, another figure emerges. Lean and tall, with features hidden in shadow. That familiar posture of ingrained superiority turns your stomach.

Mysterious Voice: ...Secure the perimeter. Don't let anyone in.

Flawed Schorl: Voiceprint matched. This individual goes by Mr. E. He was the true controller of Euphoria, dismantled a year ago, and is a senior member of the Sillage Society.

Flawed Schorl: We have discovered his traces multiple times in operations against the Underground. He's their central liaison, the one relaying orders across the Sillage Society network.

Chief: Not the Sage, but still a major catch.

**【Advance】**

- Stop waiting

**end**

You give the signal. Several HUSH units surge forward, bypassing the broken terrain without hesitation. The rush of motion catches Mr. E's attention at once, and he turns, locking his gaze on you.

Mr. E: You're...!

As in previous encounters, the sorcerer appears to possess an almost instinctive awareness of approaching threats. She has laid Puppet traps throughout the area, and she always reacts the instant the operation begins, throwing obstacles into every move you make.

Underground Sorcerer: Sir, leave! Don't let them—ngh!

Her instincts are sharp, but preparation wins the day. The Puppet sorcery is dismantled before it can activate, and Schorl's blue light drives through the sorcerer in the same breath.

Yet her distraction buys enough time. Mr. E tears open a dimensional fissure and slips through without looking back, leaving every one of his subordinates behind. The fissure collapses swiftly, sealing itself shut.

Schorl moves to pursue, but even at its current size, the Mania-phase space is too tight to enter.

Underground Sorcerer: Heh... Save the effort. You fools will never catch him, just like you'll never catch fate itself...

Chief: I'm glad you're so fond of that stolen fissure trick. We didn't catch him this time, fine. I only came here to collect data anyway. The more we gather, the better. Eventually, someone will catch you all.

Underground Sorcerer: ?!

Chief: Our goal is to collect as much spectrum data as possible before that fissure disappears! Keep her occupied! Buy Schorly enough time to get the data.

From the shadow of the ruins, the familiar face emerges once more.

Underground Sorcerer: You have come.

Underground Sorcerer: Forgive me. We have performed the ritual again and again, but still no...

The man steps out from deeper in the shadows, just as last time.

Mr. E: ...Secure the perimeter. Don't let anyone in.

The same pattern repeats. The fissure opens, and the man disappears into it without hesitation. This time, you no longer need to rush forward.

Once the sorcerer slips in after him and the entrance seals, you open your terminal quietly.

Chief: Lemma, the eigenvalue verification this time, all good?

Buzz buzz— Clack clack clack—

Lemma: ...Checked and confirmed. Coefficients line up perfectly. Analysis of the enemy's dimensional fissure is done. You're good to go.

Lemma: Stay sharp, and make sure you wipe them all out!

**【Advance】**

- Leave it to me.

**end**

The light twists before your eyes. A pinprick of brilliance splits open into the fissure, its edges curling with deep violet surges of Mania. You motion for HUSH to stay where they are. They stand out too much. You breathe in deeply and cross the threshold.

## N11-11-B Revelation

Behind you, the fissure has vanished. Before you lies an uncanny space. Instruments drift through a cold, thin mist: double basses, harps, a shattered pipe organ, all suspended in the lightless dark, floating as if waiting for a hand to play them.

As you brush past each one, broken notes sound out, plucked by invisible fingers. You recognize them at once. Every fragment belongs to the Cursed Melody.

Flawed Schorl: Cursed Melody's Mania concentration in this phase space is over four times higher than at the Rust ritual site. This is the enemy's primary facility for cultivating virtuosos. High-intensity infusion appears to have taken place here recently.

Chief: ...Let's find the victims.

You follow the trembling notes to the first trapped virtuoso. He is huddled in the corner beside a piano, slumped over the keys. His eyes are wide and unblinking. Saliva trails from his lips. His finger hammers the same key with mechanical numbness, over and over, as if trying to drive it straight through the instrument.

Vacant Musician: ...Is it this one? No... again... This one? ...No... again...

Vacant Musician: ...I can hear it... That's my music. I can hear it...

The second one is an old man clutching a broken mandolin, trembling violently as he gnaws at it. The third is a young woman ripping out her instrument's strings and wrapping them tightly around her throat. The fourth... By now, the pattern is clear. They are all gone, their selves eroded and punctured by the relentless melody.

You feel cold to the bone.

So this... is the way they are made? Has every so-called virtuoso been broken like this?

Chief: Those bastards...

Voice in the Shadows: A compliment, I take it? I'm honored.

You whip around. The man emerges from the darkness, his steps light and unhurried, like a serpent slithering out from the fissure's shadow.

Schorl shifts into battle mode instantly, veins of blue light flowing across its faceted body. You tighten your stance, blocking the victims with your own frame.

Chief: ...I thought the Underground actually cared about its virtuosos. Looks like I was wrong.

Mr. E: They volunteered. To be chosen, to carry the will of the great virtuosos before them... that is salvation for their otherwise fleeting, worthless lives.

Mr. E: A shame their aptitude is so lacking. Such sublime music, right at their ears, and they can only cower, unable to open themselves to the sacred...

Mr. E: They're spent. Take them if you wish. Your masters will find them useful. Consider it a gift, for this is our first meeting.

**【Advance】**

- You...!

**end**

The words are barely out before he moves. A corrupted blade cleaves through the air toward you. Red light flares from your shackles, erupting in front of you to catch the strike head-on. Schorl's laser follows in the same breath, a blue sweep that scorches the ground where he stood a moment ago.

The firelight catches his face at last. The man is cold to the bone, his gaze crawling over you with a half-smile that looks more like a sneer. It makes your skin crawl.

Mr. E: So we finally meet face to face, Chief. A certain sweet, charming girl has recounted your story to me countless times, and I've also seen your power for myself through the Sage's incense.

Mr. E: And let's not forget my former colleagues. You gave them quite a few lessons in the West District.

Runes spread from beneath his feet, slithering across the ground like vipers. Puppets rise from the sorcery circle one after another, dodder vines winding around their frames, feeding them with borrowed strength. The heavy tread of HUSH units grows closer. A battle unlike any other is about to erupt.

Yet in the fleeting seconds of hand-to-hand struggle, you pick up a smell on him.

It's that woman's incense. The same one you remember all too well, the one that never quite leaves your senses.

**【Advance】**

- Where's the woman who was with you?

**end**

Mr. E: The Sage? Do you want to see her? Do you miss her, or need something from her? Perhaps you're banking on her goodwill? That won't get you anywhere.

Chief: I'm going to end her. And end all of this.

No more words. You unleash your shackles at full force, Dark Sound roaring into his position and driving him back half a step.

Chief: You rounded up the most sensitive, empathic people you could find and saturated them with Mania through the Cursed Melody and your rituals. You never wanted virtuosos; you wanted vessels, the ones who resonate the most. To command the Cursed Melody, command consciousness, command time, for you.

Chief: And they still called you friends, musical companions. They gave you the Vision to protect you. That's how you've survived this long. Look at what you did with that trust! Fooled them, brainwashed them, bled them dry.

You gesture toward the vacant-eyed virtuosos at your back.

Chief: If this is the Sage of Death's doing, she's more hideous than anyone I've ever known. Where is she? If you won't talk, I'll carve my way through you until I find her.

E sidesteps your question with a shrug and a dismissive smirk.

Mr. E: Impressive reasoning. To have pieced that together...

Mr. E: You've been through quite a few rewinds to get here, haven't you?

Your breath catches. Has he figured you out?!

Mr. E: You've chased the Cursed Melody for so long. Relentless. The slightest slip and you are on our heels.

Mr. E: But this time, none of our bait worked. That's when it hit me. Maybe you've already made your attempt. To slip into this place so cleanly, so prepared... it's as if you knew everything ahead of time.

Mr. E: It was the rewind that gave you the edge to stop our ritual that night. Did Eirene Campbell have a hand in it? And Requiem?

Mr. E: ...Requiem is alive. Hidden somewhere...? That explains why the new virtuoso couldn't take on the inheritance. She's been in your hands all along. What did you do to her? When will you kill her?

He chuckles under his breath.

Mr. E: Better keep it quiet. No one must know... or else—

In the midst of the standoff, the HUSH units waiting in the shadows charge into the fray. But the nearest one never lands a blow. Mr. E pivots in a flash, and its head caves in, black oil splattering across the floor.

In his hand is a closed long umbrella. His expression warps and hardens as Mania crystallizes into a mask, melting into skin and sinew like a second face. The sight of him is more dreadful than any Corruptor.

Mr. E: Death's wrath will devour you.

**【Advance】**

- Will it?

**end**

You wipe the blood and black oil from the corner of your mouth and answer him with a cold smile.

Chief: All I see is a clown throwing a tantrum. You're an insult to death itself.

The black triangle rises back into the air. The HUSH units fall back into formation. The space around you remains in flux, with runic sigils and the shackles' crimson light glaring at each other through the gloom.

And you don't feel even a flicker of doubt. All you can think about is how you are going to wipe that loathsome smirk off his face.

Underground Sorcerer: Sir, leave! Don't let them—ngh!

The green-haired sorcerer throws herself before Mr. E, desperate to shield the man she worships. But against the HUSH units' onslaught, she is frail and insignificant, pierced through in the span of a heartbeat.

In that fleeting rupture, you surge forward through the splashing blood, quickly closing the distance between you and Mr. E. He merely tilts the tip of his umbrella to one side, and his black suit emerges untouched by the blood.

Chief: You throw away your own people the moment it's convenient.

Chief: A thing that can only deceive and drain others will never reach the god you worship.

The man remains unmoved, his gaze skimming over you with contempt. That is fine. You never planned to talk him around.

You hold his stare. Taunt him, goad him, drive him to the edge, let him tear loose with everything he has. You will hold the line!

And when he does, you will be there to catch every word he lets slip.

Crimson light seeps from your shackles, gliding noiselessly over the ground, threading through the broken sorcery circles, and winding upward along the darkness beneath the man's heels.

Then a piercing noise lances into your consciousness. The din of the battlefield recedes, becoming distant and hollow. In its place, a suppressed voice surfaces, one the man has tried to bury deep inside himself.

Mr. E?: The virtuoso will never truly vanish... The body may rot, but memory endures, feeling endures, and above all... the melody It bestowed endures...

Mr. E?: Only those whom It has chosen... only... ⬛⬛⬛⬛...

Without warning, a distorted form slams into you. The shackles coiled around Mr. E's consciousness are ripped away by an unknown power, and the recoil floods back along the connection. Everything goes dark for an instant, and you reel backward.

From the swirling mist, the corrupted sorcerer emerges, her twisted limbs still poised mid-sorcery. She stares at you with a gaze that seethes with both terror and venom.

Corrupted Sorcerer: ...You've resonated this deeply? How many times have you rewound? How much of our power are you trying to steal? You disgust me...

Corrupted Sorcerer: You think more knowledge will change the ending? No matter how many times you rewind, you're just walking down the melody we already wrote!

You press a hand to your arm, still throbbing from the backlash, and steady yourself. The shackles tighten in your grip, their red light flaring brighter and more searing than before.

Chief: You know what the most disgusting thing is? Working with filth like you.

Chief: Go on. End me. Kill me again and again. But you're cowards, and sooner or later, you'll be the ones scrambling to rewind.

Chief: But remember, we will win.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, your body is breaking down. You need to rewind now—

You ignore the warning and take another step forward.

The corrupted sorcerer falters for a second. Her gaze drops to the taint creeping across you from the shackles' backlash, and for the first time, something other than hatred flickers across her Mania-twisted face. Genuine confusion.

Corrupted Sorcerer: Why? You have no faith. You serve no great presence. So why push yourself to this point?

Chief: You left me no choice.

Corrupted Sorcerer: Arrogant fool. We only took back what was always ours. You think you'd even be standing here without us?

Corrupted Sorcerer: If the true Shepherd weren't bound... we'd never have had to sink this low, working with these people.

Corrupted Sorcerer: They're all insects... They fear. They cling to anything stronger... Without us, they'd just find another god. We just showed these hollow creatures the right path!

Chief: You made it worse!

Corrupted Sorcerer: Say what you want...

Corrupted Sorcerer: The true Shepherd will return. She will break free and hear us again.

Corrupted Sorcerer: When she does, all broken time will be mended. Every stray fate will be set right. She will lead us beyond death, to a true transcendence—

Her last syllable twists into a shriek. The sorcerer throws her arms wide, and black runes erupt from her body in cascading layers. The floating instruments shudder as one, their vibrations condensing into a tangible wave of sound that crashes toward you. At the same moment, Mr. E snaps open his long umbrella, and the space around you folds inward.

Chief: HUSH!

Schorl launches skyward first, its blue beams slicing through the leading wall of sound. You charge into the gap over scattered debris, the shackles' crimson glow hugging your frame as it tears through the remaining contamination.

Boom—!!

Schorl's laser sears past your shoulder, shattering every defense ahead and sealing off Mr. E's escape.

Mr. E?: ...!

The long umbrella flies from his grip. Your shackles pierce the ground at his feet. Each strike strips away another layer of his defense.

Until the final blow lands.

Mr. E kneels among the shattered piano keys, the shackles driven clean through his chest. Crimson blood pulses from the wound and spreads beneath him in a dark pool. He bleeds like any man.

You step closer, lungs burning. He lifts his head, and his eyes hold no fear at all. Only a fervor that looks almost like relief.

You crouch down and grip his shoulder hard.

The shackles sink deeper through the wound, breaching the threshold of his mind. The resonance comes back. This time, no sorcery conceals what lies beneath. His raw consciousness unfolds before you, and the voices rise at last, sharp and unmistakably clear.

Mr. E?: Only those whom It has chosen... only a virtuoso has the power to rewind...

Mr. E?: So precious a gift... better to shatter it myself than hand it to the enemy...

Chief: ?!

Mr. E: Hehehe... The Vision foretold it. A flawed accident should never derail a destined course. That isn't a miracle; it's an obstruction.

**【Advance】**

- The one you want to kill isn't me... it's—

**end**

He lets out a quiet laugh.

Mr. E: The Sage couldn't bring herself to do it, so I did it for her. And I will keep doing so, until the true Shepherd returns... You know her. You've seen her. Tremble in fear!

Mr. E: All for the great transcendence...

Chief: Bastard... You monster!! Schorl, we go back!

## N11-12-B Death

08:32 PM, August 7, N.F. 116

Special Mania Containment Facility

You burst into the containment facility and almost run straight into Nightingale, who is hurrying out to meet you.

Nightingale: Chief!

**【Advance】**

- How is she?!

**end**

Nightingale parts her lips, but no words come. The worry in her eyes and the hesitation in her expression, they tell you more than any words could.

Chief: I knew it... Where is she?!

You already feel where she is. Nightingale starts to speak, but you do not wait for her words. You move, following the pull of the shackles to the other end of the bond.

Chief: Move!

You force your way past every locked barrier. Move faster! You can still reach her in time! All you need is to open this one last door—

Schorl-II: You're finally back.

Schorl-II: Perfect. There's still time. I'll leave the rest to you.

You do not answer. The devastation before you leaves your mind completely blank.

Requiem: Ngh... ah...

Requiem lies among scattered shards of glass. Around her, thin red threads weave through the broken pieces, converging toward her like veins returning to a heart.

Those threads are "strings" of blood, flowing from her wrists and spreading outward in every direction.

Schorl-II: In your last rewind, you returned with an especially valuable finding. The Cursed Melody's reach is far deeper and broader than earlier experiments suggested. It can, as you said, "break down the boundaries of consciousness"?

Schorl-II: This wasn't captured in our prior work. The methodology may have been flawed. I adjusted the parameters and, as a result, the outcome has been... illuminating.

${PlayerName}: You...!

The rebuke dies on your tongue. A touch lands on your wrist.

Requiem: ...You actually... came back...

Her eyes, already drifting out of focus, somehow find yours. Requiem manages a faint smile and tries to lift her head toward you, but her arm falters before it reaches halfway. You catch her hand and hold it tight.

Her fingers are cold as stone. How long has she been bleeding like this? The thin streams of crimson have spread across the floor, and Requiem's face is drained white as paper. Only the bracelet on her wrist still glows like a dim red pulse.

She told you it was a "gift" from Death. Now she is driving it into resonance. Her blood gathers into the shape of a crystalline violin, then fractures apart, its strings scattering into droplets before reforming again.

${PlayerName}: No... you don't have to do this. I'll give you back the violin. Just stop...

You pull her into your arms and channel the shackles' power into her, desperate to heal. But her life is slipping away too fast, too far. And still, she smiles. She is the one consoling you.

Requiem: It's okay... A virtuoso can't always depend on her instrument. The best pieces are played with your life. I am my own violin.

Requiem: Don't worry. I'm from... the Underground, remember? This isn't really a wound to me.

Requiem: See... this gift from my friend will mend me. They've always taken care of me like this...

The bracelet of Death is trembling. Tiny dodder vines spill from its surface, creeping up Requiem's wrist as they strain to return the life force it once stored back into her failing body.

But the vines are too fragile themselves. They wither almost instantly, crumbling into dust against her skin.

${PlayerName}: No... please...

Your words come out broken and jumbled. Requiem keeps smiling, though you are not sure she can still make out what you are saying.

Her gaze drifts upward, unfocused, settling on the cold black triangle suspended high above.

Requiem: I just learned... something important.

Requiem: So this is what EDGE is... and this is who our "enemies" are... They never told me down there. Only now do I start to see...

Requiem: I understand a little more of this world now... I think I have something. A melody... Do you have my violin? I want to try... right now.

Requiem: That's strange... not enough blood? Where is it... hurry...

Requiem: I'm starting to... not hear so well...

Schorl-II: This containment facility was built for isolation. The Death Sage's influence cannot reach it. The power within that bracelet has already been dissolved. We can't have anyone discovering she's still alive, after all.

Schorl-II: Still, this is a valuable chance to observe. The Underground virtuoso's instrument is forming. Likely, this is their inherited power—not a physical object, but a projection of Mania consciousness.

**【Advance】**

- Enough! Get a doctor!

**end**

Schorl-II: Medical intervention will achieve little. Don't worry. When the moment comes that it must end, I will inform you. All preparations are in place. One rewind, and none of this will have happened.

Schorl-II: Remember every detail here. I want you to tell me everything when you go back.

${PlayerName}: You—

Requiem's hand settles gently against your chest. It is cold, yet her touch is impossibly soft.

Requiem: Your heart is racing... Even when I first met you, you were like this too... hurting for others, lifted high, shown a path, yet always charging ahead for someone else, throwing your own rhythm off...

Requiem: That's why I mistook you for her...

Requiem: I'm sorry. I see it now... You and her... are not the same.

Her unfocused eyes drift toward you, seeing nothing more than a hazy silhouette where you are.

Requiem: She saw only her "friends." Never once gave her "enemies" mercy. Never wavered. You... you're not like that at all.

Requiem: You can't even let go... someone like me...

Requiem: Thank you... That makes me happy...

Her fingers curl once, a faint and final squeeze. Then the strength leaves them, one breath at a time, and her hand slips slowly down the front of your coat.

**【Choose One】**

- Save her

  ${PlayerName}: ...EDGE. Please.

  Flawed Schorl: Copy that.

  **【Advance】**

  - I brought your melody...

  **end**

  ${PlayerName}: Let's go back together...

  Requiem: ...Okay...

- Save her?

  You know what she is. A virtuoso from the Underground, an enemy by every measure. Logic tells you to keep her alive; she is your lead into their world. But...

  You falter. She is broken, wounded, barely clinging to life. Maybe... she has already borne more than anyone should.

  Requiem: I hear it... the melody... I found it...

  Requiem: Please...

  You take her hand again.

  ${PlayerName}: Let go. You won't hurt anymore...

  Resonating Voice: Heheh... The so-called blood ties. One clean cut, and the satisfaction is immediate.

  Feeble Voice: ...This is good. This is the best possible ending.

  Once again, you reach the end of the road. This time, no violin music wraps around you. Your consciousness sinks and comes apart at the seams. The only thing left in your ears is silence.

  DEAD END

**end**

Hecate: Chief!

Your eyes snap open. 03:33 PM. You're back again. Hecate shakes you gently, using the shackles to anchor your scattered thoughts.

Nightingale: Chief, your Mania levels surged. Did you see the "Vision"?

Before you, the virtuoso sits upright in her chair, her eyes fixed on you with quiet concern.

Requiem: Are you all right?

## N11-9-C Shattered

03:34 PM. You are back at this moment again. Not long ago, you were drenched in another's blood, helpless as a life drained through your fingers.

Requiem: Are you all right?

Now she stands before you, whole and alive, her eyes searching yours. She stopped playing on her own, because she was worried about you.

Chief: I'm alright. Just remembered something. Feeling a little tired.

Requiem: Then would you like something gentler? Everyone says it brightens the mood.

Her question pulls you somewhere far away, as if you have slipped into a different world, one where her smile alone could make everything lighter.

But in your periphery, you spot several HUSH units already at the doorway. They are here for the violin, and soulless machines do not know how to handle anything gently.

...Better not leave it to them. You wave them off and stand. The violin is leaving either way. It might as well be your hands that take it.

**【Advance】**

- ...Let's call it here for today.

**end**

Chief: You already know that your music is more than just emotional resonance.

Chief: We can't go further today. Please hand me the violin.

She falls silent. After a long moment, she carefully places the violin in your hands.

Requiem: ...Will I see it again?

Chief: ...

**【Advance】**

- Let me think about it.

**end**

With that, you take the violin and leave the room.

The sleek, brand-new Schorl awaits you outside, wordless, as though it is weighing what it just witnessed.

You do not owe it an explanation.

Chief: I've gone through more rewinds than I can count. I've gathered enough intel to know where the Sage is. I'll handle the rest. There are still a few days. I need time to plan. When I'm ready, my Schorl will contact you.

Chief: And you said you wanted me to win S-125 over. I'll think about it. So—

You lift the violin in plain view, holding it back just as a HUSH unit steps forward to collect it.

**【Advance】**

- I'm keeping this. Any issue?

**end**

Schorl-II: None at all. If it serves the mission of eliminating the Underground's Sage, then I look forward to your next step.

## N11-10-C Message

Helix: ...What do you want?

Helix: Stay away. Don't come closer. The thief's shackles make me sick.

Unfazed, you drag a chair over and sit down across from her.

**【Advance】**

- Something happened to the virtuoso.

**end**

She lunges forward and seizes the bars.

Helix: What did you do?! If you want to hurt someone, hurt me! The virtuoso is already so fragile. Leave her alone!

Chief: She's okay. She's okay for now. I won't let her die.

...Nor do you want to see her hurt.

Chief: That's why I need to ask you something. If you really care about her, answer me. I want to understand her music. Is there any way she can play without triggering the Cursed Melody?

Helix: The virtuoso's music is...

"Every piece she plays is beautiful—no, sacred. It's a light that guides us, the brightest thing in the underground."

"How come you don't understand? You just have to listen a few times, and then you'll know."

"I knew it the first time I heard it months ago. Her music holds everything beautiful. It's the greatest gift the Divine ever gave us."

As if suddenly remembering your presence, she jolts out of her blissful daze and quickly shoots you a forced scowl.

Helix: Did you take her violin? Give it back, you devil. You'll be cursed for profaning sacred music.

- ...I hear you.

**end**

She falters, clearly taken aback, a flicker of genuine confusion crossing her face.

Helix: No... You got it all wrong...

Helix: It's not about her saving me. It's not... I never asked for it. She's the virtuoso, more important than I'll ever be. The Divine needs her. That's the only reason I want to protect her.

Helix: ...She shouldn't have come back... I didn't want her to... Everything I have should be offered to the Divine. But now because of me, the Divine lost Its virtuoso and Its music. Even the ritual was ruined...

Chief: If It does, then stay here in the MBCC. Find someone greater and worth more than a god. Honestly, I think your virtuoso is already a far better choice.

The girl stays curled in her corner, sealed inside her own little world. You cannot help but sigh again, and you set a few whirly lollipops down beside her.

There's no getting through to her today. Another time, then.

Eirene is unruffled as always. She does not greet you when you enter, only continuing her solitary chess game. When she notices your lingering gaze, she asks, lightly, what you think of her current position on the board.

You know exactly what she is really saying.

As a master who prefers to move her pieces from above, she is displeased with your dawdling, your hesitation, your wasted chances, and worst of all, your leniency toward Requiem. You have no interest in entertaining her critique. You turn and walk straight to Requiem.

Requiem's eyes brighten briefly when she sees you enter, but her gaze soon falls to what you are carrying. Not her violin, but a heavy, hermetically sealed containment case. The black triangle marked with II always brings these when it comes for her, asking for her "cooperation" in its experiments.

The light in her eyes fades. She gives a quiet, rueful smile and turns her face away, weary and resigned. She already knows this is not about music.

Chief: Sorry, it's not time for that yet.

Requiem: It's fine. I've known all along...

Requiem: You were just going along with it. You never really wanted me to play.

She looks down. Her tone remains flat, but this time, you sense a quiet trace of dejection beneath it.

Chief: I haven't decided yet. I have some questions first. Is that okay?

Requiem: Go ahead. As long as it doesn't hurt anyone, I'll answer.

Chief: You said I remind you of someone, and you've been waiting for her... Can you tell me who she is?

**【Advance】**

- Do you know her well?

**end**

Requiem: Hard to say. We never met.

She shakes her head, her gaze turning distant, suspended somewhere between remembering and yearning.

Requiem: But I've listened to so much of that music that I started seeing the memories of the virtuosos before me. And among them, she stood out the most. Always mentioned, always missed, always the most important friend. The more I listened, the more I wanted to meet her.

Requiem: I suppose that counts as knowing her well.

**【Advance】**

- Do I look like her?

**end**

<ins>Requiem: Not at all. She's a woman, for one thing. You two are completely different.</ins><br><ins>Requiem: Not at all. She has sharp features, and when she smiles, it's like this... You two are completely different.</ins>

Chief: Haha...

<ins>The misunderstanding is so absurd that you cannot help but let out a dry laugh. The tension finally breaks. Requiem waits until you have quieted, then speaks again, her voice gentle.</ins><br><ins>The face she makes is so unexpectedly funny that you cannot help but let out a dry laugh. Requiem: Too many ties... You both carry so much weight. Always being pulled, always steadfast, always hurting... You could let go and make things lighter, but you never would. That voice is rare. I can always recognize it.

After a long silence, you let out a sigh.

Chief: You don't need to thank me. If anything, I'm the one who kept you waiting too long.

**end**

You cut in.

Chief: I'm giving this to you because I don't want to see you hurt, or use that dangerous bracelet the Underground gave you. For playing, this one will do what you need. I also kept the weight and feel as close as I could so it won't feel too different.

Chief: I've found a venue too. The shielding is solid and the space is safe. I'll be the only one present during the performance. Until I'm sure your ability is stable, no one else will be exposed to the risk. If anything goes wrong, we stop immediately.

Chief: Those are my terms for giving you the violin and letting you play. Can you accept them?

Requiem listens in silence as you rattle off your lengthy explanation. When you finally pause and look to her for a response, she only asks you a gentle question in return.

Chief: Your power is dangerous, yes, but in the right hands, it could also save many lives.

Requiem: Would you like to hear it?

Requiem: That sounds lovely. When I listened to the old music, I used to dream of performing with other virtuosos.

Requiem: Or maybe... I was never playing alone.

Requiem: Every listener answers my melody in their own way. Each heart is a different instrument, and through this violin, they all sing.

The melody continues, weaving through your opened heart, carried by the shackles to everyone—

Helix is curled at the foot of her bed, wrapped in her blanket like a snail retreating into its shell. A faint pulse travels through the shackles. The dream has returned, the one that has been absent for so long.

You say goodbye to Requiem with a smile and step out of the small recital room, making your way back to the office.

## N12-1 Undercurrents of Pestilence

Fresh from the rewind, you cannot quite shake the feeling that you are still trapped inside that broken moment. Light pulses across your vision in bursts. Your lungs struggle for rhythm, and cold sweat crawls down your spine.

The sunlit greenhouse remains exactly as it was. Requiem stands before you, her eyes fixed on you with quiet concern.

Requiem: ...${PlayerName}, are you alright?

Chief: I...

Mr. E: The Sage couldn't bring herself to do it, so I did it for her. And I will keep doing so, until the true Shepherd returns... You know her. You've seen her. Tremble in fear...

Evelleon: Hehe... Stay a little longer... It won't be long now...

Evelleon: The one we've been waiting for... she's almost here...

Nausea crashes through you. You almost retch, your hands shaking violently. You lurch backward half a step, covering your mouth, and barely manage to stay upright. Requiem startles at the sight and moves closer without thinking.

Requiem: What happened?

Requiem: Come back, ▉▉▉. Protect them...

You snap your gaze up to Requiem. She is quiet, her eyes filled with nothing but open concern. She could not look more genuine.

But at the end of that last rewind, those were the same eyes that stared, unmoved, while everything fell into pieces.

- Who are you waiting for?

**end**

Requiem falters for a moment. The change in you came too fast for her to read, and though she clearly notices something is off, she simply gives a faint shake of her head.

Requiem: From the sound of your voices alone, I have a feeling you'll get along.

Chief: ...Get along? You and her?

Chief: ...I see.

You ask nothing else. As her hand moves toward you, you step aside, avoiding her touch. Without another word, you leave her standing there, lost and uncertain, and walk quickly away from the greenhouse.

You force your way through the fractured, blurring images still flickering behind your eyes. When you round the corridor, the joint operation team is already waiting, snapping to attention the moment they spot you.

Every scrap of intel has been gathered. The terminal displays the coordinates, the strategy, and the path forward. Your operatives are ready, eyes sharp with anticipation, needing nothing but your word to move in.

A flash of an image, and the answer leaves your mouth on its own.

- It's a trap. Tell everyone to pull back.

**end**

A hush drops over the operations room. Stunned, uncomprehending faces turn toward you. There is no time for explanations. You issue command after command in rapid succession.

Chief: Forget the operation. Move all equipment immediately. Evacuate every detainee in the area within four hours!

Everyone springs into action. They trust your judgment, executing your orders without hesitation even when the reasoning escapes them. You cannot afford to betray that trust.

Another wave of dizziness crashes over you. You force your expression to stay calm, fighting back the nausea, pushing your mind to its absolute limit.

*Remove the core equipment. Redraw the escort routes. Finish before they break through... How long do you have? What have you missed?*

Then the sensation crawls back, unbidden, of dodder vines tightening around your wrist. They're thin and freezing, burrowing through skin into muscle, drinking the life from their host, locking you in place.

Chief: ...Mm, everything's okay. It's safe here. We're still preparing a safer place for you.
