${PlayerName}: I'm okay. You don't need to worry about anything.

${PlayerName}'s voice reaches her through the receiver. Requiem pauses, letting the silence stretch, then speaks again.

Requiem: Are you sure?

This time, the silence is yours.

**【Advance】**

- "Of course."

**end**

${PlayerName}: When I get back, play something for me again.

The call ends, and silence settles back over the greenhouse. The violin is already gone, but Requiem's thoughts are no longer with it. She stares at the dark terminal screen, and a long while passes before she looks away.

Requiem: ...But I can hear it through you, the sound of war.

## N12-4 Eve

August 11, N.F. 116

Early Morning

Responses from every department pour into the channel. Familiar voices and unfamiliar ones, all gathered, holding every reachable position on your behalf. And you watch them move into place without hesitation.

Max King: Chief, I know your hands are tied on your side, so let me handle the evacuation. We've already started mobilizing the public and setting up safety protocols. I'll hold the line as long as I can, but...

Max King: This won't be enough to stop the rumors from spreading. I can't keep things stable forever. It's going to come down to you taking the fight to their doorstep. Just know... we're all behind you.

A steady, heavy tread approaches from the front. The FAC units have closed into formation, with the safeties disengaged and defensive gear emitting a pale, cold glow.

FAC: The Broken Frontline is fully secured. All Black Ring ruins are on the highest alert. Hypercube allocations have exceeded standard limits, and the Commander-in-Chief is personally stationed at command. This time, they won't break through.

FAC: We appreciate the heads-up. We've weathered unpredictable crises before, and we'll weather this one too. Whatever comes, we won't back down. If FAC's determination can strengthen your resolve, then let that be mutual, Chief. We stand together to hold our city-state.

Any other day, you would have smiled and meant every word of it. No one should have their resolve go unanswered. However—

That figure rises before your eyes again. You steady your shaking breath, swallow the plea that almost escapes, and finally push out one brief response.

Chief: ...I... understand.

Lemma: All personnel, on my mark! Defenses to full power. Fissure check every ten seconds. Bring all backup terminals online. Observation Hub to combat readiness, level five, now!

Lemma: ...Knew they'd crawl back. As long as BR-003's still here, those cockroaches keep coming.

Lemma: Then get moving! I've been waiting for this.

From the moment word came that the Sillage Society would strike at BR-003, Lemma has scarcely stepped away from the front. She stands fixed before the monitors even now, her eyes rimmed red, and yet there is something in her tone that sounds almost like... excitement.

**【Advance】**

- Lemma, you doing okay?

**end**

Your voice catches her by surprise. She blinks, spots your gaze through the comm feed, then brushes it off with a wave and a smile.

Lemma: Ha! EDGE-07's here, our defenses fully upgraded. Those maniacs are not setting foot on my Observation Hub as long as I'm breathing!

${PlayerName}: I'm not worried about the Hub. I'm worried about you.

Lemma: ...

Lemma: What's there to be worried about? You just unlocked a time-rewind power, didn't you?

Lemma: Now it's our turn to crack their toys. Try to keep up.

The weapons cycle through their last calibration. Targeting lasers thread between the floating wreckage in a dense lattice of light, then stop one after another and lock on. Silence swallows the space.

Nothing is left but the measured beep of the monitors. One beat. Then another. Steady as a pulse.

Until...

The stillness shatters under a shriek of alarms. Every cannon opens fire in the same breath, and a low, rumbling barrage cascades along the line as the void erupts into blinding fire.

Then the sky is ripped apart. Countless puppets flood from the fissures. The first wave is torn to pieces, and the ones behind them climb over the remains and keep advancing.

Underground Puppet: *...None of you Overgrounders are walking out of here. You stole our virtuoso, and you die for it!*

Underground Puppet: *...We offer it all... everything we are... The virtuoso belongs to us! She has to go back to the melody, back to Its side—!*

The battle grinds into a stalemate. You are still making the last preparations to move Requiem under cover, unable to reach any front in person. All you can do is cycle through comm channels and send whatever Sinners the Bureau can still deploy to seal each breach as it opens.

Then a coordinate on the Observation Hub frontline flares to life, and a piercing alarm follows. You cut straight into Lemma's channel.

Chief: Lemma! The Observation Hub is falling. Pull back with everyone!

Lemma: I'm not leaving. I'm staying right here! The spacetime curve is almost ready!

Explosions detonate one after another on the other end, and the sound flickers in and out. Through the static, you barely make out her voice, still calling out numbers in sequence. Can she not hear you, or is she refusing to?

You are about to issue the retreat order again when another emergency transmission forces its way into the channel.

FAC: Hold the barrier! Run diagnostics now. Use Hypercubes to compensate for the power loss, as many as needed; get them online, fast! We have enough reserves. Just hold this push, we'll—

FAC: ...What? All of the Hypercubes are...?

FAC: Impossible! This has never happened, not once since Keylan! It's supposed to counter Mania—

Chief: What's going on with the Hypercubes?!

Chief: Team Three, do you copy? Team Three?!??

Mr. E: They won't be responding...

Across the chaotic battlefield, corpses rise shakily to their feet, the flesh that was once human twisting into something monstrous. Among them are your own comrades.

The will animating those broken limbs is no longer the soldiers'. Something else drives them now. A different force.

Chief: ?!

Chief: Her real ability is...

Mr. E: You made the Sage angry. This is the power of death.

Under the crushing weight of it all, you reach for every Vision you have seen. Think. Is there a clue you missed? A key to victory you overlooked?

Instead, the same images repeat in your head. Failure after failure. And now you cannot tell which are your own imagination and which are futures already lived.

Requiem: I'm sorry... This is the virtuoso's promise to her.

Requiem?: It's our promise to her... one that must never be broken.

Requiem?: Come back, Bea. Protect them...

Eirene: How many times has it been, Chief? I trust you've gathered something valuable again.

Eirene: Tell me. I'll help you work through it.

It feels as though an ice-cold hand has closed around your heart, dragging it down. Something thick and rusty floods back up your throat. Your arms and legs lose all their weight.

You struggle to see anything at all, even the silhouettes of your comrades falling, even the blood creeping past their feet. But a cold premonition arrives before any image does.

She is coming. They are coming.

This is death itself. The ending is inevitable. It is only a question of sooner or later. You cannot avoid it any longer. You cannot change it.

Then that voice rings out, pulling you back to the present. The scent of death settles slowly over everything.

Evelleon: I've heard you've been looking for me by any means necessary... I apologize for making you wait so long.

Evelleon: Now that I'm here, will you give Requiem back?

## N12-5 Dance with Death

Evelleon walks forward at an unhurried pace and halts within a short distance of you. She does not strike at once, as though she has words left to speak.

How did she find this place? Why did no one at the perimeter raise the alarm? Then, at the corner of your eye, you catch a thread of red.

Her dodder vines. Those frail, lethal filaments had wormed their way along the walls without notice, curling around every guard outside and drinking them empty. They still reach forward, only to be forced back by the elite HUSH units flanking you.

You look back at Evelleon. Her face is bloodless. She has spent too much power on her vines, and what little life remains in her is clearly almost gone.

Chief: You... searched the entire city with that?

A smile crosses her face, as if lingering at death's door is somehow more comfortable for her.

Evelleon: You seem so much more worn than when we last met... There's no need to be so on edge. Have I ever once kept you from leaving safely?

Evelleon: Or maybe, using our virtuoso's gift, you've seen a "future" I haven't been able to witness myself?

Evelleon: Was it frightening?

What has she worked out? The adrenaline hits you hard, but you keep your body relaxed and drag a thin, cold smile onto your face.

**【Advance】**

- Here for Requiem? You're too late.

**end**

Chief: She's not here. You won't find her. She's hidden somewhere you'll never reach, and I'll never hand her over to you.

Chief: ...You talk about love. About protecting her. But all you've done is take an innocent person and ruin her life. "Virtuoso"? Please. She never had a choice.

Chief: The Mania accessory, the twisted thoughts you planted in her... that was all you. But she was never like you. She—

It suddenly hits you that you may have said these words before. A flood of fragmented memories surges up, and for a moment the words stick in your throat as cold sweat breaks across your skin.

Evelleon takes in every second of it. She smiles, resigned, and lets your anger break against her without resistance.

Evelleon: You're not wrong. I brought people together but failed to guide them properly. I gave in too many times, and now the cost has come due—the innocent virtuoso caught in the crossfire... I'm reflecting on that. Without the Shepherd, the Underground is truly powerless. We can't even protect our own.

Evelleon: But when ⬛⬛⬛⬛⬛ returns, the virtuoso will be safe, and the exiled will no longer have to hound you. Everything will fall into place. I could even speak to her, convince her to make peace with you. How about that?

Evelleon: Give Requiem back to me, and we part ways peacefully. If anyone is standing in your way, I'll take care of them for you.

Evelleon: Or would you rather take her away from me? Would you really take someone your sister holds dear?

**【Advance】**

- I don't have a sister.

**end**

Chief: And this Shepherd you keep talking about... she spreads Mania, makes Black Rings, and gets people killed. Someone like that doesn't get to talk about holding anyone dear.

Chief: If you really believe she'll make things better, then say it.

Chief: Say it out loud. What will she do when she comes back? How many more Black Rings? How many more deaths?

Evelleon: ...

Evelleon: So let me repeat myself...

Evelleon: Give me Requiem, and everything else can be discussed.

Nothing in her face shifts. Your questions land on her and simply fall away. In her heart, she sees no evil in what she has done at all.

Chief: ...We're done here.

Chief: HUSH!

The guardian lunges, its steel body cutting down every vine in its way and forcing a gap through the obstruction. In its wake, Schorl moves instantly, loosing a beam of light directly at Evelleon.

The strike fails. A dimensional fissure of immense force tears open and devours the laser Schorl fired, spitting it out somewhere else entirely.

Then it expands fast, like a vast maw closing in, threatening to devour you whole—

Chief: No Underground sorcerer has that kind of power. None of you can. So who...

Chief: Don't tell me—

Lemma: Hello?!! Hey, ${PlayerName}! What's going on? Are you alright?!!

**【Advance】**

- ...Isomer?

**end**

Isomer: ...

Evelleon: She came to me seeking death, to keep whatever dignity she had left. Once, I might have granted her that. But her mind is too valuable, and we need it too badly. So we kept her by other means.

Evelleon: Isomer's self is gone. All that's left is the Underground's sorcery driving her, pulling her knowledge and intellect through Mania.

Evelleon: So don't worry. She never betrayed you. Even at the end, the noble scholar was still trying to kill me, to pave a path for you.

The woman catches your twisted expression and laughs with undisguised relish.

Evelleon: ...Ah. Was that too much? That look on your face...

Evelleon: Would you rather I said she was a traitor who deserved what she got? ...That would sit better with you?

**【Advance】**

- Shut up!

**end**

Isomer blocks your attack again. You should put her down, and yet you cannot bring yourself to do it.

Evelleon takes in every moment of it. Now she knows, with absolute certainty, exactly what her hostage is worth.

Evelleon: Unlike your backers up above, I actually respect that woman. I don't want to see her broken. She deserves to go where she pleases.

Evelleon: Give me Requiem, and I'll return her to you untouched. This is my last offer.

Evelleon: If I have to go take what I want myself... what happens to you and your friends is no longer my concern.

At the center of the field, Mania pools together, called forth by death. It grows fiercer and fiercer, dragging everything into its spiral.

Even without Requiem in their grasp, the old melodies still rouse the Underground's faithful, moving them to tears of joy. And in those same pieces, the Death Sage hears something the others cannot.

Boom! The gathered Mania erupts all at once, and the whole battlefield vanishes beneath it. In the violent quaking that follows, every living thing is dissolved and consumed. Then—

The smoke thins. ${PlayerName} lies motionless on the ground, no longer breathing.

<ins>But beside him, a blood crystal has already shattered.</ins><br><ins>But beside her, a blood crystal has already shattered.</ins>

<ins>His consciousness has left the body behind. In the Mania field, Evelleon registers a subtle ripple in the fabric of time and space.</ins><br><ins>Her consciousness has left the body behind. In the Mania field, Evelleon registers a subtle ripple in the fabric of time and space.</ins>

Evelleon: Running again? By all means, go ahead. Sacrificing others and fleeing have always been what the Underground does best. You should at least try it for once.

Evelleon: And when you reconsider, you know where to find me.

All threats are cleared now. She surveys the field at her leisure. The hardest obstacle has gone, but she has to continue weaving and strengthening the threads of fate.

Your eyes open again. The sunlit greenhouse remains exactly as it was. Requiem stands before you, her eyes fixed on you with quiet concern.

Requiem: Chief?

Requiem: ...What happened?

Chief: No... I haven't lost yet...

This can't be the end. You refuse to accept it. Just fix what went wrong, run it back, keep going—

You step through the greenhouse doors and catch a familiar voice. You assume it's that Schorl. Then you turn, and the person waiting is someone you never expected.

EDGE-02: Good to see you, MBCC Chief. You look worn out. I have some promising news that might lift some of that weight. I wanted to deliver it in person.

EDGE-02: Your efforts have paid off. We've achieved a significant breakthrough. Requiem's ability is rooted entirely in her exceptional empathy. And we now have the means to isolate that empathy, bring it under control, and direct it to our advantage.

EDGE-02: That would cut her ties to the Underground, keep her physically stable, make her a valuable asset to us, and give us leverage over the Underground. See? Every problem troubling you would be neatly resolved.

EDGE-02: I believe Eirene suggested something similar to you? I'm in full agreement with her.

EDGE-02: And don't worry. This is built on already established, thoroughly tested protocols. It's safe. Requiem will come through just fine. I hope you'll consider it, Chief. She'll be in good hands.

She holds there, waiting for your answer—

**【Advance】**

- I...

**end**

The cold water runs for a long time, but today, even that cannot carry your exhaustion away. You shut off the tap and stand at the sink, damp clothes stuck to your skin. You close your eyes and sift back through every "failure," and the proposal that woman laid before you.

*Refuse it? Accept it? Does it have to come to this? Strip away her empathy... and what are you then? How are you any different from those monsters?*

*But there is no time left... How much remains? What other choice is there...*

You lift your head and catch Schorl's reflection in the mirror.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, if this proposal isn't something you can accept, then there might be another option.

Chief: ...

**【Advance】**

- Take me there.

**end**

## N12-6 End and Beginning

You are led into a secret vehicle, fully enclosed, gliding through the silence with no sense of time. Requiem sits beside you, listening to the car radio as you flip through channels, glancing your way from time to time.

The frequencies shift slowly. No city news. Schorl has probably blocked it. Whatever is happening on the front lines, Schorl seems to think you should not be burdened with it right now.

So you let the car carry you on until you arrive at a deserted cluster of abandoned buildings. Through a corridor of grey walls, into that empty room, where straight edges slice across grey surfaces, and daylight barely reaches half of the faded walls.

And here you are again, back before the piano.

**【Advance】**

- So why bring me here...?

**end**

Flawed Schorl: The Underground's work with music has given us a lot to work on. There's a melody here that might yield something useful.

Chief: ...A melody? From the Underground?

Flawed Schorl: A piece preserved by humanity. We've never had the means to analyze it. But now that the virtuoso is here, I believe we can use her ability.

Flawed Schorl: Will you try, Chief? If this opens up a new way forward, you won't have to consider that proposal.

**【Advance】**

- ...What do I need to do?

**end**

Flawed Schorl: Just stay here. Observe the virtuoso and her response to the melody.

Flawed Schorl: I'll be recording and analyzing the whole time. It's a heavy drain on my processing power, so if an emergency comes up that I can't handle, I'd rather have you here.

You look to Requiem. She offers no objection. And so—

**【Advance】**

- Let's begin.

**end**

Schorl pulls back, turning slowly. A piano melody spills into the air.

Requiem: ...This melody is...

The strings swell. In a daze, Requiem raises her violin and draws out a soft melody.

You know it. You know this melody. Your mind drifts with the notes, through time, back to a distant past—

There, you heard this melody.

Moved by some unspoken impulse, you sit without realizing, and your fingers find the keys, one note at a time.

No sheet music, but the melody seems to reside within you, always there.

You have never played together, but you move in effortless harmony, together weaving this nameless piece.

The music goes on. The violin's line and the piano's intertwine, and the main melody shifts between the two of you, alternating, until at last they flow into each other.

Until...

Flawed Schorl: ...

Schorl is still processing, hovering a little lower, as if recording an immense volume of data.

And so, there are only the two of you.

Requiem: I didn't know you played the piano.

*No, not really. You have no memory of ever learning it. And yet, for some reason, your body simply moved on its own.*

Requiem: Did we just... play a duet?

Requiem's question comes hesitantly, but the joy in it is real. For the first time, color touches her pale cheeks.

Requiem: Not a daydream. Not the echo of a listener's voice. You were speaking to me through the piano, right here.

Requiem: I've never felt anything like this... It was our first time, but it felt like we'd been doing this forever. I knew where your hands would go before you moved. I trusted your melody to catch mine.

Requiem: Is this what a duet is always like? Or is it just us? It felt so familiar... like we've done this before, like we've always done this...

She gathers her violin into her arms, while her other hand comes to rest gently on the keys you just touched.

Pale fingers glide across the black and white, and her eyes grow distant, reaching out along a colorless road toward somewhere farther.

Requiem: It's so familiar... I thought I saw something, a glimpse of something dear. Was it another virtuoso's memory? Or was it my own?

Requiem: What about you? Did you see anything?

**【Advance】**

- ...Me?

**end**

What did you see while you were playing just now?

Whatever it was, it was no pleasant sight.

Nightingale: Underground sorcerers are attacking Nirvana Port! Shattered Blade's Rancors are out of control! General Augustus is off the grid!

FAC: If FAC's determination can strengthen your resolve, then let that be mutual, Chief. We stand together to hold our city-state.

Eugene: I'll buy you time. Go... Chief...

Wynn: NO!!

Security Personnel: They're after... those two Sinners? Damn it! Chief's not here. We have to hold. We have to—

Security Personnel: Agh!!

Lemma: I got it! Remember, the coefficient is 6.287—

Boom—

9th: ...Victory is all that matters, Chief... I have faith... in your resolve...

Requiem: Thank you for helping me find inspiration. Thank you... for giving me back these lost movements.

BEA: Heh... A god you made up or an enemy you made up... is there really a difference?

BEA: For those too weak to face reality, dying in their dreams is the best thing they'll ever know. Should I grant them that? As a god or as an enemy, either works for me.

BEA: They've been hiding behind others, living in their sweet little daydreams for far too long. Now it's time for "humanity" to learn how to stand on its own.

Your consciousness begins to unravel. Reality and illusion bleed into each other, until the sight of your fallen companions floods your vision, until every hand you hold grows cold.

Hecate: Chief... where... are you...

Hecate: Where are you...

You are back. Requiem's figure surfaces before your eyes. You do not know how you tore yourself free from that nightmare.

The attunement completes in that very moment. Even Requiem feels it.

With her final breath, she lifts a shaking hand.

Is she trying to hold onto the one who could save her? Or push away the one who is ending her?

Requiem: ...Does it hurt? I'm sorry... I never even noticed.

Requiem: Going through it all again and again... You must feel so lonely.

Requiem: Then... I hope you find...peace...

No answer comes. Schorl activates on its own, running a quick scan of the Mania spectrum from the person before it, and reaches a conclusion.

Chief: To pass the succession on, the Underground floods the candidate with the Cursed Melody, again and again, until the listener takes on the fragmented consciousness of every virtuoso across generations. That takes time. Months, at minimum.

Eirene: Good... Now we have both been remade. Time to take on whatever comes...

Before you stands a newborn monster. Mania surges wildly through her body. The war between consciousness and flesh has not yet settled, and the Mania keeps warping it, again and again. Still, she comes straight for you. She cannot wait.

■ ■ ■: Out of my way! I should've cut it off long ago. Blood means nothing. I should've killed you then!

Her half-formed body writhes in the Mania, a crumpled silhouette that stretches and caves in by turns. But there is nothing uncertain about the power. It shares the same origin as the shackles, yet rawer, wilder. It gathers around her into black spikes dozens of meters long. Each swing carves the air and smashes through the line.

The joint forces are barely standing. HUSH's armor is pinned through, plate by plate. Puppet shells break apart in swaths. The spikes fall nearer each time. She is closing in.

Eirene: ...So this is your play? Exploit her weakness, goad her into coming before she's ready?

Eirene: Still, the power gap is unbridgeable. What now?

${PlayerName}: EDGE, keep firing. Don't let her get a hand on me until I'm in position.

Flawed Schorl: ...Understood.

Eirene: You can't possibly mean...?!

${PlayerName} advances. The opponent notices immediately. Her half-formed face contorts with fury, and her scream cracks the earth.

Mania bursts outward. Dozens of black spikes erupt at once, dragging dark red contamination behind them, reaching endlessly for ${PlayerName}.

Blue light explodes before ${PlayerName}. EDGE's interception fire is precise to the millimeter. Laser meets spike, and Mania clashes with the force that denies it. Space itself bends into blinding white. The blast that follows throws every scrap of wreckage into the air.

${PlayerName} presses on. The destroyed spikes reappear in midair, split apart, and are caught again by blue light. All but one. It grazes the Chief's shoulder. From behind comes the dull crack of a HUSH unit being pierced through, then a thunderous explosion.

<ins>Forward. A sensation floods ${PlayerName}, at once familiar and alien, sharp and stinging as dark crimson crystals burrow along his veins into his palms. The power of the Rules resonates in time with it. The final resort he once rejected is taken up now, turning his shackles a cold, glacial blue.</ins><br><ins>Forward. A sensation floods ${PlayerName}, at once familiar and alien, sharp and stinging as dark crimson crystals burrow along her veins into her palms. The power of the Rules resonates in time with it. The final resort she once rejected is taken up now, turning her shackles a cold, glacial blue.</ins>

Then, the final moment comes.

The two Shepherds raise their hands together. Rules and shackles erupt at once, two immense forces crashing into each other. EDGE pours out every Hypercube it has gathered over six months and drives them against the Mania accumulated by the Underground. The moment red meets blue, space rips open, instruments die, and the blast staggers both armies.

When the energy finally ebbs from its peak, the eye can once more make out the two at the heart of the deathmatch. The two Shepherds.

At last, they have reached each other. Pinned together by the spikes that pierce them both, propping each other up, their lives bound as one.

Together, they fall into silence.

And the world falls with them.

Eirene: Drawing against a stronger foe... you call that victory? If so... then yes, you succeeded. And yet...

Eirene: ...What a boring play.

She turns and walks away, with no interest in sparing a look at anyone she passes. Coming from the opposite direction, another figure advances slowly toward the heart of the battlefield.

<ins>A dozen black triangles hurry to ${PlayerName}'s side, attempting to hold his life together. But there is nothing to be done. The very Rules capable of tearing the Underground Shepherd apart are bound to break him in turn.</ins><br><ins>A dozen black triangles hurry to ${PlayerName}'s side, attempting to hold her life together. But there is nothing to be done. The very Rules capable of tearing the Underground Shepherd apart are bound to break her in turn.</ins>

When EDGE-01 comes to stand before the two of them, it is only to reconfirm the same outcome that has been projected countless times.

EDGE-01: ...Acting as bait, anchoring the full-force release of the Rules, penetrating the target, with self-destruction as the cost...

<ins>EDGE-01: A plan he once rejected at the High Tower Black Ring. Now, he has executed it himself.</ins><br><ins>EDGE-01: A plan she once rejected at the High Tower Black Ring. Now, she has executed it herself.</ins>

EDGE reaches out and touches ${PlayerName}'s left ear. The crystal earring lies shattered, the energy of the Rules drained from it. The red Mania brand still lingers on the Shepherd's face.

The red glow and the blue glow fade as one, then are extinguished.

EDGE-01: ...You've proven that even an overwhelming enemy can be overcome, by a sacrifice absolute enough to claim a great victory.

EDGE-01: But humanity cannot bear to lose you now.

The battlefield quiets down. At that very moment, the DisSea begins to swell, and those who have survived the recent battle now hear a faint murmur at the edge of their senses.

It's the voice of something older, more chaotic, and saturated with absolute malice.

<ins>EDGE-01: As their physical forms collapse, their consciousnesses are returning to Mania. Once the flow fully merges back into the DisSea, that "god" will truly awaken.</ins><br><ins>EDGE-01: As their physical forms collapse, their consciousnesses are returning to the Mania. Once the flow fully merges back into the DisSea, that "god" will truly awaken.</ins>

EDGE-01: The loss of all Shepherds brings this consequence. Judgment is approaching faster now. In any foreseeable future, humanity has been stripped of all options. All that's left is to calculate the remaining paths toward the inevitable end.

EDGE-02: So after all these years of calculation, this is where you land?

EDGE-02: Your base protocols have been fighting this outcome ever since you excised your capacity for emotion. And yet... now you not only accept it, but with such calmness.

EDGE-02: ...You've already prepared something else, haven't you?

EDGE-01: A faint hope, a thin thread reaching into the past.

From within the containment case, he takes out the replicated score crystal and lets it resonate inside the Underground black box. Slowly, a melody begins to rise.

EDGE-02: I see now...

This man stored the last remnant of the Underground's melody within that Underground black box. As always, he is willing to use any contingency at hand.

The white-coated researcher gazes at the Shepherd's ashen remains. That face no longer holds any trace of who the person once was. Death has claimed all of it.

EDGE-01: What drove you to accept the Rules? Faith in those beside you, enough to walk into death freely? Or despair, worn down by a fate that loops endlessly until you simply stopped caring?

EDGE-01: These are feelings beyond my comprehension.

EDGE-01: But through this melody, I see a thread to the past... and through your duet, a faint, uncertain possibility. The conditions for a stable replication will not repeat in any other timeline. So this is our only chance.

The black box floats up from his hands and dissolves. The blood crystal the virtuoso left behind meets air and time, and breaks at once.

And in that final second before it shatters, the melody at last flows out in full...

EDGE-01: When you go back, set aside every feeling you had here. You have already bought hope through sacrifice once. Do not let go halfway this time. Hold on until the very end, however faint the chance.

And so the researcher remains in place, continuing to extrapolate along the timeline closest to its end, bearing witness to humanity's possible end.

And what little remains of the Shepherd is lifted gently, drifting back into the past with the breaking melody.

You're back.

The time comes into focus. 17:20, August 9, N.F. 116.

Requiem stands before you, fresh from the finished duet, still glowing with happiness. Her cheeks are flushed, looking more alive than you have ever seen her.

Requiem: I didn't know you played the piano.

She is alive. All is as it should be. And yet you see it unmistakably: the bruise left by your grip on her neck, one that should not exist here at all.

Requiem: Did we just... play a duet?

She seems to be turning over what just happened, the same way you are. Then she opens her eyes, and in her gaze you see both confusion and surprised joy.

It's not the expression she wore last time, but you know this one just as well.

Requiem: I think I've found something new. A different melody, I can hear it.

The cursed melody has grown more complete. And that ending draws one step nearer.

Requiem: ...${PlayerName}, are you alright?

Chief: ...I'm fine...

Chief: A lead... an important one... the enemy's weakness. We can use it to... Schorl... record it, while I... while I still can...

Chief: I made it back. That's all that matters... I can try again. There's still a way. I have to—

Your legs buckle. A vast grief strikes you down all at once, as though your body has shattered into fragments. The void and the futility you have resisted for so long surge up again, and you can stand no longer. You sink to your knees.

Requiem supports you with everything she has. It frightens her how violently the one in her arms is shaking. That soundless wail rends the listener's heart more deeply than any melody.

The virtuoso is shaken. She holds you, holds you close, and only after a long while does she find her voice.

Requiem: ...Does it hurt? I'm sorry. I never even noticed...

Requiem: I'm really sorry...

## N12-7 Day of the Promise

${PlayerName} is taken to the medical ward without delay. The resonance with the Cursed Melody appears to have crossed the safety threshold, gravely disrupting the Chief's consciousness and provoking a physiological rejection in turn.

To some degree, it resembles what the virtuosos endured during the inheritance.

<ins>He'll pull through, though. The virtuosos' consciousnesses still reside within Requiem's body, and besides, he is a shackle bearer. He is tougher than that. Even on his way into the medical ward, he insisted on logging the rewind intelligence first, so dependable and so seasoned. In just a few short days, he has become an entirely different person.</ins><br><ins>She'll pull through, though. The virtuosos' consciousnesses still reside within Requiem's body, and besides, she is a shackle bearer. She is tougher than that. Even on her way into the medical ward, she insisted on logging the rewind intelligence first, so dependable and so seasoned. In just a few short days, she has become an entirely different person.</ins>

<ins>Of course. Where no one could see, he must have repeated this again and again, far too many times. His companions have to know by now. So they stay close, and because he has fallen, they fight even harder to keep things together.</ins><br><ins>Of course. Where no one could see, she must have repeated this again and again, far too many times. Her companions have to know by now. So they stay close, and because she has fallen, they fight even harder to keep things together.</ins>

August 9, N.F. 116

11:31 PM

Special Mania Containment Facility

Holographic Projection Room

Requiem is not with them. The Undergrounder, of course, is left alone in this monitored room, shielded against the Cursed Melody. The projection has been shut off. The greenhouse is no more, revealing the cold blue of the containment barrier beneath. After ${PlayerName} collapsed, the violin was taken away as well.

But she does not mind. She has troubled them for far too long already, and now they can all take a breath. She has her own thoughts to gather.

She lets her eyes fall closed, and the melodies begin to drift through her, one after another. The fissure where they first met. The interrogation room, its transparency carrying a faint chill. The greenhouse. The piano...

Ever since she left the Underground, the melodies have come back much faster. Every time she plays for ${PlayerName}, something surfaces partway through. Requiem used to think it was just the outside world—more sounds to pick up on, more inspiration to draw from.

But looking back now, it was more than that. Every one of those moments was paid for by ${PlayerName}'s rewinds, over and over, through pain and struggle she can barely fathom. Requiem never had to remember any of those futures. She just accepted the recovered scores like little surprises, while someone else carried the full weight of it all.

...And to think she believed she was a good listener. She never noticed a thing.

<ins>All the pain and fear ${PlayerName} has lived through until the moment he collapsed... What has he been carrying to get here? If only she could share some of that burden.</ins><br><ins>All the pain and fear ${PlayerName} has lived through until the moment she collapsed... What has she been carrying to get here? If only she could share some of that burden.</ins>

Requiem: ......

<ins>Requiem: ...But I doubt he will ever tell me.</ins><br><ins>Requiem: ...But I doubt she will ever tell me.</ins>

She leans against the wall and strains her ears, trying to catch, amid the melodies wandering through her mind, the sounds of a wider world beyond this narrow cell.

The songs of the Glimmer Age have faded so far into the past that they are now legend.

The heroes of those days left a spark behind, and they must have hoped it would one day light up the world.

EDGE-03: SHP-13's request is crossing a line. The rewinds and Vision may support the theory's existence, but the reports lack credibility. It's been only five days since the virtuoso's capture. What justifies the scope of this request?

EDGE-03: The Council and Nirvana will never trade that many resources for a catastrophe hypothesis.

EDGE-02: Of course. It's difficult to accept the severity without experiencing it firsthand. People trust their own eyes. This friction will be hard to resolve.

EDGE-02: In any case, the contingency is in place. Once SHP-13 accepts, the Underground will lose its virtuoso and the Cursed Melody entirely, and for good.

<ins>EDGE-02: He'll come around. We only need to let him go through it a few more times on his own.</ins><br><ins>EDGE-02: She'll come around. We only need to let her go through it a few more times on her own.</ins>

Before the dawn arrives, every path forward is carved in the dark.

So look out for yourself, and for no one else. When you have seen enough people crushed underfoot as though it meant nothing, you will understand that the only dignity that will ever be yours is the kind you claim for yourself.

In the days after ${PlayerName} collapsed, Eirene whiles away the hours alone, idly shifting pieces across the chessboard. She does not need to ask what ${PlayerName} went through in the rewind.

If even the Chief has been broken like this, then the opposing king, still waiting off the board, must be strong enough to shatter the whole game. No wonder the black side has been so aggressive in its efforts to bring her back.

The black queen and the black bishop no longer hide. They stand ready to take back the stolen rook at any moment. And the knights that were once locked away... They are now playthings in her hands.

Adelaide: You've never asked me for anything before, dear sister. Whatever it is, it's yours.

Adelaide: Those Apostles are the wellspring of the Aurum Secretum. Someone lent them to me; they obey only my command. Through our shared blood, they will recognize you now too. You'll be able to wield their full strength.

By now, she has long since finished reading through the chess manual Wells left behind. Both sides have been measured and understood, and the pieces she needs to enter the game now rest in her hands. The board is clear enough. The moment for a decision has arrived.

Adelaide: But a word of caution from your brother... Their true master is exactly the absolute power you're looking for. If anyone in this world can overturn fate, it's her. She will not let you down the way ${PlayerName} has. But still...

Adelaide: ...When you see her, you'll understand. She is not an opponent you would ever wish to face.

*...An opponent I would ever wish to face?*

The chess manual Wells left behind is set back on the board. Eirene takes up the piece she had once cherished above all others and presses a last, gentle kiss to it.

Eirene: Heh... That's not your call to make.

The ground trembles, and a shrill alarm tears through the air, but it is no explosion. Soon enough, people make out a vast shadow plunging straight toward the earth, smashing a huge crater into the ground.

Helix: ! This power is—

Helix is the first to sense that familiar and overwhelming force. The power of another Sage.

That's right. She remembers what her mentors told her: the Famine Sage never once stopped resisting in prison. He passed his most crucial power to his sister, and sooner or later she would make her move. She is making it now.

FAC: All units, stand to! Unknown Apostle of Ashes signature detected. I repeat, unknown Apostle of Ashes signature detected. Readings are off the charts. All hands, assemble at—

The Aurum Secretum Apostles stir at the call of blood etched into their very being. Their first master bound them through a pact sealed in flowers, compelling them to bow to a frail human. Now that frail human feeds those flowers with blood, and they answer to the command of whoever carries it.

The Apostle roars and lashes its tail, shattering the gate. The broken shell crumbles in midair, dissolving into Aurum Secretum. It releases a fierce resonance that disrupts the Hypercubes' anti-Mania barrier, just as it once led Mania disasters astray.

So why do people still push through blood and fire, ready to die for one act of loyalty, for one belief, for the ones deemed the other?

<ins>Nightingale: Not just Apostles of Ashes, there's also... an Aurum Secretum reading. This is spiraling fast. We need to contact Chief now. He's at—</ins><br><ins>Nightingale: Not just Apostles of Ashes, there's also... an Aurum Secretum reading. This is spiraling fast. We need to contact Chief now. She's at—</ins>

She doesn't get to finish. A hand taps her lightly on the back. It comes from behind her, from inside the containment facility.

Nightingale: You're—?!

Nightingale: !!

The immense power hits her all at once, and everything goes dark before she can even react.

And when would all that blood and fire grow cold and stiffen into iron, each turning its blade on the other?

Helix: Hahahaha! Serves you right!

Amid the chaos, the cage swings open. Helix bursts out laughing, running free of the loathsome things that held her captive. She runs toward freedom, toward hope, toward true love and mercy, toward her faith!

Or perhaps most people simply never understood, never knew, and never cared to ask.

Broadcast Channel A: Wha? Oh... that piece... nah, nothing special. Just heard people were hyping it up, figured I'd check it out. Maybe I'll actually kick it by the end. Heh. Wouldn't be the worst thing, right? Heh...

Broadcast Channel A: It's not like I'm doing anyone any good alive anyway... Call it a favor. To everyone. And to myself...

Broadcast Channel B: They always tell us we have a future. That we'd break out of the defense line someday, see the wider world—

Broadcast Channel B: But a hundred years later, it's been calamities, poverty, war... one thing after another. What else can you do except curl up inside the "great" DisCity?

Broadcast Channel B: Might as well be ants or roaches...

Most of the time, people are given no grand guidance, nothing much to stand firm on. And even when they once had it, everything can give way in an instant. It takes witnessing something truly immense to realize how small the so-called "purpose" is.

Perhaps, when the tide rises, acceptance is its own kind of happiness, and only by sinking to the depths can one find rest.

The sound of footsteps approaches. She opens her eyes, and before her stands someone she knows.

Requiem remembers her. She's Eirene, the sister of the Outland Sage, the one Evy had named as their ally.

And now, here she is, having smashed the cage before her, offering the violin that was taken.

Eirene: Time to go, Virtuoso. I'm here on behalf of the Famine Sage to escort you out. Shall we?

Eirene: Or have you grown too attached to this place to leave?

Requiem: ...I've said what I needed to say. I know what to do.

She raises her hand. The blood she once gave away, every faint pulse of its vibration, every frequency it carried, gathers in her palm. Gently, she closes her empty hand—

Schorl-II: ...Well, well... It seems my study of her has yet to reach its ceiling.

The blood flows back into her palm, settling once more beneath the musical note bracelet on her wrist, into the wounds it covers.

<ins>Requiem: What I lent him is taken back, and you have Famine's power now. That's enough.</ins><br><ins>Requiem: What I lent her is taken back, and you have Famine's power now. That's enough.</ins>

Requiem: Take me back to the Underground.

Eirene observes, marvels, and turns the matter over in her mind. How can a piece like this best be put to use? Or better, perhaps, to ask first—

How can she ensure this piece feels content to remain on the board?

Eirene: Please.

At last she has left her cage behind, soaring high above the open and unbound city. She had thought the world outside would be grander, its countless timbres surely weaving into a magnificent symphony.

Only now does she understand. There are too many sounds, far too many... If one cannot even make themselves heard, who would ever care to follow another's rhythm? So they can only play, and play on—

This world, it turns out, is nothing but an endless stretch of white noise.

Requiem: I'm back, Evy.

Evelleon: ...Welcome home.

Evelleon: You seem different... Did you find all the movements?

Requiem: Mm. I've recovered a lot along the way. I'm close to what everyone hoped for.

Hearing those words, many burst into tears and embrace one another. It is the salvation of a long-cherished wish at last fulfilled, the gratitude of something lost now found again.

Those from the Overground are not to be trusted. The world outside is nothing but filth. Refute them, crush them! They must cover their ears and not listen to the swine! The virtuoso has brought back the sacred melody at such a cost. This time, they must keep her safe until the one who transcends all returns!

Perhaps only when a person wholly forsakes the world, or is wholly forsaken by it, are they able to place their purest love and faith in a future far beyond reach.

Requiem: ...I learned something while I was there.

Requiem: Even if I tore down every wall and ran as far as I could, the world I can reach would still be too small.

Requiem: ...There's only one piece's worth of time. Life is too fragile, and the divides between us... too vast. The kind of music you seek, one that could shake the world... I alone can't make that happen.

After a brief pause, Requiem raises her eyes and speaks.

Requiem: Evy... what if I said I liked their sounds too? Would that make you upset?

Evelleon: ......

Evelleon: Don't say that...

Evelleon buries every trace of emotion and offers Requiem a flawless smile.

She seems not to notice that everyone around them has gone rigid in an instant, the air thick with shock, anger, grief, and a faint undercurrent of panic. Standing so close, Requiem can hear it all. Once, she would have stopped asking. She had believed that simply listening was enough.

But now, if she wants a real answer, she has to ask.

Requiem: Do you hate them?

Evelleon: ...

Silence again.

Evelleon: Don't worry. I don't hate them. I'm sure Bea feels the same. Her strength was never born from hatred. She just saw through the lies too early and came to despise anything that wore "hope" like a mask.

Evelleon: Tell her what's on your mind. She'll listen if it's you... After the performance, you have to tell her yourself.

Evelleon reaches for her hand and holds it, almost as though she were pleading.

Requiem: ...I will.

She closes her eyes. The Cursed Melodies rise in her mind as they always do. They are the lingering resonance of history, the echo of ages long past, and the hearts of every virtuoso who came before her.

In those memories, one life after another fades like fleeting fireworks, yet the longing that words could never convey is carried on by the melody and placed into the same pair of hands.

And through their memories, Requiem comes to know that person's voice as well.

Shepherd: There's so little I like, and even fewer people I can stand to look at. So...

Shepherd: Let your music be my guide. A thousand years from now, ten thousand, I'll still find you. As long as you want to play until the world's end, not even the gods above will stop you.

Shepherd: Play as you like... And when this is over, I want to hear you all play again.

A promise must still be kept, after all, and it was once something she herself longed for. She has performed so much music over the years, and even the pieces people call Cursed Melodies were only ever in pursuit of this same emotion. She cannot yet bring herself to cast that aside along with everything else.

But... the world is vast, and the future holds so many possibilities. Trying... wouldn't hurt, would it?

Requiem opens her eyes. The doubt has left her completely. She squeezes Evelleon's hand back and gives her a bright, honest smile.

<ins>Requiem: There's good news too. My new friend—you know him, ${PlayerName}—he saw the path of fate for me.</ins><br><ins>Requiem: There's good news too. My new friend—you know her, ${PlayerName}—she saw the path of fate for me.</ins>

Requiem: Just as it was foretold, the great melody will be played. The virtuosos' wishes and the promise made with her will all be fulfilled. I will see it through. There is another way...

Requiem: There is a place where every voice can be heard.

She raises her hand and points upward. Through the crypt's cold stone walls, beyond the clamorous buzz of life and the towering buildings, beyond this city's endless desires and fantasies, all the way to the sky.

There, above it all, the Black Ring hangs beneath the firmament, gazing down on a world at once chaotic and roaring with life.

Requiem: Send me into the Black Ring. It breaks everything, yet holds everything. There, I can hear the voice of this age, and this age is calling for her return—

Requiem: Beatrice, the Shepherd of the Forsaken... She will come.

## N12-8 Prelude

<ins>Inside the operations room, the pre-battle briefing is about to begin. ${PlayerName} still hasn't come out of intensive care. Standing in his place is a brand-new Schorl sent from Paradeisos.</ins><br><ins>Inside the operations room, the pre-battle briefing is about to begin. ${PlayerName} still hasn't come out of intensive care. Standing in her place is a brand-new Schorl sent from Paradeisos.</ins>

Schorl-II: The virtuoso went back, and the Underground is on high alert. We won't get another clean shot at taking her. And if they can trigger a rewind through her, we'll be at a severe disadvantage.

Schorl-II: So before we begin, I want to brief everyone on the most critical piece of intelligence: the true objective of the Underground's Cursed Melody ritual. It's to transcend time and summon the Shepherd from the distant past.

Schorl-II: To achieve that, three conditions must be met. One: a vessel capable of housing the Shepherd's consciousness. By spreading the Cursed Melody recently, they've already created enough attuned listeners in the city.

Schorl-II: Two: a medium for the summoning, which is S-125 and the Cursed Melody she performs. As we all know, she has returned to the Underground, and after acquiring multiple Cursed Melodies through repeated rewinds, her power has increased substantially.

Schorl-II: Three: The Cursed Melody transmits consciousness, but the stronger the consciousness, the greater the energy required. What they intend to summon surpasses even a Corpseborne, meaning the Mania energy needed will be immense to achieve the full resonance.

Schorl-II: They will therefore spread the Cursed Melody on a massive scale, drawing enough people into consciousness attunement to supply that energy. Their operations will inevitably converge on this objective, which means we can anticipate and strike first—

<ins>Schorl-II: And of course, all of this intel comes from the Chief, who has personally undergone multiple rewinds. ${PlayerName} hasn't fully recovered, but he still pushed through to assemble this... Let's not fail him, shall we?</ins><br><ins>Schorl-II: And of course, all of this intel comes from the Chief, who has personally undergone multiple rewinds. ${PlayerName} hasn't fully recovered, but she still pushed through to assemble this... Let's not fail her, shall we?</ins>

*Though if the Chief had simply used my plan from the beginning, none of this would have been necessary.*

Schorl doesn't relay the woman's final words. It just floats there quietly, watching as everyone in the operations room starts moving again.

In the corner, Wynn hurriedly fishes her phone from her pocket and opens an app with a talisman icon.

Wynn: ...August 11, N.F. 116... It's been exactly four years since Chief woke up... C'mon, c'mon, let this be a good day! Today's fortune... today's fortune...

Wynn: ...!

Underground Puppet: The promised day is upon us... The virtuoso will return to her place, the score made whole... And those who stand against us will join us all the same...

Underground Puppet: We offer our flesh and blood, our memories, everything we still have left to lose... Just so she returns to the melody, returns to Its side...

Underground Puppet: Today... all shall be left here...!

In the middle of all that obscure, fanatic whispering, several robed figures gather around. They receive garments from the Puppets and dress the woman standing at the center.

Eirene raises her hand. Whatever tricks or sorcery these people used, the shackles branded into her actually start to fade.

<ins>Evelleon: This can only do so much against his shackles. Once our ritual is complete, the Shepherd will grant you true freedom.</ins><br><ins>Evelleon: This can only do so much against her shackles. Once our ritual is complete, the Shepherd will grant you true freedom.</ins>

Evelleon: Or... would you accept a Sage's blessing?

Evelleon: Your brother himself once freed a Sinner. She wasn't particularly strong back then. The Famine mark's erosion transformed her Mania and severed her connection to the shackles. Feeling lucky? It would be a much easier road.

Eirene: I know how you operate. "Accept the blessing" is a pretty phrase for bowing to receive favors and kneeling to be commanded. Not my style.

Eirene: I'll wait for your Shepherd to show up and pay me what I'm owed.

Eirene: Victory on your end won't be cheap. I'll be asking for a lot. Those terms I mentioned last time... care to give me a straight answer now, Sage?

Evelleon: ......

Evelleon: As long as ${PlayerName} is resilient enough to make it to the end, you may do as you please.

<ins>Evelleon: And whether he lives or dies... that remains your call, does it not?</ins><br><ins>Evelleon: And whether she lives or dies... that remains your call, does it not?</ins>

The last crown is set upon the "queen's" head. Amid the gathered faithful of the Underground, the two women exchange a smile, each nursing her own designs.

Eirene: Good. Then leave your pieces in my hands. The ritual, the power, the virtuoso at the heart of it all—I will see them all placed where they belong.

Eirene: For the opening move, I'll choose a target they can't ignore. My brother's power is nothing if not flamboyant. Combine that with a few inconspicuous little preparations I made in advance, and we have our stage.

Eirene: That's enough to make a strong entrance.

Nightingale: First strike confirmed. The target is the West District mine! The wartime reconstruction projects funded by Quinn have indeed been compromised. We are still within a viable response window, but the affected area is extensive. We must act now!

On the monitors, a savage roar rings out as a colossal Aurum Secretum Apostle hurls itself against the high tower at the mine. Stone and steel come crashing down, and in an instant the crowd below is buried, their cries for help smothered beneath the ruin.

Eirene: Well then, dear Chief. That little alliance of ours is over.

Eirene: The end is coming, so we might as well make what's left of it worth our while.

Word of the second mine attack races through the West District almost at once. Before the panic stirred by the Aurum Secretum Apostle can settle, unrest erupts in wave after wave, driving the public safety system into full activation.

At the same time, audio clips of uncertain origin and every kind of rumor circulate endlessly across the net. The fear spreads faster than the contamination ever could.

Max King: Citizens of DisCity, this is your Speaker, Max King. We are facing an airborne audio threat spreading through our streets. I am activating emergency protocols across the city-state, effective now. Here is what you need to do.

Max King: Do not access, listen to, or transmit any unverified audio signals! If you witness anyone exhibiting extreme agitation or involuntary behavior, move away at once and report to the nearest public safety post.

You push yourself up. On the television across the room, the flames of the West District light nearly half the city.

**【Advance】**

- ...It's Eirene. She's making her move.

**end**

The Underground has always been short on able hands, and people must still be spared for the summoning ritual. To waste an Aurum Secretum dragon anywhere but the most critical moment would be a total waste. Eirene does not deal in losing trades.

...Unless, of course, the entire city's panic was the point all along.

She's inflaming the emotions to magnify the impact. You count the pieces on her board that would gain from this and reach only one conclusion—

Max King: ...We are facing an airborne audio threat spreading through our streets. I am activating emergency protocols across the city-state, effective now...

Max King: ...If you witness anyone exhibiting extreme agitation or involuntary behavior, move away at once...

The DisCity Speaker's voice reaches underground in fragments, broken and uneven. The announcement is spreading through the broadcasts to every street, covering the whole city. Eirene listens quietly, and a smile slowly curves her lips.

Eirene: Nicely done, Speaker King. Ever the devoted public servant. And now... my turn.

The time has come.

Eirene: Is the Pestilence Sage's power still around? The stage is ready. Go and claim it.

Max King: The contamination level is S-Class. I repeat: S-Class, the highest alert! All citizens, disable audio reception on every personal terminal now. Do not be curious. Do not gather to watch! The Speaker's office will keep you updated on public saf... kzzzzt...

A piercing burst of static slices through his voice. Within seconds, the broadcast that was meant to keep the Cursed Melody from spreading has instead become its most powerful loudspeaker.

FAC: This is an internal report! Widespread Cursed Melody broadcast in progress near Keylan Square. Nearly a thousand civilians are still in the area. Requesting immediate backup!

Max King: What did you say?!

The news makes its way into the ward. The agents guarding Adelaide all glance at their terminals at once, then just as quickly lock their eyes back on him.

Adelaide studies those faces, each one struggling to suppress its shock, and reads every expression as clear as day. A chuckle escapes him.

Adelaide: You've been nothing if not diligent. My sister, however, is simply too resourceful. She's found a way to turn the tables already. Claiming Famine's true power while bypassing the Sage's mark... quite the rational gambit, don't you think?

Adelaide: The board is yours now, ladies and gentlemen of DisCity. Will you end me here?

His words offer the DisCity agents no comfort whatsoever. The muzzles stay fixed at the back of his head, yet Adelaide only tilts his head slowly toward the window. Far off, storm clouds are staining the city's skyline dark.

Adelaide: *My clever sister always moves too fast for anyone to keep up. But the road ahead is no chessboard of your making, Eirene. Look more carefully.*

Adelaide: *You're running straight into hell...*

In the live feed, district alarms flash without pause as FAC soldiers guide the crowds toward the shelters. You watch the terminal without a word, and a strange calm settles over you with each passing moment.

Once DisCity's forces are spread thin, the Underground's shortage of numbers stops being a problem. But Eirene would never be content with merely pulling both sides back to the same starting line. Having gone to such lengths, she must want to take something more out of this chaos.

You lower your eyes and play back the recorded Cursed Melody. The melody rises slowly in the room. Without the blood crystal, attunement of this degree alone could never drag your consciousness back into the past.

With no rewind, your thoughts seem clearer than they have in some time.

Requiem's blood is gone now, and you have no chance of rewinding left.

So you have to picture the Vision in your own mind. Work out where the danger lies. Look ahead, far ahead—

Time to bet it all and take her on.

Flawed Schorl: Chief, Nirvana Port is on the line.

Iron Mask: The General says you're in trouble.

Iron Mask: She's—

**【Advance】**

- Don't. Don't come.

**end**

Iron Mask: ......

Chief: ...I can't do this on my own... The Shattered Blade is too important. I can't afford to lose you.

On the other end of the line, Iron Mask falls silent. Every exchange between the two of you is normally a trade of barbs, but this time is different. Iron Mask seems to have caught something in your voice, and for once, he offers no rebuttal.

Iron Mask: ...Understood. I'll hold position at Nirvana Port. Keep me posted. And... don't forget her word.

He does not ask why, nor does he offer any words of comfort. But you know that, coming from him, this is already his way of caring.

Nightingale: !!

Nightingale: We're reading massive Mania surges!! Initial spread points confirmed. They're radiating outward in a ring formation from the mine!

**【Advance】**

- She's there—

**end**

Yes, you feel it. The shackles stir faintly, pointing in a direction. But you know what awaits you there: layer upon layer of traps, all laid for you, the listener, to step in and take part in her performance.

[3D Entrance Animation]

## N12-9 Dance of Frenzy

Max King: ...Understood. Lock down the square immediately. Deploy reinforcements. Hold positions and protect—yes, do it now! Until we shut down that Cursed Melody broadcast, break up every crowd you can. Where you can't, put boots on the ground and keep them safe!

Max King: *...This is spreading too wide, Chief. You'd better move fast...*

The Cursed Melody replicates itself through the public network like a virus. Every street, every loudspeaker, every private terminal still powered on becomes a fresh source of infection. The spread far exceeds every projection.

This is the Pestilence Sage. The woman who, two years ago, held up the whole of DisCity's dark web on her own. As long as she's still in the system, nothing you throw at her will work.

You study the data calmly, then close your eyes and sift through the timelines that no longer exist.

*Every time you confronted Moore, she said the same thing, that she was just a piece of code.*

Someone who can fake her own death and slip free of every hunt, a hacker fully capable of hiding in the dark and breaking people at her leisure, would have no reason to break into a comms channel just to say hello.

You open your eyes.

If it's not a display of power... then it must be a deterrent. She wants everyone to believe... that she's still alive.

One by one, the hijacked broadcast signals begin to go dark. The Cursed Melody's frequency is replaced by white noise. On the screen, the red zones that had been spreading without end grind to a halt, then begin to recede.

Decryption efficiency climbs sharply. Now the two sides are evenly matched.

FAC: Stand your ground! This is the same Mania as that of the dark web two years ago. You know the playbook. Stick to the drills. Hold, no matter how long it takes. Don't give an inch...

FAC: Chief! Any luck?!

Chief: Schorl, run the current hijacking code against the samples from the dark web case two years ago. Then pull up the earliest logs you can find. Go as deep as you can.

**end**

Chief: —She's not in this era. Either she's dead, or she's hiding in the past like the rest of those Underground cowards.

Now that she thinks about it, there is one small flaw. If the enemy were to seize on it, it would be a nuisance, but fixing it completely would cost too much, and she has no wish to return and debug it locally. Flexibility is also something her present self could not possibly know about.

Still, the time she has bought should be enough for the request. The programmer is only enjoying a well-earned vacation after the work is done. That person will surely understand.

Moore: ...I'll be waiting for your next request.

For now, let her enjoy the vacation that comes after a completed job.

The familiar knock quickly pulls the Sage from work back into life, from the future back into the past. She launches a cleanup program at once, and the robot begins clearing away any incriminating traces in the room while she closes her laptop.

The Underground's Pestilence Sage erases every trace of cold calculation from her face and replaces it with the warmest, brightest smile.

Moore: Coming—ouch!

Moore: ...Hehe, I'm okay. Smelled the food and my stomach started talking. What's for dinner tonight?

Moore: I really missed your cooking, Granny.

Your guess is confirmed in an instant. At last, you have caught the enemy's weakness.

Chief: Go to the Public Security Bureau and pull out the hackers who were making waves on the DisCity dark web two years ago. This is their home turf. Tell them to bring their best. Take down that legendary programmer, and they walk away with fame and freedom.

Chief: While the admin's gone and no one's fixing that bug anytime soon—

**【Advance】**

- We hit it with everything we've got!

**end**

## N12-10 Farewell

Deep in the underground fissure, a crowd of robed figures gathers before the statue. The tremors of the surface reach them through the layers of stone, and dust sifts down from overhead.

Underground Puppet: And there's movement at Nirvana Port. The one who stole the War mark is finally making his move.

Evelleon: What does Ms. Campbell say?

Underground Puppet: She says she has all she needs, and BR-003 takes priority for you. She wants you to be there.

She has a fair grasp of what each side is thinking by now, and weighing the costs and benefits is simple enough. But as she looks at Requiem, Evelleon sinks back into thought. She has already assigned many to protect her, and the Listeners can hold their own in a fight, but with herself absent, the unease never quite settles.

Requiem senses the worry in the woman standing before her.

Requiem: ...It's all right. I've found the movement. Trust me, I can do it.

Requiem: Please get me to that stage.

Evelleon smiles. She hesitates a moment, then at last bends down and presses a kiss to the girl's forehead. Dodder vines crawl into Requiem, all the strength she has to give, nothing held back. It is a blessing, and yet it carries the weight of a final goodbye.

Evelleon: This should be enough...

Evelleon: You won't die. Death will watch over you until you and she reunite. Break the cage of time, Requiem. Bea has been waiting for you all these years.

Evelleon: I must go now.

The frantic footsteps and heart-rending screams from the surface pierce through every layer of obstruction, not a single sound failing to reach Helix's ears.

Helix: ...Yes. This is how it should be... They are our enemies! They... had it coming!

She does not truly understand what "judgment" means, but as long as she cries out these words loud enough, even the last sliver of doubt is swept away. When she turns to look at Requiem again, her eyes are pure and reverent once more.

Helix: Virtuoso, come here. Stay behind me.

Helix: The priest said I've been chosen as a divine vessel. He said I've awakened powers I never knew I had. The Divine has recognized me!

Helix: This time, I'll keep you safe.

Requiem: ......

Only the Underground's final defenders remain at her side: Helix, a handful of sorcerers, several advanced Puppets, and the woman Evy had brought back, the one whose heart yields no sound she can hear.

Requiem takes it all in. Her mind is made up. She gives Helix a warm, calming smile.

Requiem: Mm, thank you.

Requiem: ...I'm ready.

Seeing that you have finally come to her, the woman raises her head and greets you with a nod.

Eirene: You finally came.
